# Science course — CURRICULUM (the path)

Science is one world course, organised by **levels, not grades**:
a learner climbs when they are ready, not when a school calendar
says so. The school grades the books come from appear only in
each lesson's sources (provenance), never as the course's
organisation. The path climbs by the size of the thing (see
TITLES.md): **First Steps → Location and Maps → The Human Body →
Materials → Light → Weather and Seasons → Soil and Water →
Plants → Animals → Natural Resources → Our Environment → Family,
School and Community → Work and Heritage → Keeping Healthy →
Science Skills**.

**The first page (what learners are told):** everything in this
course comes from government textbooks — the primary level from
Ethiopia's Environmental Science books — except the **Tip
boxes**: a tip in a box is from us, the people making LiqLearns.
Tips are ours; the lessons are the books'. See ABOUT.md for the
full book list and the acceptance reckoning.

## Where the build stands

2026-11-12.** All three bands built: A (G1–G3) 74 lessons ·
B (G4) +39 created · C (G5–G6) +37 created, with the middle
bands also extending the earlier lessons' depth. **150 lessons**
in total; 296 media filed (12000–12188, 12200–12222, 13200–13248,
13300–13332, 13400–13470). Grade 1 stands on teacher-guide
evidence (no student book held); the G5/G6 teacher guides in
hand are truncated scans and are labelled as such wherever they
are used. The books' own chapter Review sections were not built
as lessons (standing exclusion). Build logs:
`liqlearns-courses-work/ethiopia-2026-11-11/build-logs/band-es-*.md`.

**General Science (Ethiopia, Grades 7–8) — COMPLETE
2026-11-12.** All 14 bands built and registered: 154
new lessons (Grade 7: 85; Grade 8: 69), 1,966
questions, 244 media, plus 12 grown lessons deepened
in place. Five new titles were opened (Earth and
Space, Motion Force and Energy, Sound, Heat,
Electricity and Magnetism). Ten lessons stand on
teacher-guide evidence only, at acceptance 0, each
labelled: the Grade 7 laboratory report, the seven
kinds of reaction around the home, the atmosphere and
measuring-the-too-big-and-the-too-small lessons,
resource depletion, environmental degradation, the
two Grade 8 measurement lessons, and the two Grade 8
solar-system comparison lessons.

## Unit 1 — First Steps

1. **Care for Your Book** (`first-steps/care-for-your-book`) — how to care for your school book so it stays clean, whole and readable all year.

## Unit 2 — Location and Maps

1. **Absolute Location — A Place's Exact Position** (`location-and-maps/absolute-location-a-places-exact-position`) — absolute location (ፍፁማዊ መገኛ) — a place's exact position, stated in latitude then longitude, in degrees and minutes.
2. **Directions — Right, Left and the Four Directions** (`location-and-maps/directions-right-left-and-the-four-directions`) — the directions — right and left, front and back on your own body, and east, west, north and south found from the sun.
3. **East Africa — Its Countries and Capitals** (`location-and-maps/east-africa-its-countries-and-capitals`) — the region of East Africa as one place: located relatively and absolutely, grouped into its nineteen countries, with every capital named.
4. **Ethiopia's Famous Places by Direction** (`location-and-maps/ethiopias-famous-places-by-direction`) — placing Ethiopia's famous places by their direction from Addis Ababa — the heritage sites and the natural sites alike.
5. **GPS and Digital Maps** (`location-and-maps/gps-and-digital-maps`) — how GPS and the digital map tools — Google Maps and Google Earth — find places, measure and show the Earth.
6. **Kebele and Woreda — Reading a Sketch Map** (`location-and-maps/kebele-and-woreda-reading-a-sketch-map`) — how villages group into a kebele and kebeles into a woreda, and how to read — and draw — a woreda sketch map.
7. **Kebele, Woreda and Zone** (`location-and-maps/kebele-woreda-and-zone`) — the next step up: a zone is a group of neighbouring woredas — and why knowing a place's location matters.
8. **Latitude Lines and the Equator** (`location-and-maps/latitude-lines-and-the-equator`) — latitude lines (የኬክሮስ ዲግሪ) — the imaginary lines drawn east–west around the earth, and the equator, the 0° line they are measured from.
9. **Longitude Lines and the Greenwich Meridian** (`location-and-maps/longitude-lines-and-the-greenwich-meridian`) — longitude lines (የኬንትሮስ ዲግሪ) — the imaginary lines drawn pole to pole, measured from the 0° Greenwich meridian.
10. **Map Scale, Key and Symbols** (`location-and-maps/map-scale-key-and-symbols`) — reading a map through the information printed around it — its scale, its north arrow, its key and colours, its symbols, and its year.
11. **My Neighbours and My First Sketch Map** (`location-and-maps/my-neighbours-and-my-first-sketch-map`) — naming the neighbours around your home, saying which side each lives on, and drawing your first sketch map (teacher-guide evidence only).
12. **The National Grid and Grid References** (`location-and-maps/national-grid-and-grid-references`) — reading positions on the National Grid — eastings, northings, and 4-figure and 6-figure grid references.
13. **Neighbours — and Measuring a Bearing** (`location-and-maps/neighbours-and-measuring-a-bearing`) — why neighbours matter to each other — trade, shared resources, security and kinship — how neighbours are located by distance and direction (teacher…
14. **Our Woreda's Neighbours** (`location-and-maps/our-woredas-neighbours`) — the names and positions of the woredas around our own — neighbours at woreda scale.
15. **Relative Location — Where a Place Lies** (`location-and-maps/relative-location-where-a-place-lies`) — relative location (አንጻራዊ መገኛ) — describing where a place lies by the things around it and by direction, from a market stall to a whole zone.
16. **The Amhara Region — Where It Sits and Its Zones** (`location-and-maps/the-amhara-region-where-it-sits-and-its-zones`) — the Amhara Region fixed every way this course has learned — by its neighbours, by its coordinates, and zone by zone with capitals — plus its famous…
17. **What Location Means** (`location-and-maps/what-location-means`) — what location (መገኛ) means — every thing and every place is somewhere, and we say where by pointing to what is near it.
18. **Where My Home Is** (`location-and-maps/where-my-home-is`) — saying where your own home is — which side of the school it lies on, and how it stands compared with the woreda.

## Unit 3 — The Human Body

1. **Adolescence and its changes** (`the-human-body/adolescence-and-its-changes`) — adolescence (ወጣትነት) is the fast change from child to adult — about 14–16 for boys and 12–14 for girls — and its changes split into the primary char…
2. **Experiment 2.1: saliva and the starch test** (`the-human-body/experiment-saliva-and-the-starch-test`) — saliva does real chemical work on food — shown by the book's two-glass experiment — and iodine tests a food for starch
3. **Menstruation and menstrual hygiene** (`the-human-body/menstruation-and-menstrual-hygiene`) — menstruation is a healthy sign that a girl has matured — not a disease and not a defect — blood flows 4–6 days about every 28 days, and it is manag…
4. **Parts of the body and the five senses** (`the-human-body/parts-of-the-body-and-the-five-senses`) — the learner can name the outside parts of the body, name the five sense organs, and say what each part and each sense does
5. **Teeth: two sets, four jobs** (`the-human-body/teeth-two-sets-and-four-jobs`) — a person gets two sets of teeth in a life — the milk set of 20 and the permanent set of 32 — and the four kinds of tooth each do a different job
6. **The brain** (`the-human-body/the-brain`) — the brain is the body's control centre — it has three parts and sits protected inside the skull
7. **The digestive system** (`the-human-body/the-digestive-system`) — food travels through one long tube — mouth, pharynx, oesophagus, stomach, small intestine, large intestine, rectum, anus — and each part does its o…
8. **The heart** (`the-human-body/the-heart`) — the heart is a muscle that pumps blood around the body by squeezing (contracting) and relaxing — and you can feel it work
9. **The human reproductive system** (`the-human-body/the-human-reproductive-system`) — the male testes make sperm and the female ovaries make eggs; when a sperm meets an egg in the uterine tube that meeting is fertilisation, and when …
10. **The kidneys** (`the-human-body/the-kidneys`) — the kidneys — two bean-shaped organs — keep the body's water and salt in balance, remove waste, and help the body make red blood cells
11. **The liver** (`the-human-body/the-liver`) — the liver is the largest internal organ, sitting on the right side under the ribs — it makes bile, filters the blood and makes the proteins that cl…
12. **The lungs and breathing** (`the-human-body/the-lungs-and-breathing`) — the lungs are a spongy pair inside the chest that take in oxygen from the air and give out carbon dioxide
13. **The microscope — seeing the unseen** (`the-human-body/the-microscope-seeing-the-unseen`) — a microscope makes tiny things big enough to see — pond life, parasites in clinic samples, and cells themselves were all unseen before it (Grade 7 General Science)
14. **The microscope — simple and compound, and total magnification** (`the-human-body/simple-and-compound-microscopes`) — a simple microscope has one lens and magnifies about 10–20×; a compound microscope multiplies eyepiece by objective — total magnification is their product (Grade 7 General Science)
15. **The microscope — parts and what each does** (`the-human-body/parts-of-the-compound-microscope`) — every part of the compound microscope has a job — the viewing parts magnify, the stage holds the specimen, and the mirror, condenser and diaphragm manage the light (Grade 7 General Science)
16. **The microscope — using it well, and making your own** (`the-human-body/using-a-microscope-and-making-your-own`) — carry a microscope by arm and base together, mount a specimen under a cover slip in a drop of water, focus low power first — and you can build a working simple microscope from a bottle and a drop of water (Grade 7 General Science)
17. **Cells — the discovery of the cell** (`the-human-body/the-discovery-of-the-cell`) — cells are the building blocks of all living things — Hooke saw dead cork cell walls in 1665 and coined the word 'cell', and Leeuwenhoek saw living single-celled animalcules in pond water in 1674 (Grade 7 General Science)
18. **Cells — inside a cell: organelles and their jobs** (`the-human-body/inside-a-cell-organelles-and-their-jobs`) — every cell has a membrane, cytoplasm, DNA and ribosomes; inside, each organelle has a job — nucleus, mitochondria, and in plants the wall and chloroplasts (Grade 7 General Science)
19. **Cells — shape, size and function** (`the-human-body/cell-shape-size-and-function`) — cells stay small — on average 10–20 µm — so materials can cross them easily, and each cell's shape fits its job: disc, spindle or long and branched (Grade 7 General Science)
20. **Cells — from one cell to a whole organism** (`the-human-body/from-one-cell-to-a-whole-organism`) — some organisms live as one cell doing everything, and multicellular organisms divide the labour across five levels: cell, tissue, organ, organ system, organism (Grade 7 General Science)
21. **Cells — respiration: releasing energy from food** (`the-human-body/respiration-releasing-energy-from-food`) — cells release energy from glucose with oxygen in the mitochondria, making ATP; without enough oxygen they ferment instead — making alcohol or lactic acid, with far less energy (Grade 7 General Science)
22. **Cells — photosynthesis and its partnership with respiration** (`the-human-body/photosynthesis-and-its-partnership-with-respiration`) — in chloroplasts, chlorophyll traps light to turn carbon dioxide and water into glucose and oxygen; photosynthesis and respiration interlock — each one's products are the other's raw materials (Grade 7 General Science)
23. **The human body — systems working together** (`the-human-body/the-human-body-systems-working-together`) — a body system is a group of organs working together on one job — parts alone do nothing, organised systems do the work — and the body runs seven systems (Grade 8 General Science)
24. **Skin — the integumentary system** (`the-human-body/skin-the-integumentary-system`) — the skin is a three-layer organ — epidermis, dermis, subcutaneous fat — with hair and nails built of keratin; it does five jobs from protection to temperature control (Grade 8 General Science)
25. **Muscles — how movement happens** (`the-human-body/muscles-how-movement-happens`) — muscles move the body by pulling in pairs — skeletal, cardiac and smooth muscle; a flexor contracts while its extensor partner relaxes (Grade 8 General Science)
26. **The skeleton — frame, joints and fractures** (`the-human-body/the-skeleton-frame-joints-and-fractures`) — the skeleton is one frame in two parts — axial (centre) and appendicular (limbs) — joined by joints with ligaments and cartilage; bones break in named ways and mend (Grade 8 General Science)
27. **Reproduction, puberty and staying safe** (`the-human-body/reproduction-puberty-and-staying-safe`) — puberty brings primary and secondary characteristics (girls about 11–13, boys about 14–16 as printed); the male and female organs do the work of reproduction, and staying safe is part of the teaching (Grade 8 General Science)

## Unit 4 — Materials

1. **Chemical change: burning and gas-making** (`materials/chemical-change-burning-and-gas-making`) — a chemical change makes a new body — proved by the book's Experiment 2 (paper burned to ash) and Experiment 3 (lemon and yeast filling a balloon wi…
2. **Density: why things sink or float** (`materials/density-why-things-sink-or-float`) — density is the amount of matter packed into a body's volume — and it decides whether a thing sinks or floats: the iron ball sinks, the sponge floats
3. **Describing things: matter and its properties** (`materials/describing-things`) — everything around us is matter, and we tell things apart by their properties: colour, shape, size, taste, smell, soft or rough, heavy or light, swe…
4. **Electricity and magnets** (`materials/electricity-and-magnets`) — electric energy comes from dry cells and generators converting other energies; current needs a charge difference and a closed circuit; magnets pull…
5. **Elements, compounds and mixtures** (`materials/elements-compounds-and-mixtures`) — matter is anything that takes up space and has mass, built of atoms; pure matter splits into elements (one kind of atom) and compounds (elements ch…
6. **Force, friction and gravity** (`materials/force-friction-and-gravity`) — a force is a push or pull — contact or non-contact; force changes shape, direction and speed; friction fights motion between touching surfaces (sta…
7. **Heat and how it travels** (`materials/heat-and-how-it-travels`) — heat is energy flowing from a hotter body to a colder one — a temperature difference is required — and it travels three ways: conduction (handed al…
8. **How materials behave: stretchy, breakable, shapable** (`materials/how-materials-behave`) — when we pull, drop and beat materials, they behave in kinds: some stretch and spring back, some break, some can be drawn into wire or beaten into s…
9. **Mixtures and how to separate them** (`materials/mixtures-and-how-to-separate-them`) — mixtures are heterogeneous (ልይ ዘር — the components show) or homogeneous (ዋህድ ዘር — hidden, like a solution of solute in solvent), and because a mixt…
10. **Motion, distance and speed** (`materials/motion-distance-and-speed`) — motion is change of place relative to a reference point; motions sort by path into straight-line, circular, oscillating and rotational; distance is…
11. **Oxides, acids, bases and salts** (`materials/oxides-acids-bases-and-salts`) — oxides — an element joined with oxygen — come in four characters (basic, acidic, amphoteric, neutral); basic oxides with water make bases, acidic o…
12. **Properties that identify a thing** (`materials/properties-that-identify-a-thing`) — every material has properties — colour, length, hardness, shape and more — and the properties are how we tell one material from another
13. **Simple machines** (`materials/simple-machines`) — simple machines multiply force, multiply speed/distance, or change force's direction — but never multiply work; the six kinds are the lever (three …
14. **Solids, liquids and gases** (`materials/solids-liquids-and-gases`) — matter exists in three states: solid keeps shape and volume; liquid keeps volume but takes the container's shape; gas has neither and spreads to fi…
15. **Sound and vibration** (`materials/sound-and-vibration`) — sound is energy made when bodies vibrate; it travels only through matter, never through a vacuum; Experiment 5 finds what vibrates in six Ethiopian…
16. **Symbols — writing the elements** (`materials/symbols-writing-the-elements`) — an element's symbol is a one- or two-letter shorthand, first letter capital, second small — most from English names, some from Latin (Fe, Cu, Au, K, Na), which is why Ca/Cu and He/Hg must not be confused (Grade 7 General Science)
17. **Formulas — reading what a compound is made of** (`materials/formulas-reading-what-a-compound-is-made-of`) — a formula lists the element types and, by subscript, the atom counts; element molecules may be monoatomic, diatomic or polyatomic (Grade 7 General Science)
18. **Formulas — valence, the combining power** (`materials/formulas-valence-the-combining-power`) — valence is how many partners an atom combines with, read off compounds; some elements vary — Fe is 2 in FeCl₂ but 3 in FeCl₃ (Grade 7 General Science)
19. **Formulas — writing binary compounds (criss-cross)** (`materials/formulas-writing-binary-compounds-criss-cross`) — symbols side by side, valences on top, simplify, cross them down as subscripts — calcium nitride becomes Ca₃N₂ (Grade 7 General Science)
20. **Formulas — naming binary compounds** (`materials/formulas-naming-binary-compounds`) — metal first, non-metal renamed with -ide; variable-valence metals carry a Roman numeral; non-metal pairs use Greek prefixes (Grade 7 General Science)
21. **Formulas — polyatomic ions and their compounds** (`materials/formulas-polyatomic-ions-and-their-compounds`) — charged atom groups with memorised formulas criss-cross the same way, brackets where a group repeats — drilled by the book's 100-card ion game (Grade 7 General Science)
22. **Formulas — the two meanings of a formula** (`materials/formulas-the-two-meanings-of-a-formula`) — qualitatively a formula names which elements are present; quantitatively its coefficients and subscripts count molecules and atoms (Grade 7 General Science)
23. **Reactions — reactants become products** (`materials/reactions-reactants-become-products`) — a reaction converts reactants into products — word equation first, then symbols; magnesium ribbon burns to a new ash (Grade 7 General Science)
24. **Reactions — five signs to look for** (`materials/reactions-five-signs-to-look-for`) — colour change, a solid forming, gas forming, texture change, energy change — and any one sign alone does not prove a reaction (Grade 7 General Science)
25. **Reactions — conservation of mass** (`materials/reactions-conservation-of-mass`) — mass is neither created nor destroyed in a reaction; atom kinds and counts are unchanged (Grade 7 General Science)
26. **Equations — balancing by inspection** (`materials/equations-balancing-by-inspection`) — equal atom counts each side, subscripts never changed; inspection balancing stepped through to 4K + O₂ → 2K₂O (Grade 7 General Science)
27. **Equations — balancing by LCM, and modelling with circles** (`materials/equations-balancing-by-lcm-and-modelling-with-circles`) — the LCM method totals valence numbers and divides back into coefficients; coloured-circle models build the balance physically (Grade 7 General Science)
28. **Reactions — chemistry in everyday life** (`materials/reactions-chemistry-in-everyday-life`) — burning, cooking, smelting, brewing — reactions as the basis of energy and culture; the guide's full tella-making process filed as teacher evidence (Grade 7 General Science)
29. **Reactions — seven kinds of reaction around the home** (`materials/reactions-seven-kinds-of-reaction-around-the-home`) — combustion, double displacement, electrochemistry, digestion, acid–base, soap action, cooking changes — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Grade 7 General Science)
30. **Matter — the particle theory** (`materials/matter-the-particle-theory`) — matter occupies space and has mass, and everything is made of tiny particles that attract each other, have spaces between them, always move, and move faster when heated; Democritus filed as teacher evidence (Grade 7 General Science)
31. **Matter — diffusion** (`materials/matter-diffusion`) — particles spread from where they are crowded to where they are spread out — fast in gases, slower in liquids, essentially not at all in solids (Grade 7 General Science)
32. **Changes — useful and harmful changes in daily life** (`materials/changes-useful-and-harmful`) — the changes of daily life sort into useful and harmful — and most useful ones are worked on purpose, at home, every day (Grade 7 General Science)
33. **Separating mixtures — magnet and evaporation** (`materials/separating-magnet-and-evaporation`) — two separations worked by hand: a magnet lifts iron out of a mixture, and heating drives water off a solution leaving the solid behind (Grade 7 General Science)
34. **Separating mixtures — settling, pouring off and filtering** (`materials/separating-settling-pouring-and-filtering`) — muddy water is cleaned in stages: let the solid settle, pour the clear layer off, then filter what remains through paper (Grade 7 General Science)
35. **Separating mixtures — the separatory funnel** (`materials/separating-the-separatory-funnel`) — two liquids that refuse to mix are parted in a stoppered funnel: settle into layers, then drain the lower layer through the stopcock and stop exactly at the boundary (Grade 7 General Science)
36. **Separating mixtures — distillation** (`materials/separating-distillation`) — a liquid mixture is parted by boiling point: the lower-boiling part vaporises first, condenses, and is collected as distillate — the way of the laboratory still and of the local araki still alike (Grade 7 General Science)
37. **Separating mixtures — local methods** (`materials/separating-local-methods`) — the community's own separation kit — sieving, winnowing, decanting coffee in the jebena, clarifying tella and tej, and the local still — is the laboratory's methods in home dress (Grade 7 General Science)
38. **Early ideas of the atom — Democritus against Aristotle** (`materials/early-ideas-of-the-atom-democritus-against-aristotle`) — matter was pictured two rival ways in ancient Greece — Democritus: built of indivisible atoms with empty space between; Aristotle and Plato: endlessly divisible — and Dalton's atomic theory later settled the argument on evidence (Grade 8 General Science)
39. **Inside the atom — protons, neutrons, electrons** (`materials/inside-the-atom-protons-neutrons-electrons`) — an atom holds protons and neutrons in a nucleus with electrons around it; Z counts the protons, A counts protons plus neutrons, and the amu is 1/12 of a carbon-12 atom (Grade 8 General Science)
40. **Isotopes — and why atomic masses are not whole numbers** (`materials/isotopes-and-why-atomic-masses-are-not-whole-numbers`) — atoms of the same element can carry different numbers of neutrons — isotopes — and an element's printed atomic mass is the weighted average of its isotopes, which is why it is not a whole number (Grade 8 General Science)
41. **Molecules — atoms joined** (`materials/molecules-atoms-joined`) — atoms join into molecules — monoatomic, diatomic or polyatomic — and the molecule is the smallest particle of a substance that still behaves as that substance (Grade 8 General Science)
42. **Organic and inorganic compounds** (`materials/organic-and-inorganic-compounds`) — carbon is the dividing line: organic compounds are carbon compounds — with named exceptions (carbon oxides, carbonates and a few others counted inorganic); inorganic compounds cover everything else (Grade 8 General Science)
43. **Hydrocarbons — alkanes, alkenes, alkynes** (`materials/hydrocarbons-alkanes-alkenes-alkynes`) — compounds of carbon and hydrogen only fall into three homologous series — alkanes CₙH₂ₙ₊₂ (single bonds), alkenes CₙH₂ₙ (one double bond), alkynes CₙH₂ₙ₋₂ (one triple bond) (Grade 8 General Science)
44. **Oxides — basic, acidic and in-between** (`materials/oxides-basic-acidic-and-in-between`) — oxides split into basic oxides (metal oxides that form bases), acidic oxides (non-metal oxides that form acids), amphoteric oxides that act both ways, and neutral oxides that do neither (Grade 8 General Science)
45. **Acids and bases — definitions and naming** (`materials/acids-and-bases-definitions-and-naming`) — acids release H⁺ in water and bases release OH⁻ — soluble bases are alkalis — and an acid's name is built from its anion: -ide → hydro-…-ic, -ate → -ic, -ite → -ous (Grade 8 General Science)
46. **Indicators and pH — testing with colour** (`materials/indicators-and-ph-testing-with-colour`) — indicators change colour in acid and in base, and the pH scale 0–14 fixes the strength: below 7 acid, 7 neutral, above 7 base — with local plant indicators as the eco-friendly test (Grade 8 General Science)
47. **Working with acids and bases — properties, uses, precautions** (`materials/working-with-acids-and-bases-properties-uses-precautions`) — acids react with active metals to release hydrogen, taste sour, conduct and serve in batteries, food and industry; bases feel soapy — and the safety rules are content, not a footnote (Grade 8 General Science)
48. **Neutralisation — cancelling acid with base** (`materials/neutralisation-cancelling-acid-with-base`) — acid + base → salt + water: neutralisation, put to work treating wastewater, farming acid soils back toward the pH a crop needs, and at home (Grade 8 General Science)
49. **Salts — preparation, names and uses** (`materials/salts-preparation-names-and-uses`) — salts are made three ways (acid + base, acid + metal, acid + carbonate), named from their acid, and used everywhere from table salt to fertiliser (Grade 8 General Science)
50. **Materials and their properties — hard, strong, flexible** (`materials/materials-and-their-properties-hard-strong-flexible`) — the three mechanical properties tested one at a time: hardness by the scratch contest (the harder scratches the softer), strength by loading to failure, flexibility by bending — with elastic and brittle as its neighbours — and materials chosen by matching the property to the job — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Gabon, Grade 4 — Super Efficace, Guide pédagogique, 4e année)
51. **Rust and corrosion — how air and water attack iron** (`materials/rust-and-corrosion-how-air-and-water-attack-iron`) — rust is iron transformed, not dirt on it: the three-nail experiment proves corrosion needs air (oxygen) AND water together, salt speeds it, and every protection — paint, oil, galvanising, tin, stainless steel, dry storage — is that proof applied — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Gabon, Grade 4 — Super Efficace, Guide pédagogique, 4e année)
52. **Materials around us — metal, wood and what things are made of** (`materials/materials-around-us-metal-wood-and-what-things-are-made-of`) — every object is made of materials, named by their family marks (metal cold, shiny, ringing, some of it magnetic; wood warm with grain; glass transparent and sharp-breaking), and most objects are assemblies — part by part, each material chosen for its part's job — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Gabon, Grade 5 — Super Efficace, Guide pédagogique, 5e année)
53. **Air is there — air takes up space and can be pushed** (`materials/air-is-there-air-takes-up-space-and-can-be-pushed`) — air is real matter: the cup pushed straight into water keeps its paper dry because trapped air occupies the cup, and pushed air pushes back — balloons, tyres, pumps and wind at work — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Gabon, Grade 3 — Super Efficace, Guide pédagogique, 3e année)
54. **Air can be squeezed — compressing air and its spring back** (`materials/air-can-be-squeezed-compressing-air-and-its-spring-back`) — sealed air compresses into a smaller space and springs back to its first volume — air is elastic — while water refuses to compress at all; the spring runs pumps, tyres, cushions, sprayers and air brakes — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Gabon, Grade 5 — Super Efficace, Guide pédagogique, 5e année)


## Unit 5 — Light

1. **Light and dark: what light is** (`light/light-and-dark`) — light and dark are a pair: where there is no light, there is darkness — ጨለማ
2. **Light travels in straight lines** (`light/light-travels-in-straight-lines`) — light travels only in straight lines — proved by the book's Experiment 4: a candle's flame is seen through a straight tube but not through a bent one
3. **Reflection, refraction and splitting light** (`light/reflection-refraction-and-splitting-light`) — on a smooth surface light reflects under two laws (the rays and the normal in one plane; angle of incidence = angle of reflection, Experiment 7); c…
4. **Sources of light: lamps people make and light from nature** (`light/sources-of-light`) — light comes from two families of sources: natural — sun, moon and stars; and human-made — kuraz lamp, bulb, candle, torch, fanos — and the learner …
5. **Sunlight keeps everything alive, and what light is used for** (`light/sunlight-keeps-everything-alive`) — the sun is our main light source: plants make their food with sunlight, animals eat plants, people eat both — without sunlight nothing lives; sunli…
6. **Things light passes through** (`light/things-light-passes-through`) — some materials let light through — glass, water, air — and we test them the book's way, with a torch
7. **Things that block light, and shadows** (`light/things-that-block-light-and-shadows`) — wall, wooden table and book block light; blocked light is why houses are dark at night and why you cast a shadow in the sun; leafy trees give shade
8. **Light — how seeing works** (`light/light-how-seeing-works`) — two rival theories of seeing are judged on evidence — rays leaving the eyes to fetch the object, against light reflected from the object into the eye; the reflected-light account wins (Grade 8 General Science)
9. **Light — sources, and the straight-line rule** (`light/light-sources-and-the-straight-line-rule`) — bodies split into luminous (making their own light) and non-luminous (seen only by reflected light — the Moon among them), and light travels in straight lines (Grade 8 General Science)
10. **Materials and light — transparent, translucent, opaque** (`light/materials-and-light-transparent-translucent-opaque`) — a semi-guided investigation — predict first, then devise your own test — sorts everyday materials into the three classes the science names (Grade 8 General Science)
11. **Reflection — laws, mirror images and the periscope** (`light/reflection-laws-mirror-images-and-the-periscope`) — reflection splits into regular (polished surfaces) and diffuse (rough surfaces) under the same two laws; a plane mirror's virtual image has four properties, and two mirrors build a periscope (Grade 8 General Science)
12. **Refraction — why the spoon looks broken** (`light/refraction-why-the-spoon-looks-broken`) — when light crosses obliquely into a different transparent medium its direction changes at the boundary — bending towards the normal going in, away coming out (Grade 8 General Science)
13. **Dispersion — splitting and remixing white light** (`light/dispersion-splitting-and-remixing-white-light`) — white light is a mixture of seven colours — violet, indigo, blue, green, yellow, orange, red — split by a prism because each colour bends differently, and remixed by Newton's disc (Grade 8 General Science)
14. **Diffraction gratings — grooved surfaces splitting light by wavelength** (`light/diffraction-gratings-grooved-surfaces-splitting-light-by-wavelength`) — a surface of fine, even grooves — a CD is one — returns a copy of the wave from every groove, and each wavelength reinforces at its own angle, fanning white light into a spectrum ordered purely by wavelength (Finland, Grade 7 pupil practical — Oppilastyö: Valon aaltoluonne)

## Unit 6 — Weather and Seasons

1. **Day and night, rainbow and flood** (`weather-and-seasons/day-and-night-rainbow-and-flood`) — a natural event happens in nature outside human control; day and night are a natural cycle — 24 hours, 12 of sunlight and 12 without — and rainbow …
2. **Measuring temperature, rain and wind** (`weather-and-seasons/measuring-temperature-rain-and-wind`) — climate's elements — temperature, rainfall amount, wind and air pressure — are measured, and two instruments have names to learn: the wind vane (ዊን…
3. **Reading climate tables and averaging** (`weather-and-seasons/reading-climate-tables`) — a climate table holds month-by-month temperature and rainfall for real stations — Gondar, Bahir Dar and Kombolcha — and reading it means finding we…
4. **Seasons and climate zones of our country** (`weather-and-seasons/seasons-and-climate-zones-of-our-country`) — Ethiopia's seasons — bega the dry season, kiremt the rainy, farming season — and its five climate zones by altitude: berha, qolla, weyna dega, dega…
5. **Today's weather** (`weather-and-seasons/todays-weather`) — weather is the air's condition now — sunny, rainy, cloudy, cold, hot, windy — changing hour by hour and morning to evening, and we observe and repo…
6. **Weather and climate** (`weather-and-seasons/weather-and-climate`) — weather changes hour by hour; climate is the air's long pattern — the Grade 3 book says judging it needs at least 30 years of records — and its ele…
7. **What controls a place's climate** (`weather-and-seasons/what-controls-a-places-climate`) — three things control a place's climate — height above sea level, distance from the equator, and distance from seas and oceans: near the equator is …

## Unit 7 — Soil and Water

1. **Drought: what it is and how we hold it back** (`soil-and-water/drought-what-it-is-and-how-we-hold-it-back`) — drought is when rain fails for a long time and water sources dry up — caused chiefly by an irregular climate, and also following deforestation — an…
2. **Ethiopia's rivers and lakes** (`soil-and-water/ethiopias-rivers-and-lakes`) — Ethiopia — Africa's water tower — drains three ways: the western system (Baro, Abbay, Tekeze → the Nile), the south-eastern (Wabi Shebele, Genale →…
3. **Famine, and how it differs from drought** (`soil-and-water/famine-and-how-it-differs-from-drought`) — famine can follow drought — food and water for animals and people run short — but drought alone does not mean famine, because stored food reserves …
4. **Loam, clay and sand — the squeeze test** (`soil-and-water/loam-clay-and-sand-the-squeeze-test`) — the three main soils are told apart by the squeeze test: fertile loam (ለም አፈር) holds then half-crumbles into a rounded lump; clay (ሸክላ) squeezes sm…
5. **Soil fertility, erosion, and why soil matters** (`soil-and-water/soil-fertility-erosion-and-why-soil-matters`) — fertility is decided by parent rock, chemical content, land shape, climate, remains and small living things — and fine smooth soil is carried away …
6. **The great lakes and the Nile** (`soil-and-water/the-great-lakes-and-the-nile`) — most of Africa's lakes lie in East Africa — Victoria (66,400 km², third in the world by area, shallow at 92 m), Malawi and Tanganyika (deepest at 1…
7. **Water problems: sharing, pollution and the lake weed** (`soil-and-water/water-problems-sharing-pollution-and-the-lake-weed`) — East Africa's water faces three printed problems: shared rivers with no agreement (Ethiopia contributes 86% of the Nile yet downstream countries ob…
8. **Water: why every living thing needs it** (`soil-and-water/water-why-every-living-thing-needs-it`) — water serves everything alive and everything people build: drinking, cooking and cleanliness; digesting food and carrying waste out of the body; gr…
9. **What soil is and what is in it** (`soil-and-water/what-soil-is-and-what-is-in-it`) — soil is the layer of the ground from the surface down to where plant roots reach, and it holds minerals, tiny living things, plant and animal remai…
10. **Soil — types, formation and loss** (`soil-and-water/soil-types-formation-and-loss`) — soil is told into loam, clay and sand by particle size and feel, and holds water very differently (clay most, loam in between, sand almost none) — and soil is lost by erosion (Grade 8 General Science)
11. **Saving the soil — conservation methods** (`soil-and-water/saving-the-soil-conservation-methods`) — soil is saved by a farmer's toolkit — afforestation, leaving crop residue as cover, terracing, check dams, shelter belts and contour ploughing (Grade 8 General Science)
12. **Water — why it is precious and how to save it** (`soil-and-water/water-why-it-is-precious-and-how-to-save-it`) — water covers 71% of the Earth's surface yet over 96% of it is saline, and the human body is about 60% water — so the fresh water living things need is precious and must be saved (Grade 8 General Science)
13. **Treating water to make it safe — filtering and settling** (`soil-and-water/treating-water-to-make-it-safe-filtering-and-settling`) — dirty water is treated in a chain: settling lets the heavy dirt sink, filtering through cloth, sand and gravel traps the fine dirt that is left, and treated water is kept covered and drawn with a clean cup — with the honest limit that boiling is the surest final step — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Gabon, Grade 3 — Super Efficace, Guide pédagogique, 3e année)

## Unit 8 — Plants

1. **Fallen leaves feed the soil; plants hold the soil** (`plants/fallen-leaves-feed-the-soil`) — fallen leaves and grass rot into the topsoil and raise its fertility; plants stop soil erosion, shelter wild animals and give recreation — and cutt…
2. **Forests and what they give** (`plants/forests-and-what-they-give`) — plants are living things that make their own food, stay in one place, grow and reproduce — and forests give building, paper, furniture, shelter for…
3. **Kinds of plants and their parts** (`plants/kinds-of-plants-and-their-parts`) — plants come in kinds — big trees, medium shrubs, small plants — and every plant has its parts: root, stem or trunk, leaf, flower (and fruit); our c…
4. **Plants give us things and food** (`plants/plants-give-us-things-and-food`) — houses, clothes, chairs, tables and paper come from plants; plants feed people — cabbage, orange, maize — and animals, and bees and butterflies fee…
5. **Plants that heal** (`plants/plants-that-heal`) — the local medicinal plants the Grade 3-level list names — garlic, tena adam, dama kese, haregresa, feto, grawa, moringa (shiferaw), kebericho, tosi…
6. **What a plant needs to grow** (`plants/what-a-plant-needs-to-grow`) — a plant needs soil, water, air and sunlight — proved by the book's four-tin experiment: bean seeds sown the same day in an empty tin, soil only, wa…
7. **Plants — seed plants: gymnosperms and angiosperms** (`plants/seed-plants-gymnosperms-and-angiosperms`) — seed plants split into gymnosperms with naked seeds, often in cones, and angiosperms whose seeds are enclosed in fruit (Grade 7 General Science)
8. **Plants — seedless plants: mosses, liverworts and ferns** (`plants/seedless-plants-mosses-and-ferns`) — bryophytes such as mosses are small moist-place plants reproducing by spores, while ferns have true roots, stems, leaves and vascular tissue — and still no seeds or flowers (Grade 7 General Science)

## Unit 9 — Animals

1. **Grouping animals: by young, by movement, by food and by body** (`animals/grouping-animals`) — animals sort into groups: egg-layers against milk-givers; by movement (flying, crawling, walking) and home (land, water, both); by food (meat-eater…
2. **Looking after our animals** (`animals/looking-after-our-animals`) — cared-for animals get enough food and water, a clean shelter, medical care when sick, and handling that does not hurt them — and cared-for animals …
3. **Tame animals and wild animals** (`animals/tame-animals-and-wild-animals`) — domestic animals live under people's care; wild animals live in forest, grassland, caves and water — and domestic animals give people great service…
4. **What animals need, and animals at work for people** (`animals/what-animals-need-and-animals-at-work`) — animals need food, shelter and water; they repay care with food (meat, milk, eggs, yoghurt, cheese, butter), transport (horse, donkey, mule, camel)…
5. **Living things — what makes something alive** (`animals/living-things-what-makes-something-alive`) — living things share a set of life characteristics together — sensitivity, reproduction, growth, respiration, nutrition, excretion, adaptation, homeostasis and metabolism — while non-living things do not (Grade 7 General Science)
6. **Classification — sorting the living world** (`animals/classification-sorting-the-living-world`) — classification groups living things by similarity and difference so life can be studied in order, newcomers identified and relationships revealed (Grade 7 General Science)
7. **Classification — scientific names and the seven ranks** (`animals/scientific-names-and-the-seven-ranks`) — every organism has one scientific name of two parts — genus and species — and sits in seven ranks from Kingdom down to Species (Grade 7 General Science)
8. **Kingdoms — the five kingdoms of living things** (`animals/the-five-kingdoms-of-living-things`) — living things are divided into five kingdoms — Monera, Protista, Fungi, Plantae and Animalia — separated by cell structure, nutrition, body organisation and reproduction (Grade 7 General Science)
9. **Animals — what makes an animal** (`animals/animals-what-makes-an-animal`) — animals are multicellular consumers with muscle and nervous tissue whose cells have no walls and no chloroplasts; they divide into invertebrates without a backbone and vertebrates with one (Grade 7 General Science)
10. **Animals — fish** (`animals/animals-fish`) — fish are cold-blooded aquatic animals that breathe through gills, move and balance with fins, and are covered in scales (Grade 7 General Science)
11. **Animals — amphibians and the frog** (`animals/animals-amphibians-and-the-frog`) — amphibians are ectothermic animals with smooth, moist, scaleless skin; their young breathe with gills in water and their adults breathe with lungs on land (Grade 7 General Science)
12. **Animals — reptiles** (`animals/animals-reptiles`) — reptiles are cold-blooded land animals with dry, rough, scaly skin that breathe with lungs — turtles and tortoises, crocodiles and alligators, lizards and snakes (Grade 7 General Science)
13. **Animals — birds** (`animals/animals-birds`) — birds are warm-blooded animals with a beak, two wings, two legs and feathers — feathers belong to birds alone — and beaks shaped to their food (Grade 7 General Science)
14. **Animals — mammals** (`animals/animals-mammals`) — mammals bear live young and feed them on milk, have hair, breathe with lungs and are warm-blooded — even whales and dolphins, which must surface to breathe air (Grade 7 General Science)
15. **Animals — invertebrates: insects and earthworms** (`animals/invertebrates-insects-and-earthworms`) — more than 95% of animals have no backbone; insects are known by six legs and three body parts, and the earthworm enriches the soil with its castings (Grade 7 General Science)
16. **Protists — protozoa and algae under the microscope** (`animals/protists-protozoa-and-algae`) — protists are mostly unicellular eukaryotes that are neither plant, fungus nor animal; protozoa move by pseudopodia, flagella or cilia, and plant-like algae photosynthesise without root, stem or leaf (Grade 7 General Science)
17. **Monera — bacteria: helpful, harmful, everywhere** (`animals/monera-bacteria-helpful-harmful-everywhere`) — Monera are prokaryotes with no nuclear membrane; bacteria sour milk and poison food yet also curdle milk usefully, and blue-green algae are photosynthetic bacteria, not true algae (Grade 7 General Science)
18. **Fungi — moulds, mushrooms and yeasts** (`animals/fungi-moulds-mushrooms-and-yeasts`) — fungi are eukaryotic, chitin-walled organisms that cannot make food — they digest dead matter and absorb it, parasitise living hosts, or live symbiotically; yeast (ersho) raises dough and ferments tella and tej (Grade 7 General Science)
19. **Animals — Who am I? vertebrate review game** (`animals/who-am-i-vertebrate-review-game`) — the five vertebrate groups can be told apart from clue cards — milk and fur, feathers and hard-shelled eggs, leathery scales, jelly eggs with gills-then-lungs, and gills with fins and scales (Grade 7 General Science)

## Unit 10 — Natural Resources

1. **Caring so they last: looking after natural resources** (`natural-resources/caring-so-they-last`) — the harms of loss — soil washed away, fertility and yields falling, wild animals fleeing and vanishing, climate disturbed — and the care that answe…
2. **Energy sources around us** (`natural-resources/energy-sources-around-us`) — energy is the ability to do a work; Ethiopia's power comes from water, sun, wind, fuel and biogas — hydro dams (GERD, Gilgel Gibe I and II, Tana Be…
3. **How a rich place becomes bare** (`natural-resources/how-a-rich-place-becomes-bare`) — a printed memoir: a place once rich in native plants, wild animals and birdsong ('the monkeys' house', 1970 E.C.) found forty years later a barren,…
4. **Minerals from the ground** (`natural-resources/minerals-from-the-ground`) — minerals are things dug from the ground or found on it — stone, gravel, sand, salt, iron, and in Grade 3 also coal, fuel/oil and gold; they are non…
5. **Ones that come back and ones that finish** (`natural-resources/ones-that-come-back-and-ones-that-finish`) — resources sort into two families: renewable ones come back after use and are not exhausted (water, air, plants), and non-renewable ones are finishe…
6. **Plants and wild animals as resources** (`natural-resources/plants-and-wild-animals-as-resources`) — plants keep the climate steady and serve food, industry and more; wild animals, not tamed and living wild, earn the country income through tourism …
7. **Water, soil and air around us** (`natural-resources/water-soil-and-air-around-us`) — soil, water and air are natural resources: soil is the fertile top layer where roots reach, in black, red and brown; water comes from river, well, …
8. **What counts as a natural resource** (`natural-resources/what-counts-as-a-natural-resource`) — a natural resource is everything found in nature, on or under the Earth, that serves people directly or indirectly — soil, air, water, plants, anim…

## Unit 11 — Our Environment

1. **Caring for our environment: soil, water and air** (`our-environment/caring-for-our-environment-soil-water-and-air`) — care, resource by resource: stopping soil erosion (eroded land against protected land), keeping water sources unpolluted, keeping the air clean — w…
2. **Caring for our surroundings, and reusing what we throw away** (`our-environment/caring-for-our-surroundings-and-reusing`) — our resources are being badly damaged, so we protect them: planting and tending trees, disposing of waste properly, preventing forest fire, cleanli…
3. **Dirty water and what it brings** (`our-environment/dirty-water-and-what-it-brings`) — liquid waste — urine, wash-water and effluent from homes and factories — brings bad smell, respiratory illness (colds, asthma), breeding places for…
4. **Dry rubbish in its place** (`our-environment/dry-rubbish-in-its-place`) — dry waste — peelings (onion, potato, banana, orange), paper and plastic — goes into a waste container and then to a proper disposal place, or the h…
5. **Pollution and its four kinds** (`our-environment/pollution-and-its-four-kinds`) — Ethiopia's main environmental problems are listed together — soil erosion, land damage, deforestation, water shortage, biodiversity loss and pollut…
6. **Resources around us, and why they matter** (`our-environment/resources-around-us-and-why-they-matter`) — natural resources are soil, water, plants, air and wild animals; man-made resources are monuments, bridges, houses of worship, government buildings…
7. **Resources people make — and looking after them** (`our-environment/resources-people-make`) — things people create for use — dam, mosque, church, school, hospital — are human-made resources; as shared property they must be used and kept prop…
8. **Waste, flies, germs and trachoma** (`our-environment/waste-flies-germs-and-trachoma`) — waste dumped anywhere breeds flies, and flies carry germs from waste to people's bodies — one named result is trachoma, an eye disease that untreat…
9. **What waste is and its three kinds** (`our-environment/what-waste-is-and-its-three-kinds`) — waste is what is left over after things are used — leftover food and products, human and animal waste — and it comes in three states: solid waste, …
10. **Cycles — the carbon cycle** (`our-environment/cycles-the-carbon-cycle`) — carbon travels in a loop — released into the air by respiration and burning, taken in by plants for photosynthesis, passed to animals that eat the plants, returned by death and decomposition, and stored away in fossil fuels until burning releases it again (Grade 7 General Science)
11. **Cycles — the water cycle** (`our-environment/cycles-the-water-cycle`) — water travels in an endless loop — evaporation and transpiration rise, condensation gathers the vapour into clouds, precipitation returns it as rain, and flow carries it back to the water bodies (Grade 7 General Science)
12. **Energy resources — resource depletion and its drivers** (`our-environment/energy-resources-resource-depletion-and-its-drivers`) — resource depletion is using a resource faster than it can be replenished until it is exhausted — driven by seven named drivers — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Grade 7 General Science)
13. **Energy resources — environmental degradation and its effects** (`our-environment/energy-resources-environmental-degradation-and-its-effects`) — environmental degradation is the environment's deterioration, chiefly through resource depletion — and its effects fall in five families: human health, biodiversity and more — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Grade 7 General Science)
14. **Ecosystems — populations, communities and their surroundings** (`our-environment/ecosystems-populations-communities-and-their-surroundings`) — living things are organised in widening levels — a habitat is where a kind lives, a population is all of one kind in a place, a community is all the kinds together (Grade 8 General Science)
15. **How living things affect each other** (`our-environment/how-living-things-affect-each-other`) — living things that meet affect each other in six named ways — predation, competition, parasitism, mutualism, commensalism and amensalism (Grade 8 General Science)
16. **Food chains, food webs and energy pyramids** (`our-environment/food-chains-food-webs-and-energy-pyramids`) — living things are linked by eating: producers make food, consumers eat, and decomposers return the remains — energy moves through the chain in a pyramid (Grade 8 General Science)
17. **Cycles of matter — carbon, nitrogen, water** (`our-environment/cycles-of-matter-carbon-nitrogen-water`) — carbon, oxygen, nitrogen and water cycle round and round between the living and non-living parts of the ecosystem, while energy flows one way through (Grade 8 General Science)
18. **Air pollution and acid rain** (`our-environment/air-pollution-and-acid-rain`) — air pollution loads the air with harmful matter — including particulates small enough to lodge in lungs — and two pollutant gases, sulphur and nitrogen oxides, make acid rain (Grade 8 General Science)
19. **Global warming — the greenhouse effect** (`our-environment/global-warming-the-greenhouse-effect`) — a parked car in the sun shows the greenhouse effect: its windows let light in and hold the heat; greenhouse gases — carbon dioxide, methane and more — do the same to the Earth (Grade 8 General Science)
20. **Forests — what they give and what cutting them costs** (`our-environment/forests-what-they-give-and-what-cutting-them-costs`) — forests give timber, fuel wood, fodder, fibre, fruit, herbal drugs and industrial raw materials, and they shelter wildlife, build and hold soil, and shape climate (Grade 8 General Science)
21. **Biodiversity — keeping the variety of life** (`our-environment/biodiversity-keeping-the-variety-of-life`) — biodiversity is the variety of life, counted in species; its value is direct (medicine and agriculture) and indirect (ecosystems that work), and it is kept by protecting habitats (Grade 8 General Science)
22. **Local knowledge, local conservation** (`our-environment/local-knowledge-local-conservation`) — the unit closes by turning to the people who have conserved a place longest: pupils interview local people on how they conserve water, forest and soil (Grade 8 General Science)
23. **Air pollution — what dirties the air** (`our-environment/air-pollution-what-dirties-the-air`) — the three families of air dirt — smoke from burning (the biggest), fumes from engines, dust from bare dry ground — what dirty air does to lungs, eyes and plants, and cleaning at the source: never burn rubbish, manage cooking smoke, plant trees — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Gabon, Grade 3 — Super Efficace, Guide pédagogique, 3e année)

10. **Our Community — Places That Help Us** (`family-school-and-community/places-that-help-us`) — the places and institutions that serve a community — health station, church and mosque, bank, police station, school, iddir, court — what each one …

20. **Tourism — the Smokeless Industry** (`work-and-heritage/tourism-the-smokeless-industry`) — tourism as an industry: what visitors come for in Ethiopia and across East Africa, what tourism earns, and what it builds.

3. **Chemicals in the house and the six safety rules** (`keeping-healthy/chemicals-in-the-house-and-the-six-safety-rules`) — homes hold chemicals — berekina (bleach), fertilisers, and many more — some poisonous through careless handling, and children are poisoned easily b…

12. **Keeping our bodies clean** (`keeping-healthy/keeping-our-bodies-clean`) — keeping the whole body clean as a daily habit — washing, teeth, nails, hair and clothes — and knowing why it matters
13. **Keeping our home clean** (`keeping-healthy/keeping-our-home-clean`) — keeping home and toilet clean with household cleaning materials (teacher-guide evidence only, counted nowhere).
14. **Moving our bodies and sleep on time** (`keeping-healthy/moving-our-bodies-and-sleep-on-time`) — exercise and sport keep people active, strong and healthy; enough sleep, on time, keeps learning and work strong

16. **Signs, lights and crossing rules** (`keeping-healthy/signs-lights-and-crossing-rules`) — the traffic signs and lights and what each colour commands, with the crossing rules gathered in one table

## Unit 15 — Science Skills

1. **Debating change: new foods or old ways?** (`science-skills/debating-change-new-foods-or-old-ways`) — the book prints a debate — 'The New One and the Old One' — over whether food culture must change: new productive crops against slow-changing habits…
2. **Experiment 1: melting ice and boiling water** (`science-skills/experiment-1-melting-ice-and-boiling-water`) — Experiment 1 proves a change of state: ice on a big spoon over a candle flame melts at 0 °C and water boils at 100 °C at sea level (thermometer tak…
3. **Living and non-living things** (`science-skills/living-and-non-living-things`) — living things have the characteristics of life; we sort the things around us into living and non-living — goat, tree, child against stone, river, c…
4. **Making a wind vane and rain gauge** (`science-skills/making-a-wind-vane-and-rain-gauge`) — the project: build a wind vane and a rain gauge with your teacher, record the wind's direction daily at break time, and report weekly to the class
5. **Measuring: making and using a balance** (`science-skills/making-and-using-a-balance`) — a working balance can be built from two shoe-polish tins, string and wood — and weighing a water-filled bottle against an air-filled one makes mass…
6. **Measuring: traditional and modern measures** (`science-skills/measuring-traditional-and-modern-measures`) — people measured long before rulers existed — with arm-measures, footsteps, shadows and the balance — and modern instruments replaced them because a…
7. **Measuring: temperature, thermometers and the Celsius scale** (`science-skills/temperature-thermometers-and-the-celsius-scale`) — temperature is the measure of a body's hotness or coldness and it decides the body's state; it is measured with a thermometer; the world unit is th…
8. **Measuring: units of mass, length and time** (`science-skills/units-of-mass-length-and-time`) — the standard units join by fixed relations — 1 kg = 1000 g; 1 m = 100 cm = 1000 mm; 1 km = 1000 m; 1 day = 24 hours, 1 hour = 60 minutes, 1 minute …
9. **Measuring: volume and displacement** (`science-skills/volume-and-displacement`) — volume (ይዘት/ምድገት) — how much space a body takes — is measured by displacement: lower the body into water and read how far the level rises in a grad…
10. **What science is — evidence, inference and tentative knowledge** (`science-skills/what-science-is`) — science is knowledge built on evidence: we observe, we infer, we hold what we know as tentative — ready to change when better evidence comes (Grade 7 General Science)
11. **The branches of science** (`science-skills/the-branches-of-science`) — science splits into branches by what it studies: life, physical and earth science — and above them, natural science studies nature while social science studies people (Grade 7 General Science)
12. **Two kinds of knowledge — conventional science and indigenous knowledge** (`science-skills/two-kinds-of-knowledge`) — conventional knowledge — tentative, evidence-tested, written down — and indigenous knowledge — built by experience over generations and woven into a culture (Grade 7 General Science)
13. **Science and technology — how they differ and feed each other** (`science-skills/science-and-technology`) — science explores and discovers; technology applies knowledge and invents — each feeds the other, and technology can help or harm (Grade 7 General Science)
14. **Doing science honestly — ethics and Ethiopian scientists** (`science-skills/doing-science-honestly`) — science has an ethics — honesty in reporting, care in analysis, credit where due — and Ethiopia's own scientists show that ethics at work (Grade 7 General Science)
15. **Measurement — scalars, vectors and prefixes** (`science-skills/scalars-vectors-and-prefixes`) — some quantities need only a size (scalars), others need size and direction (vectors) — and SI prefixes shrink or stretch units by powers of ten, from femto to peta (Grade 7 General Science)
16. **Measuring time — from shadows to clocks** (`science-skills/measuring-time-from-shadows-to-clocks`) — time was first read from the sun — shadows, the sun's position, birdsong at dawn — and clocks made it exact; building a sundial joins the two (Grade 7 General Science)
17. **Measurement — accuracy and precision** (`science-skills/accuracy-and-precision`) — accuracy is closeness to the true value; precision is closeness of repeated readings to each other — and readings can be precise yet inaccurate (Grade 7 General Science)
18. **Laboratory safety — the ten rules** (`science-skills/laboratory-safety-the-ten-rules`) — laboratories hold hazardous, poisonous, flammable and explosive materials, and most accidents come from carelessness, impatience and unauthorised experiments — the ten rules are the guard (Grade 7 General Science)
19. **Laboratory safety — hazard signs and protective equipment** (`science-skills/hazard-signs-and-protective-equipment`) — ten hazard signs warn by sight — corrosive, flammable, toxic and the rest — and protective equipment reduces harm without removing the hazard (Grade 7 General Science)
20. **Laboratory equipment — names, uses, and making your own** (`science-skills/laboratory-equipment-names-uses-and-making-your-own`) — thirteen common tools, each with its job — and when the school has no kit, the same tools are made from local materials (Grade 7 General Science)
21. **Writing a laboratory report** (`science-skills/writing-a-laboratory-report`) — an experiment is written up in seven parts — Title, Aim, Theory, Materials, Data/Observation, Result and Discussion, Conclusion — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Grade 7 General Science)
22. **The scientific method — observe, hypothesise, test, conclude** (`science-skills/the-scientific-method-observe-hypothesise-test-conclude`) — an investigation runs in a working sequence — observe, ask, hypothesise, test, analyse and conclude — and its results can harden into a law or support a theory (Grade 8 General Science)
23. **Semi-guided investigations — your question, your method** (`science-skills/semi-guided-investigations-your-question-your-method`) — in a semi-guided investigation the question is given but the method is yours — and the mark of real inquiry is revising a hypothesis when the evidence refuses it (Grade 8 General Science)
24. **Fair tests — variables and control groups** (`science-skills/fair-tests-variables-and-control-groups`) — a test is fair only when one thing changes, one thing is measured, and everything else is held still — with a control group that gets no treatment as the comparison baseline (Grade 8 General Science)
25. **Measurement — quantities, units, prefixes, accuracy and precision (Grade 8)** (`science-skills/measurement-quantities-units-prefixes-and-accuracy-grade-8`) — measurement rests on seven fundamental quantities, prefixes stretch and shrink their units by tens — and every set of readings must pass two different judgements: accuracy against the true value, precision against itself — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Grade 8 General Science)
26. **Measuring time the old way — sundial, sand-glass, water clock** (`science-skills/measuring-time-the-old-way-sundial-sand-glass-water-clock`) — before clocks, time was read from the sun, from falling sand and from flowing water — each old clock works, and each depends on something that shifts, which is exactly its reliability problem — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Grade 8 General Science)
27. **Skills of practical study — observe, predict, hypothesise, record, conclude** (`science-skills/skills-of-practical-study-observe-predict-hypothesise-record-conclude`) — practical science as a taught skill sequence — observe, predict, frame a testable hypothesis, design a fair test, record honestly in prepared tables with repeats, analyse, and conclude only what the evidence supports (Kuwait, Grade 11 — *Physics, Grade 11, Term 1 — applications workbook*, front matter, printed p. 8)

298. **The Origin of Life on Earth** (`biology/the-origin-of-life-on-earth`) — what evolution means; views on the origin of life evaluated from a scientific standpoint (unit outcomes); the deep-time timeline of life — oceans and continents forming, first l…

302. **The scientific method — observe, question, hypothesise, test, conclude** (`biology/the-scientific-method`) — the scientific method moves from observation to question, hypothesis, experiment and conclusion.

305. **The sub-fields of biology** (`biology/the-sub-fields-of-biology`) — biology is divided into sub-fields, each studying one slice of life

308. **The water cycle** (`biology/the-water-cycle`) — evaporation, condensation, precipitation and collection move water endlessly between land, living things and sky

310. **Tools technology gives biology** (`biology/tools-technology-gives-biology`) — Technology serves biology in biochemical, biomedical (including medicinal-plant chemistry), biophysical and environmental studies; the tool catalogue runs from digital thermomet…

312. **Treating plants ethically in biology** (`biology/treating-plants-ethically-in-biology`) — Plants are producers feeding all life and work in a decentralised way with no brain; destructive testing, careless collecting of seeds, berries, roots, leaves, bark and flowers,…
313. **Trophic levels — the steps of a food chain** (`biology/trophic-levels-the-steps-of-a-food-chain`) — each feeding step is a trophic level — built from real chains like grass, grasshopper, lizard, snake, hawk
314. **Tuberculosis — an airborne disease and the BCG vaccine** (`biology/tuberculosis-and-the-bcg-vaccine`) — TB is an airborne bacterial disease tied to weakened immunity and overcrowding, and BCG vaccination in infancy protects most recipients.
315. **Two kinds of cell — with and without a nucleus** (`biology/two-kinds-of-cell`) — prokaryotic cells have no nucleus; eukaryotic cells have a true nucleus and organelles.
316. **Two systems, one job — air and blood together** (`biology/two-systems-one-job-air-and-blood-together`) — the lungs take in oxygen and release carbon dioxide while the heart circulates the blood that carries both — the two systems working continually together, fuelled by digestion
317. **Types of joints** (`biology/types-of-joints`) — Where bones meet and movement happens, in named types: ball-and-socket (movement in all directions — shoulder, hip), hinge (one plane — elbow, knee), condyloid (movement without…
318. **Useful insects in farming and as food** (`biology/useful-insects-in-farming-and-as-food`) — Insects serve farming as pollinators and as natural pest regulators — predators and parasitoids such as mantises, ladybird beetles, ground beetles, lacewings and hoverflies used…
319. **Uses of Microorganisms** (`biology/uses-of-microorganisms`) — microbes exploited in food, agriculture, medicine, energy and environmental clean-up; in agriculture — decomposition and humus formation, nitrogen fixation, phosphate solubilisa…
320. **Using a microscope — carry, focus, put away** (`biology/using-a-microscope`) — a microscope is carried upright by arm and base, focused from low power upward, and put away clean.
