322. **Viral sexually transmitted infections — including HIV** (`biology/viral-sexually-transmitted-infections-including-hiv`) — Genital herpes (HSV-1 and HSV-2), genital warts (HPV), molluscum contagiosum (a poxvirus spread skin to skin), HIV — pictured, attacking CD4 immune cells through three stages: a…
323. **Viruses** (`biology/viruses`) — viruses as very small non-cellular obligate parasites — a DNA or RNA genome in a protein coat, no organelles, no metabolism, replicating only inside host cells — sized against c…
324. **Viruses — cycles, uses and HIV** (`biology/viruses-cycles-uses-and-hiv`) — Viruses are classified by virion morphology, physicochemical and genome properties, proteins, replication and antigens; six virion shapes are listed (spherical, rod, brick, tadp…
325. **Water biomes — fresh and salt** (`biology/water-biomes-fresh-and-salt`) — water biomes cover about 75 percent of Earth's surface and are set by salinity.
326. **Water — the molecule life is dissolved in** (`biology/water-the-molecule-life-is-dissolved-in`) — water's structure gives it cohesion, adhesion and surface tension — and its unusual boiling and freezing behaviour makes it life's medium
327. **What biology gives technology** (`biology/what-biology-gives-technology`) — Biological knowledge feeds technology by supplying materials and designs to imitate, routes to curing genetic conditions and making biosynthetic repair materials for damaged tis…
328. **What biology is** (`biology/what-biology-is`) — biology is the scientific study of life and living things.
329. **What blood is made of** (`biology/what-blood-is-made-of`) — blood is plasma plus red cells (adapted for oxygen transport), white cells and platelets
330. **What classification is good for** (`biology/what-classification-is-good-for`) — classification identifies helpful and harmful organisms, saves study time, and reveals relationships.
331. **What makes an animal — and the animal family tree** (`biology/what-makes-an-animal-and-the-animal-family-tree`) — Animals are eukaryotic, multicellular, heterotrophic and sensitive to stimuli; they reproduce, move, respire, excrete and grow, with different body symmetries.
332. **What makes an ecosystem self-supporting** (`biology/what-makes-an-ecosystem-self-supporting`) — an ecosystem is a self-supporting unit of community plus environment, with materials recycled through it.
333. **What regulates population growth** (`biology/what-regulates-population-growth`) — Growth is checked by density-dependent factors — predation, competition within and between species, waste accumulation and disease, all biting harder as density rises — and dens…
334. **What speeds and slows enzymes** (`biology/what-speeds-and-slows-enzymes`) — Activity peaks at an optimum and falls away with temperature (heat denatures, irreversibly), pH, substrate and enzyme concentration, end-product build-up, radiation, water, acti…
335. **Why biology matters in everyday life** (`biology/why-biology-matters-in-everyday-life`) — studying biology explains living things and touches daily life — medicine, farming, crime investigation and food-making.
336. **Why food insecurity happens** (`biology/why-food-insecurity-happens`) — Global food supply is uneven because physical factors (climate, soil quality, gradient) and human factors (technology) control what is produced where; the causes-of-food-insecur…
337. **Why species go extinct** (`biology/why-species-go-extinct`) — Causes of species extinction are listed in teaching-note form: habitat degradation and destruction (elimination or change of living space), toxicity (killing directly through fo…
338. **Why we eat — energy, building, protection** (`biology/why-we-eat`) — food gives energy, builds and repairs the body, and protects it — shortage of a group causes deficiency disease.
339. **Epidemic curves and healthcare capacity** (`biology/epidemic-curves-and-healthcare-capacity`) — an epidemic curve plots cases against time; without protective measures its peak crosses the healthcare-capacity line, with them the curve flattens and the peak stays under it — taught from Sweden's tactile "flatten the curve" graph (Sweden, Grade 1 — *Platta ut kurvan*, SPSM tactile sheet set, PDF pp. 1–2)
340. **Cloning and plant tissue culture** (`biology/cloning-and-plant-tissue-culture`) — cloning copies one donor's heredity without fertilisation; Morocco teaches it by comparing two techniques — one based on an egg, the other on a specialised somatic cell from the mammary glands — and by comparing cloning with natural reproduction to deduce the principle (Morocco, Grade 8 — *Guide Almoufid SVT*, teacher's guide — evidence only, printed pp. 85–87)
341. **Brazilian biomes and the Mata Atlântica** (`biology/brazilian-biomes-and-the-mata-atlantica`) — Brazil's own biomes taught through the Mata Atlântica, the Atlantic Forest of Brazil's coast, whose disappearance Brazil's Paraná public textbook gives a whole chapter framed as a cry for help — "socorro!!! Cadê você???" (Help!!! Where are you???) (Brazil, Grade 10 — *Biologia — Livro Didático Público, Ensino Médio*, student textbook, Ch. 12, printed p. 193)
342. **Masquerade and mimicry — the hoverfly that imitates a bee** (`biology/masquerade-and-mimicry`) — a harmless animal survives by imitating a defended one; Costa Rica's Grade 11 anthology opens its whole ecology course with the case of Eristalis tenax, the hoverfly that imitates a bee — "Un caso muy especial de mimetismo" (a very special case of mimicry) (Costa Rica, Grade 11 — *Biología Undécimo — Antologías CONED*, student anthology, Unit 1 Lectura Reflexiva, printed p. 14)
343. **Coevolution — arms races and partnerships** (`biology/coevolution-arms-races-and-partnerships`) — species that live in close relationship shape each other's evolution, running as an arms race where the relationship harms and as a partnership where both sides come to fit each other; Costa Rica teaches Coevolución inside its relationships unit, between the symbiotic relationships and the parasitology strand (Costa Rica, Grade 11 — *Biología Undécimo — Antologías CONED*, student anthology, Unit 1, printed p. 28)
344. **Thermodynamics and entropy in ecosystems** (`biology/thermodynamics-and-entropy-in-ecosystems`) — ecosystems taught through the laws of thermodynamics and entropy outright: energy is transformed, never created or destroyed, and at every transfer some useful energy is lost as heat while entropy rises — so energy flows one way through the trophic levels while materials cycle (Costa Rica, Grade 11 — *Biología Undécimo — Antologías CONED*, student anthology, Unit 2, printed pp. 66–67)
345. **Mental health disorders** (`biology/mental-health-disorders`) — the human psyche develops, and its disorders are taught by name in Azerbaijan's Grade 11 biology: anxiety disorders (Тревожные расстройства), depression (Депрессии) and psychoses (Психозы), inside the book's human part between embryonic development and healthy family relations (Azerbaijan, Grade 11 — *Биология 11 (Biology 11)*, Russian-sector student textbook, Part VI §§38–41, printed pp. 144–155)
346. **Cancer and tumours** (`biology/cancer-and-tumours`) — cell division out of control: uncontrolled division of cells piles up into tumours (Опухоли), and the book closes its controlled-and-uncontrolled-division part with a section titled simply "Cancer" (Рак) (Azerbaijan, Grade 11 — *Биология 11 (Biology 11)*, Russian-sector student textbook, Part VII §§46–47, printed pp. 175–178)
347. **Eating disorders** (`biology/eating-disorders`) — eating disorders damage health: Ecuador's book names anorexia and bulimia and teaches them inside "Salud integral" (integral health), in a cause→effect table with drugs, alcohol, sedentarism and pollution, and notes plainly that Ecuador lacks updated statistics on anorexia and bulimia (Ecuador, Grade 11 — *Biología — 1ro BGU*, student textbook, Sección 6, Tema 2, printed pp. 80–81)
348. **Classification by form vs by genes** (`biology/classification-by-form-vs-by-genes`) — living things can be classified by their form — morphological classification (التصنيف الشكلي), grouping by outward shape and structure — or by their genes — genetic classification (التصنيف الجيني), grouping by the genetic material they carry; Palestine's Grade 10 book sets the two side by side and compares them as the closing section of its modern-classification chapter (Palestine, Grade 10 — *العلوم الحياتية (Life Sciences)*, student textbook, Unit 4 Ch. 1 §4, printed p. 101)
349. **Plant immunity** (`biology/plant-immunity`) — plants defend themselves against pathogens in two ways — structural immunity (cutinised epidermis, hairs/thorns, thickened cell walls; induced cork formation, tyloses blocking xylem, gum deposition in Acacia nilotica, hypersensitive death of injured tissue) and biochemical immunity (pathogen receptors, phenols and glycosides, non-protein amino acids like canavanine, antimicrobial proteins and detoxifying enzymes, post-infection strengthening) — and Egypt's book teaches immunity in plants first, before immunity in man (Egypt, Grade 12 — *BIOLOGY*, student textbook, Ch. 4, printed pp. 85–88)
350. **Gender identity and sexual orientation** (`biology/gender-identity-and-sexual-orientation`) — France's manual teaches identity and orientation inside its brain, pleasure and sexuality chapter ("Identités et orientations"): what gender is, gender stereotypes — with the matrilineal Khasi society as the counter-example — attraction, and the evolution of LGBT rights in France (France, Grade 10 — *SVT 2de GT — Manuel Libre de Sciences de la vie et de la Terre*, student manual, Thème 3 / "Cerveau, plaisir et sexualité" / "Identités et orientations" — repository path as cited in the placements, in place of a printed page)
351. **Endocrine disruptors** (`biology/endocrine-disruptors`) — substances that interfere with the hormone system, taught by France inside its hormones and human procreation chapter: bisphenol A — its effects, and its ban in certain uses — and DDT, presented in the chapter's documents as an endocrine-system lure (France, Grade 10 — *SVT 2de GT — Manuel Libre de Sciences de la vie et de la Terre*, student manual, Thème 3 / "Hormones et procréation humaine" / "Perturbateurs endocriniens" — repository path as cited in the placements, in place of a printed page)
352. **Modifying the microbiome — probiotics and faecal transplant** (`biology/modifying-the-microbiome-probiotics-and-faecal-transplant`) — the human microbiome deliberately modified as a treatment: faecal transplantation as a treatment, and probiotics — live micro-organisms given on purpose, their effects studied on weight and glycaemia (France, Grade 10 — *SVT 2de GT — Manuel Libre de Sciences de la vie et de la Terre*, student manual, Thème 3 / "Microbiote humain et santé" — repository path as cited in the placements, in place of a printed page)
353. **Support in plants and animals** (`biology/support-in-plants-and-animals`) — living things need support: a plant holds itself up towards the light and an animal's supporting structures hold its body up and give its muscles something to pull against; Sudan gives support a whole closing unit of its Grade 10 book, after the movement unit, covering support in plants and support in animals (Sudan, Grade 10 — *علم الأحياء – الصف الأول الثانوي (Biology — First Secondary Year)*, student textbook, Unit Five, printed pp. 137–149)
354. **Polygenic inheritance** (`biology/polygenic-inheritance`) — some traits are controlled not by one gene but by many genes together — polygenes, multiple-gene inheritance — each contributing a small part, so the trait grades through a population instead of splitting into neat kinds (Sudan, Grade 12 — *علم الأحياء – الصف الثالث الثانوي (Biology — Third Secondary Year)*, student textbook, Unit Two §5, printed p. 170)
355. **Telling plants from animals** (`biology/telling-plants-from-animals`) — plants and animals are told apart by how they live: plants make their own food with chlorophyll and stay rooted in one place, while animals eat other living things and move about (Uganda, Senior One — *Biology Textbook — Senior One (NCDC Prototype)*, student textbook, Ch 1, printed p. 8 — the named closing topic of the book's introduction chapter)
356. **Modified leaves** (`biology/modified-leaves`) — a leaf's ordinary flat green shape is only its starting shape: leaves are modified for other jobs — tendrils that climb (watermelon, passion fruit), traps that catch insects (Venus flytrap, pitcher plant), scales that store food (onion), spines that defend (cactus), swollen leaves that store water (bryophyllum, aloe vera, sisal) (Uganda, Senior One — *Biology Textbook — Senior One (NCDC Prototype)*, student textbook, Ch 5, Activity 5.6, printed pp. 81–82)
357. **Assessing an activity of integration** (`biology/assessing-an-activity-of-integration`) — an integrated real-life task is scored on a four-part evaluation grid: Accuracy, Coherence, Relevance, Excellence (Uganda, Senior One — *Biology Teacher's Guide — Senior One (NCDC Prototype)*, teacher's guide, closing pages — built on teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)
358. **Soil — properties and fertility** (`biology/soil-properties-and-fertility`) — soil is a mixture whose parts settle into measurable layers — shake soil with water in a bottle, let it settle, measure each layer's height — and soil has chemical properties as well as physical ones (Uganda, Senior Two — *Biology Home-Study Learning — Senior Two* (NCDC, 2020), student home-study booklet, Topic: Soil, Lesson 1, PDF pp. 7–10)
359. **Traditional healing and medicinal plants** (`biology/traditional-healing-and-medicinal-plants`) — health as balance between natural, social and supernatural elements; the taulasea, the local healer, and fofo massage and manipulation; the named Samoan medicinal plants — 'ava'avaaitu (Macropiper puberulum) for "ghost sickness" and its sosolo bark infusion for mouth infections and coughs, ginger, Matalafi (Psychotria insularum) the most-used plant, and Moso'i (Cananga odorata) for constipation, stomachache and postpartum sickness — taught in the same unit as antiseptics, antibiotics and vaccination (Samoa, Year 12 — *Biology — Year 12* (MESC Samoa, 2004), student textbook, Unit 1, printed pp. 47–57)
360. **Transpiration and the transpiration stream** (`biology/transpiration-and-the-transpiration-stream`) — water vapour lost from the plant pulls the transpiration stream up from roots to leaves; transpiration taught by its three roles — support, transport and cooling — with the factors affecting its rate investigated (Samoa, Year 13 — *Biology — Year 13 Learning Guide* (MESC Samoa, 2004), student learning guide, Unit 4, printed p. 35)
361. **Fish adaptations to life in water** (`biology/fish-adaptations-to-life-in-water`) — a fish's body shows how it is adapted to water: streamlined shape, fins for movement and balance, gills for taking oxygen from water, a swim bladder for depth, and the lateral line for sensing water movement — the driving question of Finland's perch-dissection materials, posed before the dissection and answered after it (Finland — *Kalan rakenne* / *Kalan preparointi* AOE open teaching materials, pupil worksheets and teacher guides, grades 3–6 and 7–9 as stated — evidence only, acceptance 0, printed pp. 2, 2, 2–4 and 5 across the four fish materials)
362. **Sleep and the body** (`biology/sleep-and-the-body`) — sleep comes in stages and can be measured: total sleep, deep sleep, REM sleep and awakenings read from a wearable, and sleep drives next-day coping and concentration (Finland, grades 8–9 as stated — *Hyvinvointia mittaamalla* (Wellbeing by Measuring), student work-card pack — open teaching material, evidence only, acceptance 0, Card 4, printed p. 17)
363. **Finland's Move! fitness monitoring** (`biology/finlands-move-fitness-monitoring`) — Move! is Finland's national system for monitoring school pupils' physical functional capacity: endurance, strength and mobility test batteries read against national reference values (oph.fi/move) (Finland, grades 8–9 as stated — *Hyvinvointia mittaamalla*, student work-card pack — open teaching material, evidence only, acceptance 0, Card 14, printed p. 50)
364. **Ecosystem services — what nature does for us** (`biology/ecosystem-services-what-nature-does-for-us`) — nature's work for people, in three groups: provisioning (food, timber), regulating/supporting (pollination, water purification, nutrient cycling, carbon binding) and cultural (recreation); more diverse nature adapts better to climate change, disease and pests (Finland — *Luonnon monimuotoisuuden tutkiminen maastossa* (Exploring biodiversity in the field), teacher field-work pack — open teaching material, evidence only, acceptance 0, printed pp. 3–4)
365. **Invasive alien species and how they spread** (`biology/invasive-alien-species-and-how-they-spread`) — species that crossed impassable barriers with human help and now spread and harm native nature — Finland's cases: mink (fur-farm escapee devastating archipelago birds), Spanish slug (arrived 1994, omnivorous mass species), garden lupin (outcompetes native plants), giant hogweeds (sap + sunlight causes burn-like skin damage); control by removal work parties, starting at first sighting (Finland — *Luonnon monimuotoisuuden tutkiminen maastossa*, teacher field-work pack — open teaching material, evidence only, acceptance 0, printed pp. 4, 15–16)
366. **Mineral nutrition of green plants** (`biology/mineral-nutrition-of-green-plants`) — a green plant does not live on light, air and water alone: it must take in mineral elements, and only some are essential — leave one out and the plant shows a deficiency symptom; hydroponics, growing plants in a measured mineral solution with no soil, is the test that proves the essential list, and nitrogen fixation turns air's nitrogen into a form plants can use (India, Class XI — *Biology — Textbook for Class XI* (NCERT, 2020-21 edition), student textbook, Ch. 12, printed p. 194)

367. **The biogenetic law and developmental patterns** (`biology/the-biogenetic-law-and-developmental-patterns`) — in its individual development (ontogeny), an organism briefly repeats the stages its group passed through in its evolutionary history (phylogeny) — the biogenetic law, stated as a law at the close of the development chapter, after the embryonic and postembryonic periods (Uzbekistan, Grade 9 — *Биология — Основы цитологии и генетики (Biology: Fundamentals of Cytology and Genetics)*, student textbook, Part IV Ch. 10 §42, printed p. 101)

368. **Food security and climate hazards** (`biology/food-security-and-climate-hazards`) — the world population's food supply taught as a biology topic, closing the Grade 9 nutrition sequence, with drought and flood hazards (ган гачиг, үер усны аюул) named as the dangers that cut it (Mongolia, Grade 9 — *Биологи IX (Biology, Grade 9)*, student textbook, Ch. 2 §2.2.4, printed p. 37)

369. **Cytoskeleton, cilia and flagella** (`biology/cytoskeleton-cilia-and-flagella`) — the cytoskeleton inside the cell holds its shape and moves things within it, while cilia beat and flagella whip to move the cell itself — taught as the named lesson “cells move” (Italy, Grade 9 — *La nuova biologia.blu PLUS — Le cellule e i viventi* (Sadava et al., Zanichelli), student textbook, A4.7, printed p. A80)

370. **The biology of cancer** (`biology/the-biology-of-cancer`) — cancer studied as a subject of biology in itself, given a full closing chapter — C12 — after all eleven system chapters of the human-body volume, a closing chapter most school books omit (Italy, Grade 11 — *La nuova biologia.blu PLUS — Il corpo umano* (Sadava et al., Zanichelli), student textbook, C12, PDF p. 267)

371. **Endosymbiosis: the origin of mitochondria and chloroplasts** (`biology/endosymbiosis-origin-of-mitochondria-and-chloroplasts`) — mitochondria and chloroplasts began as free-living bacteria living inside another cell — the endosymbiosis hypothesis (Lynn Margulis, 1967), taught as an “ancient pact” at the close of the cell unit (Italy, Grade 9 — *Campbell — Biologia: concetti e collegamenti PLUS, Primo biennio* (Linx/Pearson Italia), student textbook, Unità 2 closing, printed p. 62)

372. **Programmed cell death and immortal cells** (`biology/programmed-cell-death-and-immortal-cells`) — cells are mortal and much of their death is programmed — apoptosis, the cell dismantling itself on schedule — while immortal cells escape that programme, taught in the “Training for the future” close of the cell-division unit (Italy, Grade 9 — *Campbell — Biologia: concetti e collegamenti PLUS, Primo biennio* (Linx/Pearson Italia), student textbook, Unità 4 closing, printed p. 120)

373. **The genetic basis of cancer** (`biology/the-genetic-basis-of-cancer`) — cancer depends on mutations in the genes that control cell division, with many gene alterations contributing through defective proteins that interfere with signal transduction — the closing run of the molecular-biology-of-the-gene unit (Italy, Grade 11 — *Campbell — Biologia: concetti e collegamenti, Secondo biennio* (Linx/Pearson Italia), student textbook, Unità 1 closing, printed pp. 48–50)

374. **Regulation of gene expression** (`biology/regulation-of-gene-expression`) — which genes a cell expresses, when, and how much is controlled — regulation given a whole unit of its own, straight after the molecular biology of the gene (Italy, Grade 11 — *Campbell — Biologia: concetti e collegamenti, Secondo biennio* (Linx/Pearson Italia), student textbook, Unità 2, PDF p. 46)

375. **Cells as colonies and filaments — Volvox and Spirogyra** (`biology/cells-as-colonies-and-filaments-volvox-and-spirogyra`) — between one cell and a tissue stand the colony — Volvox, a hollow coenobium of up to 20,000 cells, reproducing asexually and sexually — and the filament — Spirogyra, cells joined end to end, each with a spiral chloroplast, multiplying by fragmentation (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

376. **Chemosynthesis — food-making without light** (`biology/chemosynthesis-nitrifying-and-sulphur-bacteria`) — autotrophs that build food with chemical energy instead of light: Nitrosomonas oxidising ammonium compounds and Nitrobacter oxidising nitrites — the nitrifying steps of the nitrogen cycle — and the sulphur bacteria oxidising sulphur compounds (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

377. **Growth curves — sigmoid and intermittent growth** (`biology/growth-curves-sigmoid-and-intermittent`) — growth measured and graphed: the smooth S-shaped sigmoid curve of most organisms against the stepped, intermittent curve of insects, which grow only at each moult; the fastest-growing regions of root and stem found by marking experiments, and the environmental factors growth needs (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

378. **Plant growth hormones** (`biology/plant-growth-hormones`) — the five hormone families — auxins, cytokinins, gibberellins, abscisic acid and ethylene — with auxin and apical dominance explained through hedge-cutting: remove the tip and the side buds are released (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

379. **Cell reactions — tactic and nastic movements** (`biology/cell-reactions-tactic-and-nastic-movements`) — cells and organisms answer their surroundings by moving: tactic movements of a whole cell or organism towards or away from a stimulus, and the nastic responses whose direction does not depend on the stimulus's direction (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

380. **Parasitic nutrition and parasite adaptations** (`biology/parasitic-nutrition-and-parasite-adaptations`) — parasitic nutrition built on West African parasites — Fasciola in sheep liver, Taenia solium and T. saginata, Wuchereria bancrofti (elephantiasis), the guinea-worm Dracunculus medinensis, plant nematodes of tomato and banana roots — and the adaptations listed one by one: attachment hooks and suckers, anti-enzymes, millions of eggs, intermediate hosts, host specificity (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

381. **Filter feeding and deposit feeding** (`biology/filter-feeding-and-deposit-feeding`) — feeding without hunting: filter feeding through the gill rakers of herrings and sardines and the baleen of the blue whale, and deposit feeding through soil and sediment, digesting the organic matter out of it (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

382. **Measuring abiotic factors in the field** (`biology/measuring-abiotic-factors-in-the-field`) — each abiotic factor with its instrument — temperature, relative humidity by wet-and-dry-bulb hygrometer, wind vane and anemometer, light meter, aneroid barometer for elevation — worked with Nigerian data: the Jos mean monthly temperature table (Federal Meteorological Service, Lagos), Nigerian day length shortest in December and longest in July, and short-day flowering of dry-season okro (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

383. **Nigeria's vegetation belts — the local biomes** (`biology/nigerias-vegetation-belts-local-biomes`) — Nigeria's own vegetation belts as local biomes: rain forest, the Southern and Northern Guinea savannahs (shea butter and locust bean trees, Isoberlinia doka), and the Sahel in Borno State near Lake Chad, worked with Maiduguri rainfall figures (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

384. **Agricultural classification of crops** (`biology/agricultural-classification-of-crops`) — plants classified twice over — botanically, and agriculturally by product, useful part and economic importance into twelve groups (root, vegetable, cereal, legume, fibre, oil, latex, beverage, fruit, spice, forage, cash crops) with Nigerian examples throughout: cassava, yam, guinea corn, benniseed, kola, oil palm (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

385. **Effects of agricultural activities on ecosystems** (`biology/effects-of-agricultural-activities-on-ecosystems`) — what clearing, the farming systems and bush burning do to the ecosystem — taught crop by crop and practice by practice (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

386. **Improving crop yield** (`biology/improving-crop-yield`) — five ways: soil conservation (terracing, mulching, rotation, no bush burning), fertilisers and manure, cultural practices, plant breeding for improved varieties (disease-resistant cassava, budded oranges), and putting more land under cultivation (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

387. **Food preservation and storage** (`biology/food-preservation-and-storage`) — the causes of harvest wastage (incomplete and late harvesting, weather, pests, poor storage — yams in barns) and the preservation methods with their principles: heating, freezing, and salting and sugaring by osmosis (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

388. **Tsetse fly, trypanosomiasis and vector control** (`biology/tsetse-fly-trypanosomiasis-and-vector-control`) — control of disease through the vector: the mosquito programme, then the tsetse fly's full life history and the control built on it — clearing shade resting places, spraying vehicles and resting sites, breeding nagana-resistant cattle, prompt treatment of sleeping sickness; antelopes as harmless reservoirs (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

389. **Public health — refuse, sewage and food safety** (`biology/public-health-refuse-sewage-and-food-safety`) — community health as a shared duty: refuse disposal (dustbin, incinerator, burial), sewage disposal (pit latrines, bucket toilets, flush toilet and septic tank with sludge turned to fertiliser), protection of water and food, and Ministry of Health inspection of abattoirs and eating places (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

390. **Estuaries, mangroves and the Lagos lagoon** (`biology/estuaries-mangroves-and-the-lagos-lagoon`) — estuary types (the open mouths of the Ogun, Osun and Osse rivers, the Niger delta, the Lagos lagoon system), salinity varying with tide and season, and the observed zonation on red-mangrove stilt roots — Mercierella where salinity stays high, the oyster Gryphaea where it is lower, the barnacle Balanus where it falls lowest; red mangrove (Rhizophora racemosa) adaptations (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

391. **The marsh habitat** (`biology/the-marsh-habitat`) — the marsh as a terrestrial-aquatic border habitat — waterlogged ground between open water and dry land — and its plant and animal adaptations (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

392. **Tropical rainforest — structure and adaptations** (`biology/tropical-rainforest-structure-and-adaptations`) — the rain forest studied practically: storeys, drip tips, buttress roots, cauliflory, climbers and shade plants, arboreal animal adaptations, and forest food chains built from the species found (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

393. **Savannah grasslands and derived savannah** (`biology/savannah-grasslands-and-derived-savannah`) — grassland characteristics and world names (prairies, steppes, pampas, veldt), Nigeria's savannah types — and derived savannah, the grassland humans maintain by farming and bush burning inside the rain-forest climate, which reverts to forest when the pressure stops (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

394. **Arid lands and desert adaptations** (`biology/arid-lands-and-desert-adaptations`) — the arid lands and how their plants and animals are fitted to them — water kept, water loss cut, scarcity endured (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

395. **Reproduction in earthworm and garden snail** (`biology/reproduction-in-earthworm-and-garden-snail`) — two hermaphrodites that still cross-fertilise: earthworms exchanging sperm at the clitellum with fertilisation inside the slipped-off cocoon; snails exchanging sperm and laying egg batches in the soil (Nigeria, Grade 10 — *Senior Secondary Biology 1* (Learn Africa Plc, Third edition 2013), student textbook)

396. **Plant and animal tissues** (`biology/plant-and-animal-tissues`) — a tissue is a group of similar cells doing one job together: Yemen's Grade 11 book teaches the animal tissues — epithelial (printed p. 25), connective (p. 31), muscular (p. 39), nervous (p. 42) — then the plant tissues, permanent (p. 45) and compound permanent, the vascular tissues (p. 49), and its activities book keys six microscope practicals to the same ground (Yemen, Grade 11 — *علم الأحياء وعلم الأرض — للصف الثاني الثانوي* (Biology and Earth Science, Second Secondary), student textbook, Unit 2, printed pp. 25–49; *الأنشطة والتجارب العملية* (Activities and Practical Experiments), student activities/practical book, Activities 3–8, printed pp. 12–27)

397. **Noise and radiation pollution** (`biology/noise-and-radiation-pollution`) — pollution the eye cannot see in air or water: noise pollution (التلوث الضوضائي, printed p. 173) and radiation pollution (التلوث الإشعاعي, printed p. 176) taught as named pollution kinds of their own, beside food pollutants (p. 178) and soil pollution (p. 180) (Yemen, Grade 12 — *علم الأحياء وعلم الأرض — للصف الثالث الثانوي* (Biology and Earth Science, Third Secondary), student textbook, Unit 7, printed pp. 173–180)
398. **Cell signalling and communication** (`biology/cell-signalling-and-communication`) — cells talk: a signalling molecule released by one cell binds the matching receptor on a target cell, the signal is propagated inside through a relay chain that amplifies it, and the cell responds — genes switched, enzymes started; single-celled organisms signal too, as quorum sensing shows (United States, Grade 12 — *Biology for AP® Courses* (OpenStax, Rice University), student textbook, Ch. 9, PDF pp. 371–395; and Grade 13 record — *Biology 2e* (OpenStax, Rice University, 2nd edition), student textbook, Ch. 9, PDF p. 257)
399. **Biological waste and lab accident management** (`biology/biological-waste-and-lab-accident-management`) — biological waste is sorted and disposed of by set methods — what may and may not go down the sink — and a lab accident is met by a set order of steps (Malaysia, Grade 10 — *Biology Form 4 (DLP)*, student textbook, Ch. 1, printed p. 6; parallel Bahasa Melayu edition *Biologi Tingkatan 4* at the same printed page)
400. **Membrane transport in daily life — food preservation** (`biology/membrane-transport-in-daily-life-food-preservation`) — the membrane chapter applied: osmosis at work in daily life, salt and sugar drawing water out of food and out of the microbes that would spoil it (Malaysia, Grade 10 — *Biology Form 4 (DLP)*, student textbook, Ch. 3, printed p. 65; parallel Bahasa Melayu edition *Biologi Tingkatan 4* at the same printed page)
401. **Blood clotting mechanism** (`biology/blood-clotting-mechanism`) — a cut vessel sealed by a mechanism: platelets and plasma proteins build a fibrin net that traps blood cells (Malaysia, Grade 10 — *Biology Form 4 (DLP)*, student textbook, Ch. 10, printed p. 183; parallel Bahasa Melayu edition *Biologi Tingkatan 4* at the same printed page)
402. **Formation of twins** (`biology/formation-of-twins`) — identical twins from one fertilised egg that splits, fraternal twins from two eggs fertilised separately — examined by comparison (Malaysia, Grade 10 — *Biology Form 4 (DLP)*, student textbook, Ch. 15, printed p. 300; parallel Bahasa Melayu edition *Biologi Tingkatan 4* at the same printed page)
403. **Meristems and plant growth curves** (`biology/meristems-and-plant-growth-curves`) — growth from the meristems — dividing tissues at root and shoot tips — and growth measured as curves: the sigmoid S-curve and the stepped curve (Malaysia, Grade 11 — *Biology Form 5 (DLP)*, student textbook, Ch. 1, printed p. 6; parallel Bahasa Melayu edition *Biologi Tingkatan 5* at the same printed page)
404. **Compensation point** (`biology/compensation-point`) — the light intensity at which photosynthesis exactly balances respiration — no net gain, no net loss (Malaysia, Grade 11 — *Biology Form 5 (DLP)*, student textbook, Ch. 2, printed p. 52; parallel Bahasa Melayu edition *Biologi Tingkatan 5* at the same printed page)
405. **Diversity in plant nutrition — carnivorous, epiphytes and parasites** (`biology/diversity-in-plant-nutrition-carnivorous-epiphytes-parasites`) — three non-standard ways of feeding: carnivorous plants trapping insects for minerals, epiphytes perched for support while making their own food, parasites stealing from a host (Malaysia, Grade 11 — *Biology Form 5 (DLP)*, student textbook, Ch. 3, printed p. 68; parallel Bahasa Melayu edition *Biologi Tingkatan 5* at the same printed page)
406. **Pollen and embryo sac development** (`biology/pollen-and-embryo-sac-development`) — the gamete-carrying structures built before pollination: pollen grains in the anther, the embryo sac with its egg cell inside the ovule — both through meiosis (Malaysia, Grade 11 — *Biology Form 5 (DLP)*, student textbook, Ch. 6, printed p. 116; parallel Bahasa Melayu edition *Biologi Tingkatan 5* at the same printed page)
407. **Plant adaptations to habitats** (`biology/plant-adaptations-to-habitats`) — plants fitted to the water of their habitat: xerophytes in dry places, hydrophytes in water, halophytes in salty ground, epiphytes perched above it (Malaysia, Grade 11 — *Biology Form 5 (DLP)*, student textbook, Ch. 7, printed p. 134; parallel Bahasa Melayu edition *Biologi Tingkatan 5* at the same printed page)
408. **Green technology** (`biology/green-technology`) — technology designed to protect the environment, taught by name as a practice of environmental sustainability (Malaysia, Grade 11 — *Biology Form 5 (DLP)*, student textbook, Ch. 10, printed p. 218; parallel Bahasa Melayu edition *Biologi Tingkatan 5* at the same printed page)
409. **Variation — continuous and discontinuous** (`biology/variation-continuous-and-discontinuous`) — variation sorted by kind: continuous variation grading smoothly (height, mass) against discontinuous variation in clear separate groups (blood group), and the genetic and environmental factors behind both (Malaysia, Grade 11 — *Biology Form 5 (DLP)*, student textbook, Ch. 12, printed p. 256; parallel Bahasa Melayu edition *Biologi Tingkatan 5* at the same printed page)
410. **Affective relationships and attachment** (`biology/affective-relationships-and-attachment`) — relationships have a biology: attachment — the bond a person builds with the people close to them — and the right to a life free of violence in relationships, taught as a named unit, "Biología de las relaciones afectivas", inside the biology book itself (Bolivia, Grade 8 — *Texto de Aprendizaje — Biología-Geografía — Segundo Año de Secundaria*, student textbook, printed pp. 156–158)
411. **Lung volumes and capacities** (`biology/lung-volumes-and-capacities`) — not all the air in the lungs moves with every breath: tidal volume, the inspiratory and expiratory reserves, the residual volume that never leaves, and the capacities built by adding volumes — vital capacity above all (Bhutan, Grade 9 — *BIOLOGY — CLASS NINE*, student textbook, Ch. 5 d, printed pp. 99–100)
412. **Limiting factors and Blackman's law** (`biology/limiting-factors-and-blackmans-law`) — photosynthesis runs at the speed of its scarcest need: Blackman's law of limiting factors, read off the rising-then-flattening rate graph, with the utilisation of photosynthesis products (Bhutan, Grade 10 — *BIOLOGY — CLASS 10*, student textbook, Ch. 2.1, printed pp. 37–38)
413. **The pressure-flow hypothesis** (`biology/the-pressure-flow-hypothesis`) — phloem sap is pushed from source to sink by a pressure difference: loading and osmosis raise the pressure at the source, unloading drops it at the sink (Bhutan, Grade 10 — *BIOLOGY — CLASS 10*, student textbook, Ch. 2.2, printed p. 41)
414. **Hormones in medicine and anabolic steroids** (`biology/hormones-in-medicine-and-anabolic-steroids`) — hormones as medicines — replacement, insulin for diabetes the standing example — and anabolic steroids, the synthetic male-hormone copies misused in sport, taught as one topic with its costs (Bhutan, Grade 10 — *BIOLOGY — CLASS 10*, student textbook, Ch. 4.2, printed pp. 104–106)
415. **The Hardy–Weinberg law** (`biology/the-hardy-weinberg-law`) — in a population that is not evolving, allele and genotype frequencies stay constant — p² + 2pq + q² = 1 — the balance against which selection, mutation, migration and drift are detected (Bhutan, Grade 10 — *BIOLOGY — CLASS 10*, student textbook, Ch. 6.4, printed pp. 166–167)

## Unit 18 — Physics

1. **A current makes a magnetic field** (`physics/a-current-makes-a-magnetic-field`) — current creates magnetism — A compass needle turns beside a current-carrying wire: current makes a circular magnetic field (B = μ₀I/2πr for a long straight wire), quantified by …
2. **A current makes a magnetic field** (`physics/a-current-makes-a-magnetic-field-g10`) — A current's field forms concentric circles around the wire.
3. **Absolute and gauge pressure** (`physics/absolute-and-gauge-pressure`) — pressure in gases; absolute and gauge pressure — Gas pressure from trapped, squeezed air (the crush-the-air activity); absolute pressure counts from vacuum, gauge pressure from …
4. **Acceleration — speeding up and slowing down** (`physics/acceleration-speeding-up-and-slowing-down`) — speeding up and slowing down — Average and instantaneous acceleration as rate of change of velocity; the sign discussion is explicit: negative acceleration does not always mean …
5. **Acceleration — steady change of velocity** (`physics/acceleration-steady-change-of-velocity`) — Acceleration is a vector — rate of change of velocity.
6. **Acceleration — velocity changing** (`physics/acceleration-velocity-changing`) — Acceleration is the rate of change of velocity.
7. **Action and reaction — Newton's third law; weight** (`physics/action-and-reaction-newtons-third-law-and-weight`) — newton's third law and weight — Action–reaction pairs act on different bodies; weight is gravity's force on a mass (W = mg) and is distinguished from mass itself, with normal fo…
8. **Adding light, subtracting light — colour mixing** (`physics/adding-light-subtracting-light-colour-mixing`) — Additive: R+G = yellow, G+B = cyan, B+R = magenta, R+G+B = white.
9. **Adding vectors algebraically** (`physics/adding-vectors-algebraically`) — algebraic addition and subtraction in two dimensions — With components, addition and subtraction become arithmetic on each axis separately; exercises mix graphical checks with a…
10. **Adding vectors by drawing — triangle and parallelogram** (`physics/adding-vectors-by-drawing`) — Triangle method: nose-to-tail; resultant from first tail to last head.
11. **Adding and subtracting vectors in a line** (`physics/adding-vectors-in-a-line`) — Choose a positive direction; vectors along it are +, against it −.
12. **Adding vectors with drawings** (`physics/adding-vectors-with-drawings`) — graphical addition — Vectors are added head-to-tail (triangle rule), by the parallelogram rule for two vectors at a point, and by the polygon method for several; a scale diagram…
13. **Air pressure around us** (`physics/air-pressure-around-us`) — atmospheric pressure — Sealed-bottle and hot-water bottle activities show air pressure crushing and holding; atmospheric pressure explains drinking through a straw and the flow …
14. **Ammeter in series, voltmeter in parallel** (`physics/ammeter-in-series-voltmeter-in-parallel`) — Ammeter in series, with low resistance, measures current.
15. **Ammeters and voltmeters — where they go** (`physics/ammeters-and-voltmeters-where-they-go`) — measuring instruments — Ammeters go in series (near-zero resistance), voltmeters in parallel (near-infinite resistance); their connections are figured and exercised.
16. **Amplifiers and switches** (`physics/amplifiers-and-switches`) — transistors at work — As amplifier, the transistor enlarges a signal (the common-emitter amplifier inverts the phase); as switch, it turns devices fully on or off — the two jobs…
17. **Analog vs digital** (`physics/analog-vs-digital`) — analog and digital signals; logic levels — Analog signals vary continuously; digital signals live at two levels — high/low, 1/0 — with positive and negative logic assigning the …
18. **Archimedes' principle — the upthrust** (`physics/archimedes-principle-the-upthrust`) — archimedes' principle and buoyant force — The upthrust equals the weight of fluid displaced (F_B = ρ_fluid g V), derived from the pressure difference between a submerged block's…
19. **Average speed and instantaneous speed** (`physics/average-speed-and-instantaneous-speed`) — Average speed = total distance ÷ total time.
20. **Average velocity and instantaneous velocity** (`physics/average-velocity-and-instantaneous-velocity`) — Average velocity = displacement ÷ time, with direction.
21. **Balance and movement — physics of the body** (`physics/balance-and-movement-physics-of-the-body`) — physics of the living body — Newtonian mechanics explains animal and human movement: stability is centre of mass over the base of support (a load-carrier bends to keep it there;…
22. **Barometers and manometers** (`physics/barometers-and-manometers`) — measuring pressure — The mercury barometer reads atmospheric pressure as a column height (P = ρgh); open and closed manometers read gas pressure from level differences, adding o…
23. **Binding energy and mass defect** (`physics/binding-energy-and-mass-defect`) — mass defect and binding energy — A nucleus weighs less than its separate nucleons; the missing mass is the binding energy via E = mc² (worked in MeV with the 931.5 MeV-per-atomi…
24. **Blood, voice and eye — more physics in the body** (`physics/blood-voice-and-eye-more-physics-in-the-body`) — physics of the living body — Blood circulation runs on fluid-flow physics (viscosity, continuity, turbulence) and soft-bodied animals like the earthworm move by Pascal's princip…
25. **Calorimetry — mixing hot and cold** (`physics/calorimetry-mixing-hot-and-cold`) — calorimetry — Hot and cold bodies mixed in an insulated calorimeter exchange heat until they settle together: heat lost = heat gained, the equation that finds unknown specific h…
26. **Capacitor networks and stored energy** (`physics/capacitor-networks-and-stored-energy`) — capacitors in combination and stored energy — Series capacitors combine reciprocally, parallel ones add; a charged capacitor stores energy (½CV²) in its field, released on disch…
27. **Capacitors — storing charge** (`physics/capacitors-storing-charge`) — capacitors and capacitance — A capacitor stores charge on separated plates; capacitance C = Q/V is measured in farads, and the parallel-plate value depends on area, separation a…
28. **Careers that use physics** (`physics/careers-that-use-physics`) — the mission of physics and careers in physics — Physics aims to understand nature's laws and put them to work; the section lists career fields open to physics students — astrono…
29. **Centre of mass** (`physics/centre-of-mass`) — centre of mass — A system's centre of mass is the mass-weighted average position; worked for three point masses on a grid, then its motion: a flipped baseball bat wobbles around…

228. **Thermodynamics — the laws and heat engines** (`physics/thermodynamics-laws-and-heat-engines`) — the laws as a whole — First law ΔU = Q − W (energy conserved; heat and work are its two doors), second law (heat flows hot to cold, entropy grows, no engine turns all its heat into work), third law (absolute zero approached but never reached), and heat engines cycling hot source → work → cold sink, their efficiency capped by Carnot's limit (Ecuador, Tema 13 Termodinámica).
229. **Thermometers and temperature scales — including making your own** (`physics/thermometers-and-temperature-scales`) — A thermometer uses a thermometric property that changes with heat.

253. **Uniform circular motion — period, frequency and angular velocity** (`physics/uniform-circular-motion-period-frequency-and-angular-velocity`) — uniform circular motion itself — a body rounds a circle at constant speed: the period T (seconds per turn) and the frequency f = 1/T (turns per second, in hertz), the linear (tangential) velocity v = 2πr/T = 2πrf pointing along the tangent, and the angular velocity ω = 2π/T = 2πf, the two speeds joined by v = ωr — the lesson the centripetal lessons build on (Nicaragua, Grade 10 — Física, Módulo Autoformativo, Décimo grado, Unidad II §§2.1–2.3).
254. **Unit vectors — writing vectors with i and j** (`physics/unit-vectors-writing-vectors-with-i-and-j`) — unit vectors and the i, j notation — A unit vector has magnitude one and only points; the special unit vectors i and j along the x and y axes let any vector be written as a comb…
255. **Universal gravitation** (`physics/universal-gravitation`) — newton's law of universal gravitation — Every mass attracts every other with F = Gm₁m₂/r²; a worked example weighs a 60 kg person by the formula (≈584 N, checked against mg ≈ 58…
256. **Wave words — period, frequency, wavelength, amplitude** (`physics/wave-words`) — Period is the time for one complete oscillation; frequency is oscillations per second, in hertz.
257. **Wave–particle duality of light** (`physics/wave-particle-duality-of-light`) — wave–particle duality — Slits give interference bands (waves); the photoelectric effect gives photons, one packet freeing one electron with E = hf (particles); light shows the face the experiment asks for (Brazil, Eletromagnetismo Ch. 13).
258. **What a force is — pushes, pulls and weight** (`physics/what-a-force-is`) — A force is a push or a pull, measured in newtons.
259. **What heat is** (`physics/what-heat-is`) — the concept of heat — Heat is energy in transit between bodies at different temperatures; heating changes temperature, size and state — and heat is distinguished from work and f…
260. **What physics is** (`physics/what-physics-is`) — Physics comes from Greek phusis, meaning nature.
261. **What temperature change does** (`physics/what-temperature-change-does`) — Heating and cooling change matter, environments and living things.
262. **Wheel and axle** (`physics/wheel-and-axle`) — Wheel and axle: a large wheel fixed to a smaller axle, turning together.
263. **When waves meet — superposition and standing waves** (`physics/when-waves-meet`) — Superposition: displacements of meeting waves add.
264. **Where physics knowledge comes from** (`physics/where-physics-knowledge-comes-from`) — how physics knowledge is made — Knowledge begins in experience: sensory perception (seeing, touching, hearing), introspection (examining one's own feelings and thoughts, as when…
265. **Why measurements need standards — the SI base quantities** (`physics/why-measurements-need-standards`) — A standard must be accessible, reliable, and give the same result anywhere.
266. **Why ships float** (`physics/why-ships-float`) — sinking, floating, and big ships — Fully submerged objects rise or sink by density; a floating body displaces its own weight, with the submerged fraction set by the density rati…
267. **Work–energy theorem; power** (`physics/work-energy-theorem-and-power`) — the work–energy theorem and power — Net work equals the change in kinetic energy; power is the rate of doing work (watts), applied to machines and people in worked examples.
268. **Work — force times distance** (`physics/work-force-times-distance`) — work — Work is force times displacement in the force's direction; angled forces contribute only their along-motion component, and work can be zero or negative.
269. **Work in physics — force times distance** (`physics/work-in-physics`) — Work W = F × s, measured in joules.
270. **Young's modulus — how stiff a material is** (`physics/youngs-modulus`) — Y = stress ÷ strain — a property of the material, not the specimen's size.
271. **Zeroth law of thermodynamics and temperature equilibrium** (`physics/zeroth-law-of-thermodynamics-and-temperature-equilibrium`) — the zeroth law and thermal equilibrium — Heat flows from hotter to colder until temperatures match; if A and B each match a third body they match each other — the law that makes temperature measurable and thermometers work (Brazil, Termodinâmica Ch. 4).
272. **Signals — a message carried by sound, light or wire** (`physics/signals-a-message-carried-by-sound-light-or-wire`) — a signal is a message made travel-ready: emitter, carrier, receiver — whistles and drums on sound, flares and lighthouses on light, telephones and doorbells on electricity in wires, and distress patterns (the flare, SOS) built to be unmissable — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Gabon, Grade 5 — Super Efficace, Guide pédagogique, 5e année)
273. **Brownian motion and the kinetic theory of matter** (`physics/brownian-motion-and-the-kinetic-theory-of-matter`) — the visible evidence for the particle picture: specks suspended in a fluid jiggle forever, kicked unevenly by the fluid's own invisible moving particles — the kinetic theory (particles flying in gases, sliding in liquids, vibrating in solids) then explains temperature as average kinetic energy, diffusion as random-motion mixing, and gas pressure as wall collisions (Palestine, Grade 10 — Physics for Grade 10, Unit 3, Ch. 2: Thermodynamics, printed p. 108)
274. **The gas laws — Boyle, Charles and the pressure law** (`physics/the-gas-laws-boyle-charles-and-the-pressure-law`) — the three laws of a fixed mass of gas: Boyle (at constant temperature, P × V constant, proved by the sealed-syringe activity), Charles (at constant pressure, V/T constant) and the pressure law (at constant volume, P/T constant), temperatures always in kelvin, all three gathered in the combined law P₁V₁/T₁ = P₂V₂/T₂ (Palestine, Grade 10 — Physics for Grade 10, Unit 3, Ch. 2: Thermodynamics, printed p. 112)
275. **EMF, internal resistance and terminal voltage of a source** (`physics/emf-internal-resistance-and-terminal-voltage`) — a real source's EMF is the energy it gives each coulomb, but its own internal resistance spends part of it as lost volts Ir, so the terminal voltage V = E − Ir sags as current is drawn — measured together on the V–I graph (intercept E, slope r), with sources in series and parallel — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Papua New Guinea, Grades 11–12 — Physics, Upper Secondary Teacher Guide (OBE), Unit 11.5, printed p. 35)
276. **Photons and the photoelectric effect** (`physics/photons-and-the-photoelectric-effect`) — light arrives in photons of E = hf (Planck's quantum hypothesis); a photon above a metal's threshold frequency frees one electron instantly, and Einstein's equation hf = φ + KE_max keeps the account — brightness counts electrons, frequency sets their speed — counted from Italy, Grade 12 — *L'Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 3*, Ch. 32 La crisi della fisica classica, the photoelectric effect strand, PDF p. 200; teacher-guide evidence only (Papua New Guinea, Grade 12 — Physics Teacher Guide Grade 12, Standards-Based, Topic Photons, printed p. 96 approx.)
277. **Quantum physics foundations** (`physics/quantum-physics-foundations`) — the foundations of the quantum world as one lesson: black-body radiation and Planck's quantisation (E = hf), the photoelectric effect and Compton scattering (photons carry energy and momentum), wave–particle duality (de Broglie λ = h/p) and the uncertainty principle Δx · Δp ≥ h/4π — counted from Italy, Grade 12 — *L'Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 3*, Ch. 32 La crisi della fisica classica: black-body radiation, Planck's quanta, photoelectric and Compton strands, PDF p. 200; curriculum-programme evidence only (Andorra — Programa de física del batxillerat general de l'Escola Andorrana, Decret 238/2025, Bloc 6, printed p. 37; Programa de física 2, Decret del 9-12-2020, Bloc 6, printed p. 34)
278. **Special relativity — basic principles** (`physics/special-relativity-basic-principles`) — Einstein's two postulates (physics the same in every inertial frame; c the same for every observer) and what they force: time dilation, length contraction, relative simultaneity, spacetime, general relativity in outline, and E = mc² — counted from Italy, Grade 12 — *L'Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 3*, Ch.29–31 Relatività ristretta: postulates, simultaneity and time dilation, Lorentz transformations, relativistic dynamics, mass–energy equivalence, PDF p. 130 (Italy also adds Sub lesson 4 — the Lorentz transformations and relativistic dynamics); curriculum-programme evidence only (Andorra — Programa de física del batxillerat general de l'Escola Andorrana, Decret 238/2025, Bloc 6, printed p. 37; Programa de física 2, Decret del 9-12-2020, Bloc 6, printed p. 34)
279. **Bernoulli's principle and flight** (`physics/bernoullis-principle-and-flight`) — beside the fluids lessons: along a streamline pressure + ½ρv² + ρgh stays constant, so faster flow means lower pressure (ΔP = ½ρ(v₂² − v₁²)) — the trade that lifts wings and sails (lift priced in numbers, angle of attack and the stall), pulls shower curtains in and drives atomisers, and lifts roofs off in a hurricane (fast wind above, still air inside pushing up) — curriculum evidence only, acceptance 0 (Bahamas — Curriculum Guidelines — Physics, Grades 10, 11, 12, Ministry of Education, 2010, Bernoulli's principle, printed p. xiv)
280. **AC circuits — RC and RL dipoles** (`physics/ac-circuits-rc-and-rl-dipoles`) — beside the AC and electronics lessons: alternating current through resistor-capacitor and resistor-inductor dipoles — capacitive reactance X_C = 1/(2πfC) falling with frequency and current leading the voltage by 90°, inductive reactance X_L = 2πfL rising with frequency and current lagging by 90°, and the series impedance Z = √(R² + X²) with I = V/Z, checked by a multimeter's part-voltages adding by squares (V² = V_R² + V_X²) — programme evidence only, acceptance 0 (Haiti — Programme détaillé NS, Physique 4e année / NS4, MENFP, Nouveau Secondaire, 2008–2009, Theme: variable currents, RC/RL dipoles, multimeter on domestic supply, PDF p. 6)

281. **How this Physics course works — the Italian (Amaldi) way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-amaldi-way`) — Italy's way: the three-volume Amaldi course for the licei scientifici (Vol. 1 mechanics and thermodynamics, Vol. 2 waves and fields, Vol. 3 induction, relativity and quanta) — every chapter splitting theory from exercises, model problems (Problemi modello) solved in full before the student's sets, “stop and think” (Fermati a pensare) prompts inside the theory, maths taught exactly when physics needs it (La matematica per la fisica), chapters closing with a formula map, a test and the “Sei pronto per la verifica?” self-test, and the whole course mapped onto the esame di Stato (Italy — *Il nuovo Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 1*, Zanichelli, 2020, printed pp. III–X; *L'Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 2*, 2012, PDF p. 2; *L'Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 3*, 2012, PDF p. 2)
282. **Non-inertial frames and apparent forces** (`physics/non-inertial-frames-and-apparent-forces`) — frames that accelerate or rotate need apparent forces: the phantom push −m·a_frame (the braking-bus lurch), the outward centrifugal force of a rotating frame, and the Coriolis deflection of Earth's winds — agentless bookkeeping that vanishes in an inertial description (Italy, Grade 9–10 — *Il nuovo Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 1*, Ch. 2 §4, printed p. 59)
283. **Thermodynamic systems and the heat engine** (`physics/thermodynamic-systems-and-heat-engines`) — the full systems treatment: the system and its internal energy, the first law ΔU = Q − W, the named transformations (isothermal, adiabatic, isobaric, isochoric, cyclic) with work as area on the p–V diagram, heat engines and why efficiency is capped, Carnot's cycle η = 1 − T_cold/T_hot, the second law and entropy with its microscopic reading (Italy, Grade 9–10 — *Il nuovo Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 1*, Ch. 10, printed p. 410; Ch. 11, printed p. 456)
284. **Electric current in metals — resistance and temperature, superconductivity** (`physics/electric-current-in-metals-resistance-and-superconductivity`) — current as the drift of a free-electron sea; resistivity rising with temperature as the vibrating lattice scatters the drift (ρ ≈ ρ₀(1 + αΔT)); electron extraction from metals; and superconductivity — resistance exactly zero below the critical temperature, Onnes's mercury of 1911 and the Meissner effect (Italy, Grade 11 — *L'Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 2*, Ch. 23, PDF p. 282)
285. **Alternating-current circuits — reactance, impedance and resonance** (`physics/alternating-current-circuits-reactance-impedance-and-resonance`) — the full AC lesson: the alternator and RMS values; capacitive reactance X_C = 1/(2πfC) falling with frequency and inductive reactance X_L = 2πfL rising; impedance Z = √(R² + (X_L − X_C)²) combining the parts by phase; and resonance at f₀ = 1/(2π√(LC)) — impedance minimum, current maximum — from radio tuning to power-system hazard (Italy, Grade 12 — *L'Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 3*, Ch. 27, PDF p. 60)
286. **Bohr's model of the hydrogen atom and energy levels** (`physics/bohrs-model-of-the-hydrogen-atom`) — Bohr's two postulates (allowed orbits mvr = nħ that radiate nothing; jumps that emit or absorb one photon hf = ΔE), the level ladder E_n = −13.6/n² eV with ionisation at 13.6 eV, the Lyman, Balmer and Paschen series as named falls, and the model's honest limits (Italy, Grade 12 — *L'Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 3*, Ch. 33, PDF p. 240)
287. **Matter waves and the uncertainty principle** (`physics/matter-waves-and-the-uncertainty-principle`) — de Broglie's λ = h/p for every particle and its electron-diffraction proof; bound electrons as standing probability waves (the real reason Bohr's rungs exist); and Heisenberg's Δx·Δp ≥ h/4π — a floor set by what a wave is, forcing zero-point motion in anything confined (Italy, Grade 12 — *L'Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 3*, Ch. 34, PDF p. 280)
288. **Elementary particles and quarks — the fundamental interactions** (`physics/elementary-particles-and-quarks`) — the Standard-Model survey: quarks confined in hadrons (proton uud, neutron udd), the elementary leptons, three generations with ordinary matter using only the first, antimatter and annihilation, the exchange particles of each interaction (photon, gluons, W and Z), and mass from the Higgs field (Italy, Grade 12 — *L'Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 3*, Ch. 35, PDF p. 330)
289. **Frontiers of physics — Planck units and the scientific worldview** (`physics/frontiers-of-physics-planck-units-and-the-scientific-worldview`) — the course's capstone: the three constants c, G and h combine into nature's own scale — Planck length ≈ 1.6 × 10⁻³⁵ m, time ≈ 5.4 × 10⁻⁴⁴ s, mass ≈ 2.2 × 10⁻⁸ kg, energy ≈ 2 × 10⁹ J — where general relativity and quantum theory must merge into a quantum gravity that does not yet exist; and the closing essay: physics advances by models tested against the world and revised when they fail (Italy, Grade 12 — *L'Amaldi per i licei scientifici.blu, Vol. 3*, Ch. 36, PDF p. 392)

290. **The vernier caliper and travelling microscope** (`physics/the-vernier-caliper-and-travelling-microscope`) — the vernier principle: 10 vernier divisions span 9 main divisions, so the least count is 0.1 mm; a caliper reading is main scale plus coinciding division × least count, corrected for zero error, across outside, inside and depth measures; the travelling microscope puts cross-wires on tiny features and takes lengths as the difference of two vernier readings (Sri Lanka, Grade 12 — *Science for Technology: Physics Part I*, §1.1, printed pp. 7–18)
291. **The micrometer screw gauge and the screw principle** (`physics/the-micrometer-screw-gauge-and-the-screw-principle`) — the screw principle: one turn advances the spindle by the pitch, so least count = pitch ÷ thimble divisions = 0.01 mm; reading sleeve then thimble, finishing on the ratchet, correcting zero error, and averaging measures taken at several places (Sri Lanka, Grade 12 — *Science for Technology: Physics Part I*, §1.2, printed pp. 20–21)
292. **Errors and error estimation in measurement** (`physics/errors-and-error-estimation-in-measurement`) — systematic errors corrected, random errors averaged, gross errors discarded; absolute, relative and percentage error; sums add absolute errors and products add percentage errors, and a result is reported as value ± error (Sri Lanka, Grade 12 — *Science for Technology: Physics Part I*, §1.5, printed p. 30)
293. **Resolving and combining forces — resultant and the parallelogram law** (`physics/resolving-and-combining-forces-resultant-and-the-parallelogram-law`) — a force resolves into F cos θ along and F sin θ across; two forces combine by the parallelogram law, R = √(A² + B² + 2AB cos θ); many forces are handled by summing resolved components ΣFx and ΣFy (Sri Lanka, Grade 12 — *Science for Technology: Physics Part I*, §§3.1.5–3.2, printed pp. 93–103)
294. **Angular momentum** (`physics/angular-momentum`) — L = mvr for a particle and L = Iω for a rigid body; torque is its rate of change, τ = dL/dt; with zero external torque L is conserved, so a spinner that reduces I must raise ω — the skater, the diver and the planets (Sri Lanka, Grade 12 — *Science for Technology: Physics Part II*, §1.10, printed p. 14)
295. **Efficiency of a machine** (`physics/efficiency-of-a-machine`) — efficiency = useful output ÷ total input, for work, energy or power, always below 100%; input = useful output + wasted output, and the waste is traced mainly to friction turned into heat, cut by lubrication and bearings but never removed (Sri Lanka, Grade 12 — *Science for Technology: Physics Part II*, §2.6, printed p. 43)
296. **Energy stored in a stretched wire** (`physics/energy-stored-in-a-stretched-wire`) — the work stored in stretching is ½Fx, the triangular area under the force–extension graph; per unit volume it is ½ × stress × strain, growing with the square of the strain, and it is returned in full only within the elastic limit (Sri Lanka, Grade 12 — *Science for Technology: Physics Part II*, §3.6, printed p. 54)
297. **Magnetic theory — poles, inverse-square law and magnetic measurements** (`physics/magnetic-theory-poles-inverse-square-law-and-magnetic-measurements`) — the classical pole theory: force between poles ∝ m₁m₂/r², field strength as force on a unit north pole, and measurement by the tangent law tan θ = B/H in the deflection magnetometer (Sri Lanka, Grade 12 — *Intermediate Physics, Part V: Magnetism and Electricity* (1968), Ch. 45, printed p. 169)
298. **Thermoelectricity — Seebeck effect and thermocouples** (`physics/thermoelectricity-seebeck-effect-and-thermocouples`) — unlike-metal junctions at different temperatures generate an EMF (Seebeck); the thermocouple turns it into a thermometer of tiny size, fast response and furnace range against a reference junction in ice; the Peltier effect runs the trade backwards (Sri Lanka, Grade 12 — *Intermediate Physics, Part V: Magnetism and Electricity* (1968), Ch. 51, printed p. 350)
299. **Magnetic properties of iron — permeability and hysteresis** (`physics/magnetic-properties-of-iron-permeability-and-hysteresis`) — permeability μ = B/H measures how strongly iron multiplies a field, up to saturation; the hysteresis loop gives remanence, coercive force and the energy lost as heat per cycle — narrow-loop soft iron for cores and electromagnets, wide-loop hard steel for permanent magnets (Sri Lanka, Grade 12 — *Intermediate Physics, Part V: Magnetism and Electricity* (1968), Ch. 54, printed p. 488)
300. **How this Physics course works — the Syrian Grade 7 way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-syrian-grade-7-way`) — the Syrian Grade 7 book's way: a research-and-experiment curriculum tied to daily life and the local environment; every lesson opens with printed objectives and key words, is built around an "I try and I conclude" bench experiment with an explicit "I conclude" box, worked applications and enrichment boxes, and units close with unit questions plus a hands-on project (Syria, Grade 7 — *Physics & Chemistry, Grade 7 Basic*, whole book, printed p. 3)
301. **How this Physics course works — the Syrian Grade 8 way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-syrian-grade-8-way`) — the same Syrian lesson design in the Grade 8 combined volume, physics and chemistry alternating by unit: U2 Motion & forces, U3 Electricity, U4 Light (Syria, Grade 8 — *Physics & Chemistry, Grade 8 Basic*, whole book, printed p. 3)
302. **How this Physics course works — the Syrian Grade 9 way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-syrian-grade-9-way`) — the Syrian lesson design in the Grade 9 combined volume, physics units first (Electricity & magnetism, Mechanics & energy, Waves & oscillations), chemistry after (Syria, Grade 9 — *Physics & Chemistry, Grade 9 Basic*, whole book, printed p. 3)
303. **How this Physics course works — the Syrian Grade 12 way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-syrian-grade-12-way`) — the Grade 12 book's derivation-first way: activities feed an "I conclude" box, then the mathematics is carried through in full across five units (Syria, Grade 12 — *Physics, Third Secondary, Scientific Track*, whole book, contents p. 6)
304. **Damped and forced oscillations — the torsion and gravity pendulums** (`physics/damped-and-forced-oscillations-the-torsion-and-gravity-pendulums`) — the torsion pendulum T = 2π√(I/κ) measures moments of inertia, the gravity pendulum T = 2π√(L/g) measures g; damping drains amplitude, and driving at the natural frequency gives resonance (Syria, Grade 12 — *Physics, Third Secondary, Scientific Track*, U1, contents pp. 20, 28)
305. **Standing waves in air columns — longitudinal standing waves** (`physics/standing-waves-in-air-columns-longitudinal-standing-waves`) — nodes and antinodes in pipes: a closed pipe fits λ = 4L and sounds odd harmonics only, an open pipe fits λ = 2L and sounds the full series (Syria, Grade 12 — *Physics, Third Secondary, Scientific Track*, U3, contents p. 182)
306. **Atomic spectra** (`physics/atomic-spectra`) — continuous, emission and absorption spectra; lines arise from electrons falling between fixed energy levels, h f = E_upper − E_lower, and each element's line pattern is a unique fingerprint (Syria, Grade 12 — *Physics, Third Secondary, Scientific Track*, U4, contents p. 198)
307. **X-rays and the laser** (`physics/x-rays-and-the-laser`) — X-rays from fast electrons striking a target: penetrating, ionising, bone-stopping; laser light from stimulated emission under population inversion: coherent, monochromatic, collimated (Syria, Grade 12 — *Physics, Third Secondary, Scientific Track*, U4, contents pp. 240, 246)
308. **The first law of thermodynamics — heat, work and internal energy in one account** (`physics/the-first-law-of-thermodynamics-heat-work-and-internal-energy-in-one-account`) — one account for a system's energy: ΔU = Q − W — heat in raises internal energy, work done by the system spends it; the adiabatic, isothermal and cyclic cases, and why every heat engine is this same bookkeeping run round and round (Slovenia, Grade 9 — *Fizika 9 — i-učbenik*, Heat and internal energy: Energijski zakon, printed p. 141 (unit folder 188))

309. **Damping and resonance** (`physics/damping-and-resonance`) — damping is the steady shrinking of an oscillation's amplitude as friction and air resistance drain its energy; resonance is the maximum-amplitude answer when a driving force arrives in step at the oscillator's natural frequency — the swing, the tuning fork, the bridge and the building damper (Malaysia, Form 4 — *Physics Form 4*, Ch. 5 Waves, §5.2 Damping and Resonance, printed p. 184)
310. **Diffraction of waves** (`physics/diffraction-of-waves`) — waves spread into the shadow region through gaps and past edges; the spread is strongest when the gap is about one wavelength wide — the rule that prices harbours, hearing around corners and why light needs a hair-wide slit to spread (Malaysia, Form 4 — *Physics Form 4*, Ch. 5 Waves, §5.5 Diffraction of Waves, printed p. 202)
311. **The thin lens formula and magnification** (`physics/the-thin-lens-formula-and-magnification`) — 1/f = 1/u + 1/v under the real-is-positive convention, and magnification m = v/u, worked through the projector, the camera and the concave lens (Malaysia, Form 4 — *Physics Form 4*, Ch. 6 Light and Optics, §6.4 Thin Lens Formula, printed p. 259)
312. **How this Physics course works — the Malaysian KSSM way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-malaysian-kssm-way`) — Malaysia's way: the Form 4 book organised by four themes (Elementary Physics; Newtonian Mechanics, with gravitation as its own chapter; Heat, with the gas laws inside it; Waves, Light and Optics, with mirrors last), Career Info boxes and 21st-century-skills activities inside the chapters, the same book issued in Malay and English DLP in parallel, and every chapter closing with a Performance Evaluation in SPM examination format (Malaysia, Form 4 — *Physics Form 4*, whole book)
313. **How this Physics course works — the Malaysian KSSM Form 5 way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-malaysian-kssm-form-5-way`) — the Form 5 completion of the two-year arc: four themes, all of pressure in one applied sweep (liquids, atmosphere, gas, Pascal, Archimedes, Bernoulli), electronics opening with the electron itself, and the course ending in modern physics — nuclear and a full Quantum Physics chapter as examined SPM core (Malaysia, Form 5 — *Physics Form 5*, whole book)

314. **The sign rule for motion equations** (`physics/the-sign-rule-for-motion-equations`) — before any motion equation is used, one direction is chosen as positive and every displacement, velocity and acceleration is signed by it — along is +, against is − — so with upward positive gravity is −9.8 m/s² for a thrown ball's whole flight, and the signed values are fed into v = u + at and its partners exactly as they stand (Sudan, Grade 10 — *Physics — First Secondary Year*, Ch 3: the sign rule, printed p. 31)
315. **Lami's theorem for three concurrent forces** (`physics/lamis-theorem-for-three-concurrent-forces`) — three forces at a point in equilibrium each stand over the sine of the angle between the other two, F₁ / sin α = F₂ / sin β = F₃ / sin γ, pricing the tensions in two strings that hold a hanging body from the angles alone (Sudan, Grade 11 — *Physics — Second Secondary Year*, Unit 3 worked examples, printed p. 92)
316. **Satellites and escape velocity** (`physics/satellites-and-escape-velocity`) — gravity itself supplies the centripetal force, fixing a circular orbit's speed at v = √(GM/r), about 8 km/s just above the Earth; escape takes √2 times that, about 11.2 km/s from the surface, and at 35,800 km the 24-hour period gives the geostationary perch (Sudan, Grade 12 — *Physics — Third Secondary Year*, Book 1, printed p. 50)
317. **Modulation — AM and FM** (`physics/modulation-am-and-fm`) — sound is written onto a high-frequency carrier: AM varies the carrier's amplitude and keeps its frequency fixed (simple, far-reaching, crackly), FM varies its frequency and holds amplitude constant (clean sound, wider channel, VHF band) (Sudan, Grade 12 — *Physics — Third Secondary Year*, Book 4 (4-17), printed p. 202)
318. **The microphone and radio receiver circuits** (`physics/the-microphone-and-radio-receiver-circuits`) — the microphone's diaphragm-driven coil turns sound into a copying current by induction; the receiver's LC resonance circuit selects one carrier, its diode detector strips the carrier away, and amplification drives the loudspeaker (Sudan, Grade 12 — *Physics — Third Secondary Year*, Book 4, printed p. 203)
319. **Television transmission and reception** (`physics/television-transmission-and-reception`) — the camera scans a scene into 625 lines, brightness becoming a vision signal (with synchronising pulses) that modulates a carrier beside a separate sound carrier; the receiver tunes, separates and detects, and the screen repaints the same lines in step (Sudan, Grade 12 — *Physics — Third Secondary Year*, Book 4 close, printed p. 210)
320. **Wave optics — interference and diffraction of light** (`physics/wave-optics-interference-and-diffraction-of-light`) — light caught behaving as a wave: coherent sources superpose, the path difference deciding bright from dark (whole wavelengths reinforce, half-odd cancel), Young's double-slit fringes of width w = λD/a measuring the wavelength itself, single-slit diffraction spreading the beam (minima at a sin θ = nλ), and the diffraction grating sharpening the maxima to d sin θ = nλ so each wavelength takes its own angle (Fiji, Year 13 — *Physics for Year 13*, Strand 3 §3.3 Wave Optics, printed p. 80)
321. **How this Physics course works — the Indonesian Kurikulum Merdeka way** (`physics/how-this-physics-course-works-the-indonesian-kurikulum-merdeka-way`) — Indonesia's way: physics organised as activity-first chapters (Fase F) — every chapter opens with apperception and a misconception check, builds concepts through numbered Aktivitas investigations, closes each stretch with “Ayo, Cek Pemahaman” checks and the chapter with an Asesmen, and carries a Profil Pelajar Pancasila character dimension and project options (Indonesia, Grade 11 — *Fisika untuk SMA/MA Kelas XI*, whole book)
322. **Teaching physics the Kurikulum Merdeka way — schemes, misconception checks and LKPD** (`physics/teaching-physics-the-kurikulum-merdeka-way-schemes-misconception-checks-and-lkpd`) — the guide's per-chapter apparatus: Skema Pembelajaran (meetings, hours, strategies), miskonsepsi lists taught against, LKPD worksheets, parent interaction, alternative routes and teacher reflection — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Indonesia, Grade 11 — *Buku Panduan Guru Fisika untuk SMA/MA Kelas XI*, whole guide)
323. **Misconception-first teaching — the Indonesian guide way** (`physics/misconception-first-teaching-the-indonesian-guide-way`) — every chapter carries a named Miskonsepsi section listing this topic's wrong ideas before the teaching plan; teaching confronts them (predict, watch it fail, Pembelajaran Alternatif as a second route), and checks, reflection and follow-up track whether the wrong idea moved — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Indonesia, Grade 12 — *Buku Panduan Guru Fisika untuk SMA/MA Kelas XII*, whole guide)
324. **Sinusoidal alternating current — period, frequency and rms values with the oscilloscope** (`physics/sinusoidal-alternating-current-period-frequency-and-rms-with-the-oscilloscope`) — AC reverses in a sine wave: period T and frequency f = 1/T (mains 50 Hz, T = 0.02 s), period and peak read off an oscilloscope trace, and the working value is the rms value, Urms = Umax/√2 — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Morocco, Grade 8 — *Al Moufid en Physique/Chimie, 2AC — Guide de l'enseignant(e)*, Chapitre 16, printed p. 136; *Al Marjie Physique Chimie, 2AC — Guide de l'enseignant(e)*, Partie 2, printed pp. 115–125)
325. **Speed and road safety — braking, reaction and the dangers of speed** (`physics/speed-and-road-safety-braking-reaction-and-the-dangers-of-speed`) — the reaction distance travelled while a driver reacts grows in proportion to speed, the braking distance grows with the square of the speed, and their sum, the stopping distance, sets the safe gap — road safety as a timetabled activity inside the speed chapter — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Morocco, Grade 9 — *Al Moufid en Physique/Chimie, 3AC — Guide de l'enseignant(e)*, Chapitre 9, Activity 3, printed p. 85)
326. **Electrical energy consumed — the meter, E = P·t and the electricity bill** (`physics/electrical-energy-consumed-the-meter-and-the-electricity-bill`) — E = P·t counted in kilowatt-hours by the home's meter, the bill pricing the units (cost = kWh × price per unit), worked from a real electricity bill as the closing activity of collège physics — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Morocco, Grade 9 — *Al Moufid en Physique/Chimie, 3AC — Guide de l'enseignant(e)*, Chapitre 16, printed p. 117; *Al Marjie Physique Chimie, 3AC — Guide de l'enseignant(e)*, Partie 2, printed pp. 93–107)
