441. **Chemical analysis — qualitative and quantitative** (`chemistry/chemical-analysis-qualitative-and-quantitative`) — What analysis is for (medicine, agriculture, industry, environment, with Egyptian-style examples from blood sugar to soil and air pollutants) and its two kinds: qualitative (identify the constituents) always before quantitative (measure how much), qualitative analysis of inorganic salts meaning the acidic radical (anion) and basic radical (cation). (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Chemistry — For the General Secondary Certificate (3rd Secondary / Grade 12), Term 1 (2025–2026), English edition)*, Chapter Two, printed pp. 31–32)
442. **Gravimetric analysis — by volatilisation and precipitation** (`chemistry/gravimetric-analysis-by-volatilisation-and-precipitation`) — Gravimetric analysis in its two forms: volatilisation (heating hydrated BaCl₂ to constant mass to find its water of crystallisation, BaCl₂·2H₂O) and precipitation (weighing BaSO₄ on ashless filter paper, ignited in a crucible, to find the barium chloride in a solution). (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Chemistry — For the General Secondary Certificate (3rd Secondary / Grade 12), Term 1 (2025–2026), English edition)*, Chapter Two, printed pp. 46–48)
443. **Catalysts and light — in rate and equilibrium** (`chemistry/catalysts-and-light-in-rate-and-equilibrium`) — Catalysts speed both directions equally so the equilibrium position does not move; enzymes named as biological catalysts and catalytic converters as the car application; light as a rate factor through photosynthesis and the silver bromide photographic film (Ag⁺ + e⁻ → Ag). (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Chemistry — For the General Secondary Certificate (3rd Secondary / Grade 12), Term 1 (2025–2026), English edition)*, Chapter Three, printed pp. 64–65)
444. **Ostwald's dilution law and weak-electrolyte calculations** (`chemistry/ostwalds-dilution-law-and-weak-electrolyte-calculations`) — Ostwald's dilution law derived (Ka = α²C, α = √(Ka/C)), degree of ionisation computed for hydrocyanic acid, then [H₃O⁺] = √(Ca·Ka) for weak acids and [OH⁻] = √(Cb·Kb) for weak bases such as ammonia, each with a worked number. (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Chemistry — For the General Secondary Certificate (3rd Secondary / Grade 12), Term 1 (2025–2026), English edition)*, Chapter Three, printed pp. 69–72)
445. **Electroplating and electrolytic metal purification** (`chemistry/electroplating-and-electrolytic-metal-purification`) — Electrolysis at work, first electroplating (a silver-plated jug: object as cathode, silver anode, AgNO₃ electrolyte; chromium on car parts and taps), then the purification of copper — impure anode dissolves, pure copper plates the cathode at 99.95%, iron/zinc impurities stay in solution, silver and gold fall as anode mud and are recovered. (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Chemistry — For the General Secondary Certificate (3rd Secondary / Grade 12), Term 1 (2025–2026), English edition)*, Chapter Four, printed pp. 107–110)
446. **Organic vs inorganic compounds — compared** (`chemistry/organic-vs-inorganic-compounds-compared`) — The eleven-row comparison table Egypt drills (structure, solubility, melting/boiling points, odour, flammability, bond type, conductivity, reaction rate, polymerisation, isomerism), set up from a bench comparison of paraffin, naphthalene, ethanol, acetone and glycerol against water, salt and copper sulphate. (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Chemistry — For the General Secondary Certificate (3rd Secondary / Grade 12), Term 1 (2025–2026), English edition)*, Chapter Five, printed pp. 119–120 (chapter pagination))
447. **Molecular and structural formulas** (`chemistry/molecular-and-structural-formulas`) — Molecular formula vs structural formula, valency as the count of covalent bonds (C tetravalent, O divalent, N trivalent, H monovalent), with structural formulas drawn for C₂H₄Cl₂, C₂H₄, C₂H₂ and CH₃OH. (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Chemistry — For the General Secondary Certificate (3rd Secondary / Grade 12), Term 1 (2025–2026), English edition)*, Chapter Five, printed p. 120 (chapter pagination))
448. **Detecting carbon and hydrogen in an organic compound** (`chemistry/detecting-carbon-and-hydrogen-in-an-organic-compound`) — The standard practical: heat the substance with copper(II) oxide; water turns white anhydrous copper sulphate blue, and CO₂ turns lime water milky — the two observations that prove C and H. (Egypt, Grade 12 — *Chemistry — For the General Secondary Certificate (3rd Secondary / Grade 12), Term 1 (2025–2026), English edition)*, Chapter Five, printed pp. 122–123 (chapter pagination))
449. **Food additives — what each kind does and its limit** (`chemistry/food-additives-what-each-kind-does-and-its-limit`) — Substances added to food on purpose, each named on the label by its E-number, sorted by the job each kind does (colourings first) and sorted again by the harm the book presents — sulfur dioxide and sulfites and phosphates classed toxic, citric acid E330 and sodium benzoate E211 among those classed carcinogenic — closing with the book's advice to avoid long-term consumption of synthetically additivated products. (Moldova, Grade 12 — *Chimie: Manual pentru clasa a XII-a*, Unit 9 §9.1 Aditivii alimentari, printed p. 190)
450. **Radicals and valency** (`chemistry/radicals-and-valency`) — Radicals as charged groups of atoms (ammonium, hydroxide, nitrate, carbonate, sulfate, phosphate), then the valency of elements and radicals as combining power — the balance rule, brackets and all — taught as one step immediately before formulae and nomenclature. (South Sudan, Secondary 1 — *South Sudan Secondary 1 Chemistry — Student's Book 1*, Unit 2 §§2.13–2.14, printed pp. 73–74)
451. **Action of heat on salts** (`chemistry/action-of-heat-on-salts`) — How salts behave on heating, taught as decomposition patterns: most carbonates to the oxide and CO₂, nitrates splitting three ways by the metal's reactivity, the whole read from the gases (limewater, glowing splint, brown fumes) and the residue's colours hot and cold. (South Sudan, Secondary 2 — *South Sudan Secondary 2 Chemistry — Student's Book*, Unit 3 §3.3, printed p. 88)
452. **Carbonic acid and carbonates** (`chemistry/carbonic-acid-and-carbonates`) — Carbon dioxide in water forming weak, dibasic carbonic acid; its two salt families, hydrogencarbonates and carbonates; and the carbonates' reactions — the acid fizz test, heat, and the cave-making dissolution as hydrogencarbonate that heating reverses. (South Sudan, Secondary 2 — *South Sudan Secondary 2 Chemistry — Student's Book*, Unit 4 §4.5, printed p. 153)
453. **Back titration** (`chemistry/back-titration`) — For substances that cannot be titrated directly — insoluble, slow, or without a sharp end point: add a known, measured excess of a standard reagent, titrate the leftover, and added − leftover is the measurement. (South Sudan, Secondary 3 — *South Sudan Secondary 3 Chemistry — Student's Book 3*, Unit 1 §1.7, printed p. 22)
454. **Water of crystallisation** (`chemistry/water-of-crystallisation`) — Water built into a salt's crystal in fixed proportion (CuSO₄·5H₂O), driven off by heating to constant mass — the mass lost is the water, the mole ratio fixes the dot-number — and percentage water of crystallisation, the percentage investigation framed by the Secondary 3 teacher guide. (South Sudan, Secondary 3 — *South Sudan Secondary 3 Chemistry — Student's Book 3*, Unit 1 §1.8, printed p. 30)
455. **Zinc — extraction and uses** (`chemistry/zinc-extraction-and-uses`) — Zinc blende (ZnS) roasted to the oxide, the oxide reduced with carbon, the metal distilling off as vapour to condense; then its work: galvanising steel sacrificially, brass, and dry-cell cases. (South Sudan, Secondary 4 — *South Sudan Secondary 4 Chemistry — Student's Book*, Unit 4 §4.5, printed p. 92)
456. **Chromium — extraction and uses** (`chemistry/chromium-extraction-and-uses`) — Chromite (FeCr₂O₄) by way of the chromates to chromium(III) oxide, reduced by aluminium; most chromium travelling as ferrochrome into stainless steel, its self-sealing oxide layer the root of chrome plating and rust-proof shine. (South Sudan, Secondary 4 — *South Sudan Secondary 4 Chemistry — Student's Book*, Unit 4 §4.6, printed p. 96)
457. **Chromatography — paper chromatography and Rf values** (`chemistry/chromatography-paper-and-rf-values`) — Separation by travel between a stationary paper and a climbing solvent, building on the paper chromatography of Secondary 1: the chromatogram, and Rf = spot distance ÷ front distance as the identifying constant, spent on identification and purity from a single drop. (South Sudan, Secondary 4 — *South Sudan Secondary 4 Chemistry — Student's Book*, Unit 5 §5.1, printed p. 103)
458. **Analytical spectroscopy — IR, NMR, UV and mass** (`chemistry/analytical-spectroscopy-ir-nmr-uv-mass`) — The four spectroscopies of the analytical techniques unit: infrared (each bond vibration absorbing at its characteristic wavelength), NMR (the structure-determining count of hydrogen environments), ultraviolet (the conjugated clue), and mass spectroscopy — the book's own spelling — weighing the molecule and reading its fragments. (South Sudan, Secondary 4 — *South Sudan Secondary 4 Chemistry — Student's Book*, Unit 5 §§5.2–5.5, printed pp. 109–116)
459. **Testing purity by melting and boiling point** (`chemistry/testing-purity-by-melting-and-boiling-point`) — A pure solid melts at one sharp, fixed temperature and a pure liquid boils at one fixed temperature; impurities lower and widen the melting point and raise the boiling point — the moved, smeared point is the verdict, backed by the single-spot chromatogram, and run in the practical workbook on naphthalene and on water or ethanol. (Libya, Grade 10 — *الكيمياء — كتاب الطالب (Chemistry — Student Book)*, Unit 1 §1-3 Testing the Purity of Substances, printed p. 21)
460. **Giant covalent structures** (`chemistry/giant-covalent-structures`) — The third structural class: every atom covalently bonded into one continuous network — diamond, silica (sand) and graphite-style networks — hence very hard with very high melting points, since melting must break the bonds themselves. (Libya, Grade 10 — *الكيمياء — كتاب الطالب (Chemistry — Student Book)*, Unit 3 §3-5, printed p. 46)
461. **Variables in a fair test** (`chemistry/variables-in-a-fair-test`) — The independent variable you change, the dependent variable you measure, and the controlled variables you hold fixed — taught through the practical course's opening investigation, timing how fast an unknown solid dissolves, and answered in the teacher guide in the full variables framework (particle size, solubility, and the controlled set). (Libya, Grade 10 — *الكيمياء — كراسة النشاط العملي (Chemistry — Practical Activity Workbook)*, Experiment 1, printed p. 7)
462. **Flame tests — colours in the flame** (`chemistry/flame-tests-colours-in-the-flame`) — A clean wire loop, a hot flame, and the colour the metal gives it: observation first, the metal's name as the inference — Experiment 10 inside the Grade 12 practical workbook's qualitative-analysis run of ion-identification experiments. (Libya, Grade 12 — *كراسة النشاط العملي (Practical Activity Workbook)*, Experiment 10, printed p. 36)

463. **Hazards of polymers and plastics** (`chemistry/hazards-of-polymers-and-plastics`) — the polymer chain itself is large and unreactive; the hazards ride with what the plastic carries and sheds: unreacted residual monomer trapped in the material, small molecules released on heating and burning, and plasticizers that were mixed in but never bonded in and slowly leach out — closing with the harm discarded plastic does to animals and the prevention measures paired to each hazard (Bhutan, Class IX — *A Textbook of Chemistry, Class IX (Bhutan Edition)*, Ch.6 §6.8 Hazards of polymers, printed pp. 109–111)
464. **Transition elements — characteristic properties** (`chemistry/transition-elements-characteristic-properties`) — the family's six signatures as one lesson: variable oxidation states, coloured ions and compounds, catalytic activity, complex formation, paramagnetism, and hard alloy-forming metals — every one traced to the partly filled (n−1)d subshell — closing with group 11, copper, silver and gold, their similarities, falling reactivity and uses (Bhutan, Class X — *Chemistry, Class X*, Ch.5 §5.3–5.4, printed pp. 138–148)
465. **Denatured alcohol and illicit liquor** (`chemistry/denatured-alcohol-and-illicit-liquor`) — ethanol made undrinkable on purpose (methylated spirit: methanol, foul taste, violet dye) so industrial spirit can be sold duty-free, against spurious liquor faked with methanol that blinds and kills and how suspect liquor is identified — closing with the chapter's reckoning of ethanol's impacts on environment, economy, society and health and its case study "The Myth behind Alcohol Happiness" by Dr. Chencho Dorji (Bhutan, Class X — *Chemistry, Class X*, Ch.9 §9.4/§9.6, printed pp. 223–233, case study pp. 234–236)
466. **Phase rule and phase diagrams** (`chemistry/phase-rule-and-phase-diagrams`) — the formalism under every phase diagram: phase, component and degrees of freedom counted by their own rules, Gibbs' phase rule F = C − P + 2 with the criteria for phase equilibrium, read on water three ways — area F = 2, line F = 1, triple point F = 0 (Bhutan, Class XI — *A Textbook of Chemistry, Class XI (Bhutan Edition)*, Ch.9 §9.2–9.4, printed pp. 286–288)
467. **Haloalkanes — nucleophilic substitution** (`chemistry/haloalkanes-nucleophilic-substitution`) — the family built on the one polar bond C–X: nomenclature, preparation and properties, then the swap itself in its two mechanisms — S_N2's backside attack with inversion, S_N1's flat carbocation — closing with chirality, enantiomers and optical activity, the product's handedness as the mechanism's fingerprint (Bhutan, Class XI — *A Textbook of Chemistry, Class XI (Bhutan Edition)*, Ch.12 Haloalkanes, printed pp. 451–479)
468. **Biological role of sodium, potassium, magnesium and calcium** (`chemistry/biological-role-of-sodium-potassium-magnesium-and-calcium`) — four metal ions on staff in living bodies: Mg²⁺ at the centre of chlorophyll and, with Ca²⁺, catalysing enzymatic reactions; Ca²⁺ triggering muscle contraction and stored in bones as phosphate, its deficiency in children causing malnutrition; Na⁺ outside cells and K⁺ inside them holding the fluids and carrying the nerve impulse (Bhutan, Class XI — *A Textbook of Chemistry, Class XI (Bhutan Edition)*, Ch.5 §5.6, printed p. 178)

469. **The Arab and Muslim founders of chemistry** (`chemistry/the-arab-and-muslim-founders-of-chemistry`) — Yemen's Grade 10 history honour roll: Jabir ibn Hayyan, called the true founder of chemistry for his experimental method and his classification of substances, his first statement of fixed proportions, and the scholars who followed him — al-Razi, Ibn Sina, al-Jildaki, al-Hamdani and al-Zahrawi — with the word chemistry itself traced to an Arabic verb meaning to hide/conceal (Yemen, Grade 10 — *الكيمياء للصف الأول الثانوي (Chemistry for the First Secondary Year)*, Unit 1, printed pp. 11–16)
470. **Phlogiston — the theory that burning died of** (`chemistry/phlogiston-the-theory-that-burning-died-of`) — Stahl's phlogiston theory (1701 onwards) as Yemen's Grade 10 book teaches it: burning releases phlogiston, a metal's calx is the metal minus phlogiston, charcoal restores the metal by giving phlogiston back — and the theory fails on Lavoisier's weighing, since sulfur and phosphorus gain weight when they burn, so phlogiston would need negative weight (Yemen, Grade 10 — *الكيمياء للصف الأول الثانوي (Chemistry for the First Secondary Year)*, Unit 1, printed pp. 16–17)
471. **Basic, applied and technological research — the penicillin chain** (`chemistry/basic-applied-and-technological-research-the-penicillin-chain`) — Yemen's Grade 10 link between chemistry, technology and society: three kinds of research — basic, applied and technological — traced through one worked chain (basic research discovers penicillin and its structure, applied research turns it into an antibiotic industry, technology mass-produces it), with the positive applications, the negative side and society's feedback on science (Yemen, Grade 10 — *الكيمياء للصف الأول الثانوي (Chemistry for the First Secondary Year)*, Unit 1, printed pp. 21–24)
472. **The Downs cell — making sodium from its salt** (`chemistry/the-downs-cell-making-sodium-from-its-salt`) — Sodium prepared by electrolysing molten sodium chloride in the Downs cell, as Yemen's Grade 10 book draws it: graphite anode and iron cathode ring separated by an iron gauze diaphragm, chloride oxidised to chlorine at the anode, sodium ions reduced at the cathode, and the liquid metal floating up into its own collecting chamber so the products never meet (Yemen, Grade 10 — *الكيمياء للصف الأول الثانوي (Chemistry for the First Secondary Year)*, Unit 5, printed pp. 109–110)
473. **Common salt — extraction and uses** (`chemistry/common-salt-extraction-and-uses`) — Sodium chloride placed in Yemen first, as the Grade 10 book places it: high-purity rock salt from al-Salif in al-Hudaydah and the deposits of Shabwa and Marib needing only crushing and packing, solar evaporation of sea water at places such as Aden yielding salt carrying CaCl2 and MgCl2 impurities, and the salt itself — an ionic crystal melting at 804 °C — at work in food, preservation, freezing mixtures and as feedstock for NaOH, soda and hydrochloric acid (Yemen, Grade 10 — *الكيمياء للصف الأول الثانوي (Chemistry for the First Secondary Year)*, Unit 5, printed p. 112)
474. **Sodium bicarbonate — baking soda** (`chemistry/sodium-bicarbonate-baking-soda`) — Sodium hydrogencarbonate as Yemen's Grade 10 book teaches it: prepared in the laboratory by passing CO2 into cold caustic soda solution and met industrially as the Solvay intermediate, breaking down above 100 °C to sodium carbonate, CO2 and water, fizzing CO2 with acids — and at work in baking powder, leather tanning, fire extinguishers and medicine against stomach acidity (Yemen, Grade 10 — *الكيمياء للصف الأول الثانوي (Chemistry for the First Secondary Year)*, Unit 5, printed p. 116)
475. **Gypsum and plaster — setting with water** (`chemistry/gypsum-and-plaster-setting-with-water`) — Calcium sulfate as gypsum, CaSO4·2H2O, met through Yemen's Grade 10 book's own bench moment: powder the plaster of Yemen's traditional qamariya window-screen craftsmen, add water, and it sets quickly into a hard mass giving out heat — the paste that fills ceiling voids and casts statues, setting before it is even placed if you dawdle (Yemen, Grade 10 — *الكيمياء للصف الأول الثانوي (Chemistry for the First Secondary Year)*, Unit 6, printed p. 128)
476. **Group 13 — the boron family survey** (`chemistry/group-13-the-boron-family-survey`) — The third main group (IIIA) read off its data table, as Yemen's Grade 11 book reads it: B, Al, Ga, In, Tl with configurations ns2np1 — all metals except boron the metalloid, hydroxides that will not dissolve in water unlike groups IA/IIA, boron's acidic oxide against alumina the amphoteric, and basic character growing steadily down the group, opened by gallium photographed melting in a hand (Yemen, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثاني الثانوي (Chemistry for the Second Secondary Year)*, Unit 1: Elements of the third main group (IIIA), printed pp. 9–10)
477. **The thermite reaction — welding with aluminium** (`chemistry/the-thermite-reaction-welding-with-aluminium`) — Aluminium's hunger for oxygen put to work, as Yemen's Grade 11 book teaches it: a reducing agent for winning metals (3MnO2 + 4Al -> 3Mn + 2Al2O3) and the heart of thermite — aluminium powder with iron(III) oxide in a crucible, lit by a magnesium ribbon, welding rail joints at about 3500 °C (Fe2O3 + 2Al -> 2Fe + Al2O3) (Yemen, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثاني الثانوي (Chemistry for the Second Secondary Year)*, Unit 1, printed p. 13)
478. **Aluminium — a metal that fights acids and alkalis** (`chemistry/aluminium-a-metal-that-fights-acids-and-alkalis`) — Aluminium's reactions run systematically, as Yemen's Grade 11 book runs them: with oxygen to Al2O3; fast with dilute HCl but slowly with concentrated acid; SO2 from hot concentrated H2SO4; nothing with nitric acid, because the protective oxide coat forms — which is why aluminium tankers carry nitric acid; soluble aluminates plus hydrogen with NaOH or KOH; and compounds with sulfur, nitrogen, carbon and the halogens — the amphoteric metal behaviour done by hand in the practical workbook's first two activities, aluminium powder on the Bunsen flame and heated with sodium hydroxide solution (Yemen, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثاني الثانوي (Chemistry for the Second Secondary Year)*, Unit 1, printed pp. 13–15; Yemen, Grade 11 — *الأنشطة والتجارب العملية — الكيمياء للصف الثاني الثانوي (Activities and Practical Experiments: Chemistry for the Second Secondary Year)*, Activities 1–2, printed pp. 6–8)
479. **Aluminium sulfate — from paper to fire foam** (`chemistry/aluminium-sulfate-from-paper-to-fire-foam`) — Aluminium sulfate as Yemen's Grade 11 book teaches it: made from Al2O3 + 3H2SO4, crystallising with 18 waters, losing them on heating and at red heat breaking down to alumina, SO2 and O2, its solution acidic by hydrolysis — and at work in foam fire extinguishers, where with sodium bicarbonate it makes a stable CO2 foam for oil fires, and in paper making, leather tanning, dye fixing and water purification (Yemen, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثاني الثانوي (Chemistry for the Second Secondary Year)*, Unit 1, printed pp. 16–17)
480. **Carbon monoxide — the silent killer and furnace gas** (`chemistry/carbon-monoxide-the-silent-killer-and-furnace-gas`) — Carbon monoxide as Yemen's Grade 11 book teaches it: formed when carbon burns in limited oxygen (2C + O2 -> 2CO), colourless and odourless, riding the haemoglobin in place of oxygen and so killing — the text names the yearly winter deaths when families burn charcoal for warmth in closed rooms in cold regions — and industrially the reducing gas of the blast furnace (Fe2O3 + 3CO -> 2Fe + 3CO2), closing with radioactive 14C in natural carbon, used to date fossils and old trees (Yemen, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثاني الثانوي (Chemistry for the Second Secondary Year)*, Unit 2, printed pp. 27, 29)
481. **Group 15 — the nitrogen family survey** (`chemistry/group-15-the-nitrogen-family-survey`) — The fifth main group (VA) surveyed as Yemen's Grade 11 book surveys it: N, P, As, Sb, Bi with configurations ns2np3, existing as molecules N2, P4, As4 and Sb4 with bismuth a crystalline solid metal, several members showing two allotropic forms while nitrogen has none, their hydrides running NH3 and PH3 (bismuth's falling apart to the element), and their oxides in +3 and +5 forms whose acidity fades from nitrogen down to bismuth (Yemen, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثاني الثانوي (Chemistry for the Second Secondary Year)*, Unit 3: Elements of the fifth main group (VA), printed pp. 35–37)
482. **Nitrates — gunpowder, saltpetre and exploding fertiliser** (`chemistry/nitrates-gunpowder-saltpetre-and-exploding-fertiliser`) — The nitrates as Yemen's Grade 11 book teaches them: sodium nitrate (Chile saltpetre), potassium nitrate — named in Arabic gunpowder salt because it fed the old gunpowder — and silver nitrate; all nitrates dissolving in water, and heat sorting them three ways (alkali-metal nitrates to nitrite and oxygen, silver nitrate all the way to the metal, ammonium nitrate to nitrous oxide and water, with the boxed warning that strongly heated NH4NO3 can detonate) — with the practical workbook's two nitrate tests, hot concentrated sulfuric acid and the brown ring (Yemen, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثاني الثانوي (Chemistry for the Second Secondary Year)*, Unit 3, printed pp. 41–43; Yemen, Grade 11 — *الأنشطة والتجارب العملية — الكيمياء للصف الثاني الثانوي (Activities and Practical Experiments: Chemistry for the Second Secondary Year)*, Activities 8–9, printed pp. 23–25)
483. **Group 16 — the oxygen family survey** (`chemistry/group-16-the-oxygen-family-survey`) — The sixth main group (VIA) surveyed with its anomaly first, as Yemen's Grade 11 book surveys it: O, S, Se, Te, Po and element 116 with configurations ns2np4 — oxygen's high electronegativity letting it form ionic compounds with some metals while the rest cannot, hydrogen bonding in H2O but in none of the other hydrides — the family's reactions with metals, oxygen, halogens and hydrogen, and its uses from sulfur for sulfuric acid and vulcanisation to selenium in photocells and polonium, named by Marie Curie for Poland (Yemen, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثاني الثانوي (Chemistry for the Second Secondary Year)*, Unit 4: Elements of the sixth main group (VIA), printed pp. 47–49)
484. **Activation energy — the barrier every reaction must climb** (`chemistry/activation-energy-the-barrier-every-reaction-must-climb`) — Activation energy read off a distribution curve, as Yemen's Grade 11 book reads it: at temperature T1 only the black-shaded molecules have enough energy, at T2 the red-shaded crowd joins them, so heating multiplies the activated fraction; the activated complex drawn for H2 + I2 as [H...H...I...I], a fleeting arrangement where the old bonds are half-broken and the new half-formed, and the whole journey plotted as an energy hill — with the homely picture of a rock pushed from a mountainside over the pass before it can roll down to the valley (Yemen, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثاني الثانوي (Chemistry for the Second Secondary Year)*, Unit 7, printed pp. 89–91)
485. **Salt hydrolysis — why salt solutions are not always neutral** (`chemistry/salt-hydrolysis-why-salt-solutions-are-not-always-neutral`) — Salt hydrolysis built on the general scheme, as Yemen's Grade 11 book builds it (and its practical workbook checks on the pH meter): the salt dissociates, water ionises slightly, the ion from the weak partner reacts with water — and the pH verdict follows the parentage: both strong (NaCl) neutral at pH 7; weak acid + strong base (CH3COONa) alkaline, the acetate ion hydrolysing, with pH = 7 + 1/2(pKa + log Cs); strong acid + weak base (NH4Cl) acidic, with its own working formula — the five salt solutions of workbook Activity 14 measured with a pH meter and universal indicator paper (Yemen, Grade 11 — *الكيمياء للصف الثاني الثانوي (Chemistry for the Second Secondary Year)*, Unit 8, printed pp. 126–128; Yemen, Grade 11 — *الأنشطة والتجارب العملية — الكيمياء للصف الثاني الثانوي (Activities and Practical Experiments: Chemistry for the Second Secondary Year)*, Activity 14, printed p. 36)
486. **Systems, surroundings and specific heat** (`chemistry/systems-surroundings-and-specific-heat`) — Thermochemistry's working vocabulary fixed with a flask diagram, as Yemen's Grade 12 book fixes it: energy in its many forms with the conservation law, chemical energy as the potential energy stored in a substance's structure, system, surroundings and the boundary, adiabatic against isothermal, heat against temperature — then heat capacity and specific heat bilingually defined, the formula worked on a copper block (95.49 g absorbing 849 J from 25 °C to 48 °C gives 0.387 J/g·°C), and a specific-heat table whose lesson is water's 4.18 J/g·°C against iron's 0.449 (Yemen, Grade 12 — *الكيمياء للصف الثالث الثانوي (Chemistry for the Third Secondary Year)*, Unit 2: Thermal energy accompanying changes of matter (Thermochemistry), printed pp. 22–27)
487. **The named heats — solution, neutralisation, combustion, formation** (`chemistry/the-named-heats-solution-neutralisation-combustion-formation`) — A family of standard heats defined and exemplified, as Yemen's Grade 12 book defines them: heat of solution (NH4NO3 dissolving at +25.7 kJ/mol, the cold-pack effect, against NaOH dissolving strongly exothermic), heat of vaporisation read off water's heating curve (+44 kJ/mol), heat of neutralisation constant at -57.7 kJ/mol for any strong acid with any strong base because the only real reaction is H+ + OH- -> H2O, standard heat of combustion measured in the bomb calorimeter, and standard heat of formation with its stability rule — the more negative, the more stable the compound (Yemen, Grade 12 — *الكيمياء للصف الثالث الثانوي (Chemistry for the Third Secondary Year)*, Unit 2, printed pp. 31–35)
488. **Transition elements — the B-groups survey** (`chemistry/transition-elements-the-b-groups-survey`) — The B groups toured one by one with their personalities, as Yemen's Grade 12 student book tours them (with the teacher guide's sharpening classification as evidence): IB the coinage metals with variable valency unlike IA's fixed +1; IIB zinc, cadmium, mercury at only +2; IIIB the most electropositive of the transition metals; IVB hard, high-melting, expensive; VB and VIB, chromium sealing itself under an oxide skin; VIIB manganese from +2 to +7; VIIIB the three horizontal families — with the chromium and copper 4s1 configuration exceptions and the inner-transition lanthanides and actinides tabled separately; the guide adds the definition (a partly filled d or f sub-shell, in the free state or in a compound), the A/B chemical-similarity bridge, and the characteristic traits — several oxidation states, coloured compounds, paramagnetism, complex-ion formation [teacher evidence] (Yemen, Grade 12 — *الكيمياء للصف الثالث الثانوي (Chemistry for the Third Secondary Year)*, Unit 1: Transition elements, printed pp. 11–15; Yemen, Grade 12 — *دليل المعلم لتدريس كتاب الكيمياء للصف الثالث الثانوي (Teacher's Guide for Teaching the Chemistry Book, Third Secondary Year)*, Unit 1 background [teacher evidence], printed pp. 11–12)
489. **Isotopes and the mass spectrometer** (`chemistry/isotopes-and-the-mass-spectrometer`) — Nuclear chemistry opened through isotopes and the machine that separated them, as Yemen's Grade 12 book opens it: chemical and nuclear reactions contrasted in an eight-row table, isotopes earned from the fractional atomic masses (oxygen 15.9994, boron 10.811) through Aston's 1919 mass spectrometer and Bainbridge's 1933 refinement, the weighted-mean formula for relative atomic mass worked for neon (20.197), and isotopes sorted into stable and radioactive kinds, with artificial radioisotopes made by bombardment (Yemen, Grade 12 — *الكيمياء للصف الثالث الثانوي (Chemistry for the Third Secondary Year)*, Unit 4: Nuclear energy and reactions, printed pp. 69–72)
490. **Nuclear binding energy and the stability band** (`chemistry/nuclear-binding-energy-and-the-stability-band`) — Binding energy and nuclear stability as Yemen's Grade 12 book builds them: the three conservation rules for nuclear equations, helium-4's mass defect (4.00260 u against 4.032979 u of separate particles — 0.030379 u, the binding energy 7.75 MeV per nucleon), the binding-energy-per-nucleon curve peaking near iron so uranium-235 gains stability by fission while light nuclei gain it by fusion, and the neutron-to-proton stability band — n = p up to atomic number 20, n > p beyond, everything past 83 unstable — with the magic numbers 2, 8, 20, 50, 82, 126 and even-even nuclei picked out as specially stable (Yemen, Grade 12 — *الكيمياء للصف الثالث الثانوي (Chemistry for the Third Secondary Year)*, Unit 4, printed pp. 73–78)
491. **Paints — water-based and oil-based, and how they dry** (`chemistry/paints-water-based-and-oil-based-and-how-they-dry`) — Paints divided by their solvent, as Yemen's Grade 12 book divides them: water-based emulsion paints of styrene-butadiene, polyvinyl acetate and acrylic polymers, coloured with pigments such as titanium dioxide for white and the lead compounds banned from 1977 after child poisonings, with barium sulfate and carbonate as extenders — against oil-based paints built on polymerised oils with turpentine as the classic solvent; and the two drying mechanisms contrasted step by step: the water paint simply loses its water by evaporation, while the oil paint first loses its solvent, then the oil itself dries chemically as oxygen stitches the unsaturated chains into a hard cross-linked film (Yemen, Grade 12 — *الكيمياء للصف الثالث الثانوي (Chemistry for the Third Secondary Year)*, Unit 8, printed pp. 162–164)
492. **Food tests — sugars, proteins and fats in the lab** (`chemistry/food-tests-sugars-proteins-and-fats-in-the-lab`) — Five bench tests written only in Yemen's Grade 12 teacher guide [teacher evidence]: Benedict's or Fehling's solution distinguishing glucose from fructose by speed (the aldehyde group against the ketone group) and maltose from sucrose by the free aldehyde group; the urine sugar test read off a printed per cent scale from healthy through traces and moderate diabetes to severe; alkaline hydrolysis of oils and fats ending with the soap floating on brine; and the three food classes detected themselves — carbohydrate by phenolphthalein pink and sulfuric-acid charring, protein by the xanthoproteic orange, fat through cholesterol's red product with concentrated sulfuric acid — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Yemen, Grade 12 — *دليل المعلم لتدريس كتاب الكيمياء للصف الثالث الثانوي (Teacher's Guide for Teaching the Chemistry Book, Third Secondary Year)*, Unit 6 activities [teacher evidence], printed pp. 61–64)
493. **Polymerisation in the classroom — glue, borax and interfacial films** (`chemistry/polymerisation-in-the-classroom-glue-borax-and-interfacial-films`) — A polymer prepared in class, written only in Yemen's Grade 12 teacher guide [teacher evidence]: 60 mL of paper glue mixed with 60 mL of water, poured into four paper cups, with borax solution (5 g in 100 mL water) added in increasing doses of 5, 10 and 20 mL — the stretch length growing as the borax share rises, because borax cross-links the glue polymer and more cross-linking means greater molecular weight and a stronger polymer — with the guide's classification of the ways polymerisation is run (bulk, solution, suspension and emulsion polymerisation) and interfacial polymerisation, the nylon rope trick's family, named as the same kind of process — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Yemen, Grade 12 — *دليل المعلم لتدريس كتاب الكيمياء للصف الثالث الثانوي (Teacher's Guide for Teaching the Chemistry Book, Third Secondary Year)*, Unit 8 activities [teacher evidence], printed p. 78)
494. **Measuring carbon dioxide in air and breath** (`chemistry/measuring-carbon-dioxide-in-air-and-breath`) — Carbon dioxide measured volumetrically with a drawn two-tube manometer apparatus, written only in Yemen's Grade 12 teacher guide [teacher evidence]: air drawn into a conical flask over a small beaker of 6M sodium hydroxide sealed under mineral oil, the flask shaken by hand — held by the neck so the hand's heat does not falsify the reading — until the liquid level in tube B stops rising, and the CO2 percentage computed from partial pressure against 760 mmHg, matching the known ~0.03% (2NaOH + CO2 -> Na2CO3 + H2O); Activity 14 repeats the measurement on exhaled breath, blown through tube A six to eight times with the cheek, and the class compares breath against air — teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0 (Yemen, Grade 12 — *دليل المعلم لتدريس كتاب الكيمياء للصف الثالث الثانوي (Teacher's Guide for Teaching the Chemistry Book, Third Secondary Year)*, Unit 9 activities [teacher evidence], printed pp. 85–86)
495. **Molar volume of a gas** (`chemistry/molar-volume-of-a-gas`) — at the same temperature and pressure one mole of any gas occupies the same volume — the molar volume Vm: 22.4 L/mol at 0 °C and 1 atm in the Lettres manuals, 24 L/mol in the worked butane-bottle example of the science-track Chimie 5AS — and the amount of a gas is read from its volume as n = V/Vm (Mauritania, Grades 11 — *Chimie 5ème AS*, Ch. 1, printed p. 12; *Sciences Physiques 5AS — Lettres Modernes*, Part IV Ch. 2, printed pp. 101–109; *Sciences Physiques 5AS — Lettres Originelles* (Arabic), Ch. 2, printed pp. 51–55)
496. **The advancement table** (`chemistry/the-advancement-table`) — the tableau d'avancement — the French-tradition course's signature calculation tool: the amounts of every species are tracked against the advancement x from the initial state to the final state, on Lavoisier's conservation of matter; at full depth the final advancement xf is set against the maximal xmax and the ratio τ = xf/xmax decides whether a reaction was total (Mauritania, Grades 11 and 13 — *Chimie 5ème AS*, Ch. 1, printed p. 17; *Chimie 7ème AS*, Ch. IV, printed p. 70)
497. **Atoms, elements and chemical symbols** (`chemistry/atoms-elements-and-chemical-symbols`) — a pure substance has one kind of constituent and cannot be split into simpler substances; the atom is the fundamental constituent — the smallest separable constituent of matter; atoms of one kind make an element, and each element carries a symbol written with one capital letter, or a capital followed by a small letter (Mauritania, Grades 8, 9 and 10 — *Sciences Physiques 2AS*, Unit I, printed p. 11; *Sciences Physiques 3AS*, Ch. I, printed p. 9; *Sciences Physiques 4AS*, Ch. II, printed p. 22)
498. **Pure substances — simple and compound** (`chemistry/pure-substances-simple-and-compound`) — pure substances sorted by their building: a corps pur simple is built from atoms of one kind (H₂, O₂), a corps pur composé from several kinds (H₂O, CO₂, C₄H₁₀, NaCl); the molecule is an association of atoms, and the chemical formula is the writing that shows the nature and number of its atoms (Mauritania, Grades 8 and 9 — *Sciences Physiques 2AS*, Unit I, printed p. 11; *Sciences Physiques 3AS*, Ch. I, printed p. 9)
499. **Solutions — solute and solvent** (`chemistry/solutions-solute-and-solvent`) — a solution defined as a homogeneous mixture made by dissolving one or more solutes in a solvent — aqueous when the solvent is water; liquid-in-liquid dissolving is bounded by miscibility, solid-in-liquid by the solute's solubility (Mauritania, Grades 9 and 10 — *Sciences Physiques 3AS*, Ch. II, printed p. 21; *Sciences Physiques 4AS*, Ch. III, printed p. 36)
500. **Separating mixtures — decantation, filtration, distillation** (`chemistry/separating-mixtures-decantation-filtration-distillation`) — taking a mixture apart by its parts' own behaviour: décantation of muddy water by settling, filtration (the residue stays on the filter, the filtrat passes), and distillation of a homogeneous liquid mixture, where the most volatile part vaporises first and condenses to a distillate — with evaporation applied to the salt marshes, 40 L of seawater yielding 1 kg of salt (Mauritania, Grade 8 — *Sciences Physiques 2AS*, Unit I, printed p. 8)
501. **Naming alkenes and alkynes** (`chemistry/naming-alkenes-and-alkynes`) — naming the unsaturated chains: the longest chain carrying the multiple bond, numbered from the end nearest it, with the bond's position index placed in the name — alkene names in -ène/-ene, alkyne names in -yne, from the science-track 6AS rule to the Lettres books' lowest-locant naming (Mauritania, Grade 12 — *Chimie 6ème AS*, Ch. 3, printed p. 36; *Sciences Physiques 6AS — Lettres Modernes*, Ch. 1, printed pp. 46–58; *Sciences Physiques 6AS — Lettres Originelles* (Arabic), Ch. 1, printed pp. 39–45)
502. **Amines — from ammonia** (`chemistry/amines-from-ammonia`) — amines built from ammonia by replacing one, two or three of its hydrogens with alkyl groups — primary, secondary and tertiary — and named from their substituents, trimethylamine the worked case (Mauritania, Grade 12 — *Chimie 6ème AS*, Ch. 5, printed p. 72)
503. **Amides and the peptide bond** (`chemistry/amides-and-the-peptide-bond`) — an amide is a carboxylic-acid derivative whose carbonyl carbon carries nitrogen; the amide link is the peptide bond that chains amino acids into proteins — with the amides' classes, their naming by swapping -oïque for -amide, and their preparation from amines (Mauritania, Grade 13 — *Chimie 7ème AS*, Ch. III, printed p. 55)
504. **Stereoisomerism — chirality and Z/E** (`chemistry/stereoisomerism-chirality-and-z-e`) — configuration isomerism: mirror-image molecules that cannot be superimposed — chirality — and the Z/E naming that replaces cis/trans for RCH=CHR′ alkenes (Mauritania, Grade 12 — *Chimie 6ème AS*, Ch. 5, printed p. 76)
505. **Acid–base neutralisation and equivalence** (`chemistry/acid-base-neutralisation-and-equivalence`) — dosing an acid against a base to the equivalence point — the point where the acid and base have been mixed in exactly the reaction's proportions, Ca·Va = Cb·Vb, and the mixture is neutral (Mauritania, Grade 11 — *Chimie 5ème AS*, Ch. 5, printed p. 90)
506. **Half-reaction time** (`chemistry/half-reaction-time`) — the half-reaction time t½, read graphically off the advancement and disappearance curves: at t½ the advancement has reached half its final value, x = xf/2 (Mauritania, Grade 13 — *Chimie 7ème AS*, Ch. V, printed p. 86)
507. **Catalysis and enzymes** (`chemistry/catalysis-and-enzymes`) — a catalyst speeds a reaction's formation and disappearance while appearing in no bilan (overall) equation and being used in tiny quantity; homogeneous catalysis is distinguished from heterogeneous, enzymes are presented as biological protein catalysts — and light is explicitly ruled out as a catalyst (Mauritania, Grade 13 — *Chimie 7ème AS*, Ch. VI, printed p. 95)
508. **Particle accelerators — the Large Hadron Collider** (`chemistry/particle-accelerators-the-large-hadron-collider`) — the Large Hadron Collider as the modern instrument for probing inside the atom — the heir of the unit's own probes, from discharge tubes and gold foil to collisions whose fragments are read as evidence of what was inside (Jordan, Grade 9 Term 1 — *الكيمياء — كتاب الطالب، الصف التاسع، الفصل الدراسي الأول (Chemistry, Student Book, Grade 9, Term 1)*, Unit 1 enrichment, printed p. 39)
509. **Degree of ionisation — and acid–base strength** (`chemistry/degree-of-ionisation-and-acid-base-strength`) — strength defined by the degree of ionisation in water — strong acids and bases ionise (almost) completely, weak ones only partly — with equal-concentration acids compared by their reaction rate with zinc (Jordan, Grade 9 Term 1 — *الكيمياء — كتاب الطالب، الصف التاسع، الفصل الدراسي الأول (Chemistry, Student Book, Grade 9, Term 1)*, Unit 2, Lesson 1, printed pp. 52–54)
510. **Ionic radius — cations and anions compared** (`chemistry/ionic-radius-cations-and-anions-compared`) — ionic sizes set against the parent atoms — cations smaller (electrons lost, the outer shell often gone), anions larger (electrons gained into the same shell) — the group charge pattern charted (1+, 2+, 3+, 2−, −) and chlorine worked against its own ion, Cl against Cl⁻ (Jordan, Grade 10 Term 1 — *الكيمياء — كتاب الطالب، الصف العاشر، الفصل الدراسي الأول (Chemistry, Student Book, Grade 10, Term 1)*, Unit 2, Lesson 2, printed pp. 42–43)
511. **Air-bag chemistry — sodium azide** (`chemistry/air-bag-chemistry-sodium-azide`) — the vehicle air bag as applied stoichiometry — a measured charge of sodium azide decomposing in the crash instant, 2NaN₃ → 2Na + 3N₂, the bag's fixed volume turned by the mole ratio into the exact mass to load (Jordan, Grade 10 Term 2 — *الكيمياء — كتاب الطالب، الصف العاشر، الفصل الدراسي الثاني (Chemistry, Student Book, Grade 10, Term 2)*, Unit 4 enrichment, printed p. 38)
512. **Bond energies — breaking bonds costs, making bonds pays** (`chemistry/bond-energies-breaking-bonds-costs-making-bonds-pays`) — bond energy as the glossary defines it — the energy to break one mole of bonds between two gas-state atoms — and the reaction's enthalpy kept as a balance sheet, broken against made, with the alkane combustion energy rising with molar mass as the closing trend (Jordan, Grade 10 Term 2 — *الكيمياء — كتاب الطالب، الصف العاشر، الفصل الدراسي الثاني (Chemistry, Student Book, Grade 10, Term 2)*, Unit 5, Lesson 3, printed pp. 62–66)
513. **Octet-rule exceptions — expanded and incomplete octets** (`chemistry/octet-rule-exceptions-expanded-and-incomplete-octets`) — the octet rule's own limits, tabulated by the book itself: Lewis structures of molecules that obey the rule set against a dedicated table of those that violate it — incomplete octets below eight, expanded octets above (Jordan, Grade 11 Term 1 — *الكيمياء — كتاب الطالب، الصف الحادي عشر، المسار الأكاديمي، الفصل الدراسي الأول (Chemistry, Student Book, Grade 11, Academic Track, Term 1)*, Unit 1, Lesson 1, printed p. 16)
514. **Atom economy — measuring reaction efficiency** (`chemistry/atom-economy-measuring-reaction-efficiency`) — atom economy as the stoichiometry unit's efficiency measure — the share of the reactants' atoms the balanced equation sends into the wanted product, computed on paper before any experiment, with addition reactions at 100% by construction (Jordan, Grade 11 Term 1 — *الكيمياء — كتاب الطالب، الصف الحادي عشر، المسار الأكاديمي، الفصل الدراسي الأول (Chemistry, Student Book, Grade 11, Academic Track, Term 1)*, Unit 2, Lesson 3, printed p. 93)
515. **Osmosis and osmotic pressure** (`chemistry/osmosis-and-osmotic-pressure`) — osmosis drawn particle by particle — solvent crossing a semi-permeable membrane toward the solution, the solute held back — and osmotic pressure as the pressure that stops the flow, with nanotube membranes as the enrichment (Jordan, Grade 12 Term 1 — *الكيمياء — كتاب الطالب، الصف الثاني عشر، المسار الأكاديمي، الفصل الدراسي الأول (Chemistry, Student Book, Grade 12, Academic Track, Term 1)*, Unit 2, Lesson 2, printed p. 74)
516. **Amines — structure, naming and basic character** (`chemistry/amines-structure-naming-and-basic-character`) — the amine family in full portrait — entered through aniline of the dye works, the –NH₂ group and its substituted forms, IUPAC naming worked on butylamine, boiling points tabulated against alkanes and alcohols, and the family's basic character from nitrogen's lone pair (Jordan, Grade 11 Term 2 — *الكيمياء — كتاب الطالب، الصف الحادي عشر، المسار الأكاديمي، الفصل الدراسي الثاني (Chemistry, Student Book, Grade 11, Academic Track, Term 2)*, Unit 4, Lesson 1, printed pp. 63–65)
517. **Grignard reagents — and nucleophilic addition** (`chemistry/grignard-reagents-and-nucleophilic-addition`) — nucleophilic addition by the Grignard reagent, drawn in full — the δ− oxygen, the –OMgX product, carbon delivered onto the carbonyl carbon — with ethyl magnesium chloride reacting with ethanal as the worked case (Jordan, Grade 12 Term 2 — *الكيمياء — كتاب الطالب، الصف الثاني عشر، المسار الأكاديمي، الفصل الدراسي الثاني (Chemistry, Student Book, Grade 12, Academic Track, Term 2)*, Unit 7, Lesson 1, printed pp. 126–127)
518. **Hydrogen sulphide and sulphides** (`chemistry/hydrogen-sulphide-and-sulphides`) — H₂S in two states with two names — the gas hydrogen sulphide and its aqueous solution hydrosulphuric acid — and sulphur's main salts, the sulphides, built on the sulphide ion S²⁻ (Mozambique, PESD I 1º ciclo — *Módulo 4 de Química*, Lição 9, printed p. 59)
519. **Classifying substances — the inorganic and organic family trees** (`chemistry/classifying-substances-inorganic-and-organic-family-trees`) — Bulgaria's two classification trees — inorganic substances into simple substances and the five compound families, organic compounds into hydrocarbons, oxygen-containing and nitrogen-containing branches — the map the whole grade-10 year hangs on (Bulgaria, Grade 10 — *Химия и опазване на околната среда, 10. клас*, Review §1, printed p. 6)
520. **Open, closed and isolated chemical systems** (`chemistry/open-closed-and-isolated-chemical-systems`) — a chemical system against its surroundings — open exchanges matter and energy, closed energy only, isolated neither (and none exist in reality); homogeneous against heterogeneous (Bulgaria, Grade 10 — §3, printed p. 15)
521. **Hess's law and heats of formation and combustion** (`chemistry/hess-law-and-heats-of-formation-and-combustion`) — the heat effect depends only on the initial and final states — heats of formation (elements count zero) and combustion, with CO's 111 kJ/mol found by subtraction on graphite's two paths (Bulgaria, Grade 10 — §5, printed pp. 19–20)
522. **Calorific values of fuels — and food energy** (`chemistry/calorific-values-of-fuels`) — fuels judged by energy carried and emissions per 1000 GJ — Bulgaria's 2022 mix (gas 48%, coal 22%, biofuels 16%), the closed ethanol loop, and food at 4/9/4 kcal per gram (Bulgaria, Grade 10 — §6, printed pp. 22–23)
523. **Catalysts and inhibitors — homogeneous and heterogeneous catalysis** (`chemistry/catalysts-and-inhibitors-homogeneous-and-heterogeneous-catalysis`) — positive catalysts and inhibitors, both unchanged at the end — homogeneous catalysis through intermediates, heterogeneous by adsorption lowering activation energy, and enzymes as biocatalysts (Bulgaria, Grade 10 — §9, printed pp. 30–31)
524. **Diffusion and osmosis — isotonic solutions** (`chemistry/diffusion-and-osmosis-isotonic-solutions`) — particles spreading high to low concentration, and solvent crossing a semipermeable membrane toward the concentrated side — 0.9% saline isotonic with blood, hypotonic bursting and hypertonic shrivelling red cells (Bulgaria, Grade 10 — §19, printed pp. 56–57)
525. **Degree of dissociation α — strong, weak and medium electrolytes** (`chemistry/degree-of-dissociation-alpha-strong-weak-and-medium-electrolytes`) — α = a/A measured by the lamp test — Bulgaria's three bands: strong above 30%, medium 3–30%, weak below 3% (Bulgaria, Grade 10 — §22, printed p. 63)
526. **Full and net ionic equations** (`chemistry/full-and-net-ionic-equations`) — ion-exchange written three ways — molecular, full ionic, and the net equation keeping only the interacting ions; every neutralisation netting to H⁺ + OH⁻ ⇌ H₂O (Bulgaria, Grade 10 — §§24–25, printed pp. 69–72)
527. **General reaction schemes of metals and nonmetals** (`chemistry/general-reaction-schemes-of-metals-and-nonmetals`) — each family's whole chemistry on one scheme-chart — the metal's five arrows mirrored by the nonmetal's, with characteristic oxidation states and halogen displacement (Bulgaria, Grade 10 — §39, printed p. 103)
528. **Glucose and the monosaccharides** (`chemistry/glucose-and-the-monosaccharides`) — glucose's structure deduced test by test — five –OH groups and one aldehyde group making a polyhydroxyaldehyde — with fructose the ketose beside it and glucose as the cellular fuel (Bulgaria, Grade 9 — §40, printed pp. 115–117; Grade 10 — §44, printed p. 113)
529. **Food tests — starch, lactose, casein and unsaturated fats** (`chemistry/food-tests-starch-lactose-casein-and-unsaturated-fats`) — a food-analysis laboratory — iodine for starch, permanganate for unsaturated residues, casein precipitated by dilute acetic acid, lactose proven with Cu(OH)₂, protein by biuret (Bulgaria, Grade 10 — §46, printed p. 118)
530. **Phenol — properties and reactions** (`chemistry/phenol-properties-and-reactions`) — the –OH group on a benzene ring becomes a weak acid — phenol's dissociation, its violet FeCl₃ test, white tribromophenol with bromine water, picric acid, and Bakelite (Bulgaria, Grade 9 — §29, printed pp. 87–89; Grade 10 — §§42–43, printed p. 111)
531. **Zinc and its compounds — amphoterism and uses** (`chemistry/zinc-and-its-compounds-amphoterism-and-uses`) — zinc the reducer sealing itself under a carbonate skin, and amphoteric ZnO/Zn(OH)₂ at work — zinc white, parchment paper from ZnCl₂, timber preserved by ZnSO₄, lithopone (Bulgaria, Grade 10 — §49, printed pp. 124–125)
532. **Protecting metals — coatings and sacrificial zinc** (`chemistry/protecting-metals-coatings-and-sacrificial-zinc`) — protection in three families — barrier coatings, electrochemical protection with sacrificial zinc (iron resting as cathode, safe even at a scratch), and corrosion-resistant alloys (Bulgaria, Grade 10 — §53, printed p. 133)
533. **Plastics — additives, recycling codes and plastic waste** (`chemistry/plastics-additives-recycling-codes-and-plastic-waste`) — a plastic as a formulated material — fillers to 50–60%, plasticisers, stabilisers — and its afterlife sorted by recycling codes 01–07, thermosets shredded to filler (Bulgaria, Grade 10 — §56, printed pp. 142–144)
534. **Rubber — natural, synthetic and vulcanisation** (`chemistry/rubber-natural-synthetic-and-vulcanisation`) — isoprene's coiled chains cross-linked by Goodyear's 1839 sulfur vulcanisation into a network, then buna and buna-S synthetics and the worn-tyre mountain (Bulgaria, Grade 10 — §57, printed pp. 146–147)
535. **Fibres — natural, artificial and synthetic** (`chemistry/fibres-natural-artificial-and-synthetic`) — natural cellulose and protein fibres, artificials rebuilt from cellulose (viscose, acetate), and true synthetics (PET, nylon-6,6, PAN) — identified forensically by burning behaviour (Bulgaria, Grade 10 — §§58–59, printed pp. 149–152)
536. **Materials — metals, ceramics, polymers and composites** (`chemistry/materials-metals-ceramics-polymers-and-composites`) — materials science from the definition up — structure at atomic, micro and macro levels, six property groups, the three families compared, and composites engineered where no family suffices (Bulgaria, Grade 10 — §§60–61, printed pp. 154–158)
537. **Green chemistry and the circular economy** (`chemistry/green-chemistry-and-the-circular-economy`) — the linear economy bent circular — repair, reuse, recycle — under green chemistry's principles, against Bulgaria's pollutant census for air, water and soil (Bulgaria, Grade 10 — §62, printed pp. 162–163)
538. **Waste management — the hierarchy and e-waste** (`chemistry/waste-management-hierarchy-and-e-waste`) — five ranked activities — prevent, reduce, reuse, recycle, landfill — and e-waste, the fastest-growing stream, with Bulgaria landfilling 318 kg per person against the EU's 162 (Bulgaria, Grade 10 — §63, printed p. 166)
539. **Climate agreements and environmental law** (`chemistry/climate-agreements-and-environmental-law`) — the agreement line Rio 1992 → Kyoto 1997 → Paris 2015 → Katowice 2018, brought home to Article 55 of Bulgaria's constitution — the right to a healthy environment and the duty to protect it (Bulgaria, Grade 10 — §64, printed p. 168; §66, printed p. 171)
540. **Sulfur dioxide — bleacher and preservative** (`chemistry/sulfur-dioxide-bleacher-and-preservative`) — SO₂ bleaches temporarily (most bleached materials regain their colours in air), sterilises a space and preserves food when sulfur burns inside it, and is a serious health hazard poured into the air from coal burning and industry (Iraq, Grade 9 — *الكيمياء (Chemistry), Third Intermediate*, Ch.8, printed p. 128)
541. **Carbocations and carbanions — charged carbon** (`chemistry/carbocations-and-carbanions-charged-carbon`) — carbon carrying a charge — the positive carbonium (carbocation) ion and the negative carbanion — and the structural (displayed) formula, which shows each atom's partners, bond types and valence where the molecular formula cannot (C₂H₆O is both ethyl alcohol and dimethyl ether) (Iraq, Grade 10, Scientific Track — *الكيمياء / CHEMISTRY, Fourth Scientific*, Ch.4, printed p. 90)
542. **Primary, secondary, tertiary and quaternary carbons** (`chemistry/primary-secondary-tertiary-and-quaternary-carbons`) — a carbon classified by how many other carbons it touches: primary (one), secondary (two), tertiary (three), quaternary (four), with the matching classes of hydrogen (Iraq, Grade 10, Scientific Track — *الكيمياء / CHEMISTRY, Fourth Scientific*, Ch.4, printed p. 93)
543. **Heterocyclic rings — with nitrogen, oxygen or sulfur** (`chemistry/heterocyclic-rings-with-nitrogen-oxygen-or-sulfur`) — rings holding other atoms: pyridine (six-membered nitrogen ring, part of nicotine), pyrrole (five-membered nitrogen ring, a building unit of haemoglobin and chlorophyll), furan (oxygen) and thiophene (sulfur), both resonance hybrids, with penicillin and many antibiotics carrying heterocyclic systems (Iraq, Grade 11, Scientific Track — *الكيمياء (Chemistry), Fifth Scientific*, Ch.8, printed pp. 206–207)
544. **Cuba's nickel and cobalt industry — Moa** (`chemistry/cuba-nickel-cobalt-industry-moa`) — how Cuba obtains its metals, taught through its own mining-metallurgical industry: lateritic ores, Cuba among the world's main nickel holders (Canada, Cuba, Russia, New Caledonia), and the industrial production of nickel and cobalt compounds at Moa, Holguín — nickel the leading export of Cuban mining (Cuba, Grade 11 — *Química — Onceno grado*, Ch. 5 §5.5, printed p. 150; Grade 11 programme, Unit 3 §3.4, printed p. 22, teacher evidence)
545. **Systematisation — review of Grades 10 and 11** (`chemistry/systematisation-review-grades-10-11`) — Cuba's closing pattern for the pre-university course: a whole 20-hour unit that systematises two years of chemistry — periodic law and the 18-column table, the substance classes and their genetic relation, reaction classification, energy and rate, equilibrium constants and shifts (teacher evidence only, acceptance 0) (Cuba, Grade 11 programme, Unit 4, printed p. 25; Grade 11 methodological guide, Unit 4, printed pp. 88–104)
546. **Avogadro's principle — and why water is H2O, not HO** (`chemistry/avogadro-s-principle-and-why-water-is-h2o-not-ho`) — Equal volumes of different gases, at the same temperature and pressure, contain the same number of molecules — this is Avogadro's principle. (Italy, Grade 9–10 — *Chimica: molecole in movimento (2ª edizione, volume unico)*, Capitolo 5, §1, printed pp. 104–106)
547. **Electric charge and Coulomb's law** (`chemistry/electric-charge-and-coulombs-law`) — Like charges repel and opposite charges attract; Coulomb's law, F = k·Q1·Q2/d², makes the force directly proportional to the charges and inversely proportional to the square of the distance, with k ≈ 9.0·10⁹ N·m²/C² in vacuum. (Italy, Grade 9–10 — *Chimica: molecole in movimento (2ª edizione, volume unico)*, Capitolo 7, §1–2, printed pp. 152–155)
548. **Proteins of the future — food footprints as chemistry** (`chemistry/proteins-of-the-future-food-footprints-as-chemistry`) — Producing 1 kg of beef takes 16,000 L of water against 2,000 L for 1 kg of soy, while soy delivers 0.34 kg of protein per kg against beef's 0.17 kg. (Italy, Grade 9–10 — *Chimica: molecole in movimento (2ª edizione, volume unico)*, Sezione F closing spreads, "Pensa sostenibile: Dove sono le proteine del futuro" and "Calore e acidità: gli effetti sulla vita", printed pp. 438–439, 479)
549. **Newman projections — eclipsed and staggered conformers** (`chemistry/newman-projections-and-eclipsed-and-staggered-conformers`) — Alkane conformations are represented formally with Newman projections and sawhorse (a cavalletto) formulas. (Italy, Grade 13 — *Chimica organica, Biochimica, Biotecnologie (Campbell) — Guida per l'insegnante*, Modulo B, Capitolo 2, §4, guide p. 4; teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)
550. **Thiols — the sulfur analogues of alcohols** (`chemistry/thiols-the-sulfur-analogues-of-alcohols`) — Thiols (tioli) are the sulfur analogues of the alcohols, given a named treatment beside them: formula, nomenclature, acidity and reactivity. (Italy, Grade 13 — *Chimica organica, Biochimica, Biotecnologie (Campbell) — Guida per l'insegnante*, Modulo C, Capitolo 7, §3, guide p. 10; teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)
551. **Epoxides — and why their ring opens** (`chemistry/epoxides-and-why-their-ring-opens`) — Epoxides (epossidi) are a class of their own among the ethers: their ring opens with acids, halogen acids and water, where ordinary ethers are inert. (Italy, Grade 13 — *Chimica organica, Biochimica, Biotecnologie (Campbell) — Guida per l'insegnante*, Modulo C, Capitolo 7, §§4–5, guide p. 10; teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)
552. **Keto–enol tautomerism** (`chemistry/keto-enol-tautomerism`) — Tautomerism is a functional isomerism of the carbonyl compounds: the ketone form and the enol form interconvert, ketone ⇌ enol. (Italy, Grade 13 — *Chimica organica, Biochimica, Biotecnologie (Campbell) — Guida per l'insegnante*, Modulo C, Capitolo 8, §2, guide p. 11; teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)
553. **Aldol and Claisen condensations** (`chemistry/aldol-and-claisen-condensations`) — The aldol condensation builds carbon–carbon bonds, and the guide derives it from the α-hydrogen's acidity. (Italy, Grade 13 — *Chimica organica, Biochimica, Biotecnologie (Campbell) — Guida per l'insegnante*, Modulo C, Capitolo 8, §3 and Per approfondire, guide p. 11; teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)
554. **Miller's experiment — the abiotic synthesis of biomolecules** (`chemistry/millers-experiment-and-the-abiotic-synthesis-of-biomolecules`) — Miller's experiment is mapped step by step onto the events that formed the first organic molecules on Earth. (Italy, Grade 13 — *Chimica organica, Biochimica, Biotecnologie (Campbell) — Guida per l'insegnante*, Modulo D, Capitolo 10, §2, guide p. 14; teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)
555. **Cellular respiration — glycolysis, Krebs cycle and oxidative phosphorylation** (`chemistry/cellular-respiration-glycolysis-krebs-cycle-and-oxidative-phosphorylation`) — Respiration is staged redox: glycolysis, pyruvate decarboxylation and the Krebs cycle, then the electron-transport chain and chemiosmosis, with the ATP balance per glucose computed. (Italy, Grade 13 — *Chimica organica, Biochimica, Biotecnologie (Campbell) — Guida per l'insegnante*, Modulo E, Capitolo 12, §§10–17, guide pp. 19–20; teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)
556. **The integrated metabolism of carbohydrates, fats and proteins** (`chemistry/the-integrated-metabolism-of-carbohydrates-fats-and-proteins`) — The pathways interlock on one map: glycogen metabolism and glycogenesis, gluconeogenesis, the pentose-phosphate pathway, β-oxidation against lipogenesis, and amino-acid catabolism by transamination into the urea cycle. (Italy, Grade 13 — *Chimica organica, Biochimica, Biotecnologie (Campbell) — Guida per l'insegnante*, Modulo E, Capitolo 12, §§18–23, guide pp. 20–21; teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)
557. **Photosynthesis — light reactions, Calvin cycle and hot-climate plants** (`chemistry/photosynthesis-light-reactions-calvin-cycle-and-hot-climate-plants`) — The light reactions — two photosystems, water splitting, ATP, NADPH and O₂ by chemiosmotic photophosphorylation on the thylakoid membrane — feed the Calvin cycle, with Rubisco at its centre. (Italy, Grade 13 — *Chimica organica, Biochimica, Biotecnologie (Campbell) — Guida per l'insegnante*, Modulo E, Capitolo 13, guide pp. 22–23; teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)
558. **Viruses — structure, lytic and lysogenic cycles, retroviruses and prions** (`chemistry/viruses-structure-lytic-and-lysogenic-cycles-retroviruses-and-prions`) — Viruses are obligate parasites built of capsid and envelope, with DNA or RNA genomes; phages run the lytic cycle against the lysogenic cycle. (Italy, Grade 13 — *Chimica organica, Biochimica, Biotecnologie (Campbell) — Guida per l'insegnante*, Modulo F, Capitolo 14, §§1–8, guide p. 24; teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)
559. **Bacterial gene transfer — transformation, transduction, conjugation and plasmids** (`chemistry/bacterial-gene-transfer-transformation-transduction-conjugation-and-plasmids`) — Bacteria gain genetic variability by mutation at short generation times and by horizontal transfer between cells. (Italy, Grade 13 — *Chimica organica, Biochimica, Biotecnologie (Campbell) — Guida per l'insegnante*, Modulo F, Capitolo 14, §§9–11, guide p. 25; teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)
560. **Recombinant DNA — restriction enzymes, ligase, vectors and cDNA** (`chemistry/recombinant-dna-restriction-enzymes-ligase-vectors-and-cdna`) — Restriction enzymes cut DNA — blunt ends against sticky ends — and DNA ligase reforms the phosphodiester bond. (Italy, Grade 13 — *Chimica organica, Biochimica, Biotecnologie (Campbell) — Guida per l'insegnante*, Modulo F, Capitolo 15, §§1–5, guide pp. 25–26; teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)
561. **PCR, gel electrophoresis and DNA profiling** (`chemistry/pcr-gel-electrophoresis-and-dna-profiling`) — PCR's three temperature steps produce an exponential yield, and the guide computes it. (Italy, Grade 13 — *Chimica organica, Biochimica, Biotecnologie (Campbell) — Guida per l'insegnante*, Modulo F, Capitolo 15, §§6–9, guide p. 26; teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)
562. **DNA sequencing — from Sanger to nanopores — and CRISPR editing** (`chemistry/dna-sequencing-from-sanger-to-nanopores-and-crispr-editing`) — Sanger's method is taught with reading an electropherogram, before second-generation sequencing and genomic libraries. (Italy, Grade 13 — *Chimica organica, Biochimica, Biotecnologie (Campbell) — Guida per l'insegnante*, Modulo F, Capitolo 15, §§10–12, guide p. 26; teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)
563. **Genomics — the Human Genome Project, shotgun sequencing and bioinformatics** (`chemistry/genomics-the-human-genome-project-shotgun-sequencing-and-bioinformatics`) — The omic sciences are mapped as one frame: genomics, transcriptomics, proteomics, metabolomics, epigenomics. (Italy, Grade 13 — *Chimica organica, Biochimica, Biotecnologie (Campbell) — Guida per l'insegnante*, Modulo F, Capitolo 15, §§13–17, guide pp. 26–27; teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)
564. **Cloning by nuclear transfer — and stem cells** (`chemistry/cloning-by-nuclear-transfer-and-stem-cells`) — Reproductive cloning by nuclear transfer (Dolly) is set against therapeutic cloning, with its ethics and law. (Italy, Grade 13 — *Chimica organica, Biochimica, Biotecnologie (Campbell) — Guida per l'insegnante*, Modulo F, Capitolo 15, §§18–20, guide p. 27; teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)
565. **Biotechnology applied — gene therapy, new vaccines and the GMO debate** (`chemistry/biotechnology-applied-gene-therapy-new-vaccines-and-gmo-debate`) — Recombinant organisms produce proteins and drugs, and mammalian-cell transfection runs by electroporation, viral vectors and microinjection. (Italy, Grade 13 — *Chimica organica, Biochimica, Biotecnologie (Campbell) — Guida per l'insegnante*, Modulo F, Capitolo 15, §§21–27, guide pp. 27–28; teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)

566. **Nutrients — proteins, carbohydrates, fats and vitamins** (`chemistry/nutrients-proteins-carbohydrates-fats-and-vitamins`) — The six nutrient classes (protein, carbohydrate, oil/fat, vitamin, mineral salts, water); proteins built from amino acids, digested and rebuilt, about 18 kJ/g; haemoglobin's Fe²⁺ carrying oxygen and carbon monoxide binding 200–300 times more strongly — the chemistry of coal-gas poisoning; a data card on enzymes as protein catalysts, and cards on smoking and on formaldehyde denaturing protein. (China, Grade 9 — *义务教育教科书·化学九年级下册*, Unit 12 Topic 1 人类重要的营养物质, printed p. 90)
567. **Sodium peroxide — breathing oxygen from a solid** (`chemistry/sodium-peroxide-breathing-oxygen-from-a-solid`) — Na₂O₂ compared with Na₂O: with water it gives NaOH + O₂ (glowing splint, warmth), with CO₂ it gives Na₂CO₃ + O₂ — the chemistry behind oxygen supplies in breathing masks and submarines. (China, Senior High, Compulsory Volume 1 — *普通高中教科书·化学必修 第一册*, Ch. 2 §1, printed p. 37)
568. **Etching circuit boards with iron(III) chloride** (`chemistry/etching-circuit-boards-with-iron-iii-chloride`) — Learners etch a pattern into copper-clad board with FeCl₃ "corrosion liquid" (the printed-circuit-board process), analyse it as redox, and plan how to recycle the spent etchant. (China, Senior High, Compulsory Volume 1 — *普通高中教科书·化学必修 第一册*, Ch. 3 §1 inquiry, printed p. 74)
569. **Rare-earth metals — the vitamins of metallurgy** (`chemistry/rare-earth-metals-the-vitamins-of-metallurgy`) — Rare earths (17 elements) as "vitamins of metallurgy": China's reserves/production first in the world, Xu Guangxian's separation work, and the duty to use the resource sustainably (China's own framing). (China, Senior High, Compulsory Volume 1 — *普通高中教科书·化学必修 第一册*, Ch. 3 §2 STS, printed p. 84)
570. **Sulphur and sulphur dioxide** (`chemistry/sulphur-and-sulphur-dioxide`) — Sulfur (yellow, brittle; reacts with Fe, Cu, H₂, O₂) and SO₂: very soluble (Experiment 5-1), an acidic oxide forming unstable sulfurous acid — the book's first reversible reaction, SO₂ + H₂O ⇌ H₂SO₃; SO₂ is both oxidising (with H₂S) and reducing (to SO₃), and bleaches by forming an unstable colourless compound (fuchsin Experiment 5-2), so its bleaching fades — unlike chlorine's. (China, Senior High, Compulsory Volume 2 — *普通高中教科书·化学必修 第二册*, Ch. 5 §1, printed p. 2)
571. **Using precipitation — separating ions, cleaning wastewater, dissolving scale** (`chemistry/using-precipitation-separating-ions-cleaning-wastewater-dissolving-scale`) — Crude salt still holds SO₄²⁻, Mg²⁺ and Ca²⁺ after filtering: learners plan and then perform the precipitation sequence (excess BaCl₂, then NaOH, then Na₂CO₃ — which also removes the added Ba²⁺), filter, neutralise with HCl and evaporate, debating reagent order and why each excess must itself be removed. (China, Senior High, Compulsory Volume 2 — *普通高中教科书·化学必修 第二册*, Ch. 5 §1 discussion + Experiment Activity 4, printed pp. 6, 29)
572. **Advanced ceramics and carbon nanomaterials** (`chemistry/advanced-ceramics-and-carbon-nanomaterials`) — New ceramics (SiC "carborundum", Si₃N₄ bearings working at 1,600 °C; STS box adds structural, piezoelectric, transparent and superconducting ceramics with a maglev photo) and carbon nanomaterials — fullerenes (C₆₀ cage), carbon nanotubes, and single-layer graphene — with graphene supercapacitor and super-sponge photographs. (China, Senior High, Compulsory Volume 2 — *普通高中教科书·化学必修 第二册*, Ch. 5 §3, printed pp. 23–24)
573. **Seawater — desalination, salt and the blowing-out of bromine** (`chemistry/seawater-desalination-salt-and-the-blowing-out-of-bromine`) — Seawater as a chemical storehouse (elements card: 11 elements make >99 %, yet even gold totals 5×10⁴ t): desalination by distillation, electrodialysis or reverse osmosis; solar salt feeding the chlor-alkali chain （纯碱/烧碱/氯气/盐酸）; Mg, K and Br won from bittern; uranium and heavy water flagged as strategic; bromine's industrial "blowing-out" process analysed step by step (Cl₂ oxidises Br⁻, air blows Br₂ out, SO₂ absorbs it as HBr, Cl₂ again, distil). (China, Senior High, Compulsory Volume 2 — *普通高中教科书·化学必修 第二册*, Ch. 8 §1, printed pp. 100–101)
574. **Artemisinin — Tu Youyou — and designing better drugs** (`chemistry/artemisinin-tu-youyou-and-designing-better-drugs`) — Drug design from molecular structure and dose: in the 1970s Chinese scientists, inspired by traditional medical texts, isolated artemisinin from sweet wormwood, then modified its structure into a family of antimalarials that saved millions (Tu Youyou's 2015 Nobel Prize photograph); sustained-release designs (polymer-coated aspirin) show chemistry improving a drug's delivery. (China, Senior High, Compulsory Volume 2 — *普通高中教科书·化学必修 第二册*, Ch. 8 §2 STS, printed p. 111)
575. **Thermochemical equations — writing the heat into the equation** (`chemistry/thermochemical-equations-writing-the-heat-into-the-equation`) — Thermochemical equations: states must be marked, ΔH signed and unit-ed per mole of reaction as written, coefficients may be fractions, and the same reaction at a different scale or reversed carries a scaled/sign-flipped ΔH. (China, Senior High, Selective Compulsory 1 — *普通高中教科书·化学选择性必修 1 · 化学反应原理*, Ch. 1 §1, printed pp. 8–9)
576. **Bond parameters — energy, length and angle** (`chemistry/bond-parameters-energy-length-and-angle`) — A bond measured three ways: bond energy (kJ/mol to break 1 mol of the bond into gas atoms; average values, e.g. C—H, tabulated — and used to estimate reaction heats), bond length (nuclear distance; C—C 154 pm > C=C 133 pm > C≡C 120 pm as energy rises), and bond angle (proving bonds are directional; values from X-ray diffraction). (China, Senior High, Selective Compulsory 2 — *普通高中教科书·化学选择性必修 2 · 物质结构与性质*, Ch. 2 §1, printed pp. 37–40)
577. **Supramolecules — molecular recognition and self-assembly** (`chemistry/supramolecules-molecular-recognition-and-self-assembly`) — Supramolecules: two or more molecules associated by intermolecular forces into an organised aggregate — molecular recognition (a host shaped for one guest, crown ethers sized to specific metal ions) and self-assembly as the working principles behind enzyme action and much of modern materials chemistry. (China, Senior High, Selective Compulsory 2 — *普通高中教科书·化学选择性必修 2 · 物质结构与性质*, Ch. 3 §4, printed pp. 98–100)
578. **Chromatography — from Tswett's column to HPLC** (`chemistry/chromatography-from-tswetts-column-to-hplc`) — Chromatography introduced through its history: Tswett's ignored column experiment separating plant pigments, rediscovery 25 years later in Nobel-winning carotenoid/vitamin work, then paper, thin-layer, gas and high-performance liquid chromatography as the modern family. (China, Senior High, Selective Compulsory 3 — *普通高中教科书·化学选择性必修 3 · 有机化学基础*, Ch. 1 §2, printed p. 16)
579. **Formic acid — the acid that pretends to be an aldehyde** (`chemistry/formic-acid-the-acid-that-pretends-to-be-an-aldehyde`) — Carboxylic acids surveyed as a family (fatty, aromatic — benzoic acid; dicarboxylic — oxalic acid as analytical reductant), and formic acid's double life: its structure holds both —COOH and an aldehyde-like H—C=O, so it is acidic AND gives a silver mirror, ending as CO₂ + H₂O; a research task designs the experiment chain that orders acetic acid > carbonic acid > phenol by strength (with saturated NaHCO₃ as the CO₂ scrubber in the apparatus). (China, Senior High, Selective Compulsory 3 — *普通高中教科书·化学选择性必修 3 · 有机化学基础*, Ch. 3 §4, printed pp. 73–75)
580. **Designing an organic synthesis — build the skeleton, place the group, protect it** (`chemistry/designing-an-organic-synthesis-build-the-skeleton-place-the-group-protect-it`) — Organic synthesis as design: Wöhler's urea opens it; the two tasks are building the carbon skeleton (grow, shorten, ring-form) and introducing functional groups; routes judged by raw-material cost, step yield, atom economy, mild/green operations; a hydroxyl group that would not survive a step is protected as an ether and unmasked afterwards — worked route-design exercises (e.g. (China, Senior High, Selective Compulsory 3 — *普通高中教科书·化学选择性必修 3 · 有机化学基础*, Ch. 3 §5, printed pp. 84–92)
581. **Protein structure — four levels and insulin's two chains** (`chemistry/protein-structure-four-levels-and-insulins-two-chains`) — Protein architecture in levels: primary sequence; hydrogen-bonded coils/sheets (secondary); folding (tertiary); multi-chain assemblies (quaternary) — with insulin as the fully-known case (A chain 21 + B chain 30 amino acids, joined by disulfide bridges; first protein ever sequenced); proteins stay amphoteric, hydrolyse stepwise to amino acids (digestion), denature and salt out (linked back to Bk 5), and enzymes are introduced as protein catalysts of extreme specificity and mildness. (China, Senior High, Selective Compulsory 3 — *普通高中教科书·化学选择性必修 3 · 有机化学基础*, Ch. 4 §2, printed pp. 114–120)
582. **Nitric oxide — the little molecule that messages the body** (`chemistry/nitric-oxide-the-little-molecule-that-messages-the-body`) — A full background dossier on NO: Science magazine's 1992 "Molecule of the Year"; the bonding puzzle (15 valence electrons; quantum-chemical energy levels match N₂'s pattern, not O₂'s; a three-electron π bond), bond length 105 pm and bond energy 678 kJ/mol tabulated against N₂ (111 pm, 942) and O₂ (121 pm, 494), paramagnetic as a one-unpaired-electron radical; then its second life — the endothelium-derived relaxing factor that dilates blood vessels, synthesised from L-arginine, with a biological half-life of only 3–5 seconds, acting as a neurotransmitter that needs no receptor or storage vesicle. (China, Senior High — *普通高中教科书·化学必修 第二册 教师教学用书* (teacher guide), Ch. 5 teaching resources (teacher evidence), guide printed pp. 26–27; PARTIAL (Chapters 5–6 only); teacher-guide evidence only, acceptance 0)
583. **States beyond solid, liquid and gas — plasma and BEC** (`chemistry/states-beyond-solid-liquid-gas-plasma-and-bec`) — Matter has more states than the familiar three: plasma — a hot, ionised gas of charged particles — and the exotic states reached at the extremes, among them the Bose–Einstein condensate, where particles cooled almost to absolute zero merge into a single quantum state; between solid and liquid stand the intermediate states, the liquid crystals, which flow like liquids while keeping some of a crystal's order, described with their daily-life uses and differentiated from pure liquids and crystalline solids. (Pakistan (Punjab), Grade 9 — *CHEMISTRY 9* (PCTB, SNC 2023), Chapter 1, printed p. 1; Pakistan (Punjab), Grade 11 — *Chemistry-XI* (PECTAA, National Curriculum 2023), Ch. 5, printed p. 91)

584. **Ions — cations and anions** (`chemistry/ions-cations-and-anions`) — An atom becomes an ion when its electrons stop balancing its protons: losing electrons makes a positive cation, gaining makes a negative anion, charge q = p − e⁻, and an ion's properties differ from its atom's. (Kazakhstan, Grade 8 — *«Chemistry» (bilingual edition, ed. Kaliev D.)*, Ch. 1.2, PDF p. 20; Kazakhstan, Grade 8 — *Chemistry — Grade 8* (O'Callaghan et al., Express Publishing), Module 1, printed p. 16; Kazakhstan, Grade 8 — *«Химия» — Ospanova et al. (Russian, Mektep)*, Ch. I §4, printed p. 14; Kazakhstan, Grade 9 Pt 1 — *«Химия. Часть 1» — Ospanova et al.*, Ch. I §5, printed p. 19)
585. **Silicon and its compounds** (`chemistry/silicon-and-its-compounds`) — Silicon's diamond-like lattice and semiconductor behaviour (n- and p-type doping, the p–n junction, diodes and chips), and its compounds — silica, silicic acid, silicates and aluminosilicates — with glass, ceramics and Kazakhstan's silicate industry. (Kazakhstan, Grade 9 — *«Химия (пробная версия)» — NIS trial*, Ch. 10 §§10.1–10.4, printed pp. 154, 158; Kazakhstan, Grade 9 — *Chemistry (English edition, ed. Baikenov K.)*, Ch. 9 §§9.7–9.8, PDF pp. 291, 297)
586. **Chemical elements in the human body** (`chemistry/chemical-elements-in-the-human-body`) — The body's element inventory: O 65%, C 18.5%, H 9.5%, N about 3% and the rest of the macroelements, against the microelements in traces — each group's jobs, deficiency signs, finding elements in food, and the balanced diet read chemically. (Kazakhstan, Grade 7 — *«Химия. 7-сынып» — NIS (Kazakh)*, Ch. 5.1, printed p. 44; Kazakhstan, Grade 9 — *«Химия. Часть 2» — Ospanova et al.*, Ch. IX §§46–47, printed pp. 70, 75; Kazakhstan, Grade 9 — *Chemistry Grade 9 Student's Book (Express Publishing)*, Module 9, printed p. 115)
587. **Qualitative analysis of unknown substances** (`chemistry/qualitative-analysis-of-unknown-substances`) — The capstone of the analysis chapter: devise the experiment, build the cation-and-anion test scheme and the theoretical results table before touching a reagent, then identify the unknown salt from the evidence. (Kazakhstan, Grade 9 — *«Химия (пробная версия)» — NIS trial*, Ch. 5 §5.4, printed p. 96; Kazakhstan, Grade 9 — *Chemistry (English edition, ed. Baikenov K.)*, Ch. 2 §2.3, PDF p. 81; Kazakhstan, Grade 9 — *Chemistry Grade 9 Student's Book (Express Publishing)*, Module 2, printed p. 28)
588. **Chemistry of Kazakhstan — its scientists** (`chemistry/chemistry-of-kazakhstan-its-scientists`) — The 'Chemistry of Kazakhstan in Faces' gallery opening the Usmanova Grade 9 book: Usanovich's acid–base theory (1938), Birimzhanov on salt formation, Kozlovsky's amalgam metallurgy, Sokolsky's catalysis school, Songina's rare-metal electrochemistry, Azerbayev's acetylene chemistry, Rafikov's polymerisation principles, Nadirov on West Kazakhstan oil.
